Definitions and retained proof interface\n\nThe complete original-index definitions and their derivations are reproduced\nin Appendix A. They are part of this joint draft, not unverified results being\nimported as facts. The appendix's z is znew, the baseline occupation column;\nthroughout Sections2--9 below z means a general column or is explicitly zrel.\nThe appendix retains its original equation numbers to make the input audit\ntraceable; its numbered sections are internal to that appendix.\n\nFor immediate reference, fix m>=M0 and set\n\n eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), h=H_m,\n n=2^m-1, l=n-1, S=sum h, D=1+2S,\n q=17/(16D), pi=sqrt(q), b=1+a, g=1+beta.\n\nIndices0,...,l are queried; n is the unqueried terminal. L is the n-by-n\nlower triangular matrix with Lii=1 and Lij=h_j for j<i. It is not the original\nsmoothness constant. Let e be the all-ones column, e_i the standard columns,\nt=Le, ell=Lt, T=t_l=S-a, Delta=T-1=S-b, and Utotal=S+1.\n\nLet pop(i) count the ones in the binary expansion of i. Then\n\n w_i=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-pop(i)), s=beta^n c^(-m),\n k_i=h_i product_(u>i)(1-h_u), E=pi+s, F=pi-s,\n eta=F/E, V=pi^(-1)-1, Z=-E/a, Qgap=FE=q-s^2.\n\nThe terminal distribution alpha uses its actual two-coordinate clock bracket,\nnot a replaced mean: tbar=(S-V)/(1-pi), j=min{i:t_(i+1)>=tbar},\nalpha_j=(1-pi)(t_(j+1)-tbar)/h_j,\nalpha_(j+1)=(1-pi)(tbar-t_j)/h_j, all others zero. Put v=k-alpha.\nIn particular alpha0=alpha1=0, v0=bs/a, v1=-gs/(a beta), and\n\n e^Tv=E, t^Tv=V, e^Talpha=1-pi, t^Talpha=S-V.\n\nDefine the physical moments tmass=sum h_i t_i/S,\nVmass=sum h_i(t_i-tmass)^2, X=D-pi^(-2)-eta Z^2,\nrho=X/Vmass, Kclock=rho[diag(h)-hh^T/S], yclock=Kclock t.\nKnew is exactly Appendix A equation(3.3): the original consecutive\nthree-query details with omega0 chosen so K00=3q, all other prescribed\nomegas, and the final-six matrix U Rtest U^T with\nRtest=3(ehat0-ehat1)(ehat0-ehat1)^T+ehat3 ehat3^T.\nNo summand or crossing detail is removed. In Sections2--9 write K=Knew.\n\nThe literal common column is\n\n p_i=h_i w_i/(1+s), p0=b/(1+s), p1=-ga/(1+s),\n r2=(a beta+s)/(1+s), u=p-p0e0-p1e1-r2e2,\n Mu=t^Tu, uhat=u-(Mu/g)(e_l-e_(l-1)),\n gamma=q(1+s)/(2Qgap), zsrc=(E/a)(b e0-e1),\n znew=zsrc+E gamma uhat.\n\nThe correct source product is\n\n (1+s)Csrc=b+ga^2+(a beta+s)a beta.\n\nThe positive ga^2 is essential; the earlier TASK minus sign is not used.\nAppendix A proves all of the following by finite sums and elementary bounds:\n\n 41/100<a<5/12, 7/5<b<17/12, 199/100<g<2,\n 12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1,\n 2^m-1<S<c^m<2^(2m), 1/(3S)<q<1/S,\n 0<s<2^(-4m^2), s^2/q<1/100, pi<2^(-m/2),\n K>=0, Ke=0, Kt=yclock, t^TKt=X, K00=3q,\n e^Tznew=E, t^Tznew=Z, znew0=bE/a, znew1=-E/a,\n 0<rho<125/S^2, yclock0<0, |yclock0|/(bq)<7/5.          (Bounds)\n\nThe accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is used only for the original ordered\nsmooth-convex interpolation convention and full unqueried-terminal-square\ncertificate interface. It does not imply feasibility of our arrays. No\nconditional benchmark statement is invoked as a full-function upper bound.\n\n## 2. Full polynomial and all coefficient/moment equations\n\nTake any real z with e^Tz=E and t^Tz=Z, including znew and zrel. Define\n\n P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T, dvec=F(v-z), rvec=yclock+Vv+eta Zz-2alpha,\n A^T=-A, Ae=dvec, At=rvec, N=L^T(P+A).                    (Full)\n\nHere rvec is a vector, not the integer r in the moving query. Compatibility is\nnot an unspecified feasibility hypothesis: e^Tdvec=0,\ne^Trvec=-t^Tdvec, and t^Trvec=0 follow from the listed moments and\nX=D-pi^(-2)-eta Z^2. For instance, with\n\n tmean=e^Tt/n, cvec=t-tmean e, Ve=cvec^Tcvec,\n fvec=rvec-tmean dvec+(dvec^Tt)e/n,\n Acan=(dvec e^T-e dvec^T)/n+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve,\n\none has e^Tfvec=cvec^Tfvec=0. Multiplication gives Ae=dvec and At=rvec.\nVe>0 because t is nonconstant. Every compatible A is Acan+C with\nC skew and Ce=Ct=0. All our impossibility statements include every such C.\n\nFor EVERY original pair,\n\n N_ij=P_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(P_uj+A_uj),\n mu=Ne=2pi L^Tv, N^Te=2alpha,\n lambda_ij=-N_ij for distinct queried i,j,\n lambda_(i,star)=mu_i, lambda_(star,i)=0,\n lambda_(n,star)=2pi, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n lambda_(i,n)=lambda_(star,n)=0.                       (Coefficients)\n\nIndeed (P+A)e=2pi v and (P-A)t=2alpha. Finite product telescoping gives\n\n mu_i=2pi[h_i(pi+sum_(u<i)alpha_u)+(h_i-1)alpha_i]>=2q h_i.\n\nThus all optimum and terminal signs hold for both columns independently of A.\nEvery original finite sign is still required. In particular the row and column\ncapacities, whenever the off-diagonals are nonpositive, are\n\n sum_(j!=i)lambda_ij=Nii-mu_i,\n sum_(j!=i)lambda_ji=Nii-2alpha_i.                     (Capacities)\n\nThe full square identity can be expanded directly. Normalize smoothness to1,\nx_star=g_star=f_star=0, and let\nQ_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\ny_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Let Gpre be the original formal column\nof queried gradients. GD implies y=e x0-L Gpre and\nx_n=-s x0+k^Ty. The incoming-free terminal multipliers sum to2, and the\nfinite flow is2alpha_i; hence the prefix sum is\n2alpha^Tdelta-Gpre^TN y. The terminal terms cancel the sampled values and give\n\n q||x0||^2+||g_n||^2-2<g_n,-s x0+v^Ty>-Gpre^TN y.\n\nNow Gpre^TN y=(e x0-y)^T(P+A)y and\ne^T(P+A)=2s v^T+2Fz^T. Since A is skew and q=s^2+eta E^2, this equals\n\n q||x0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n   =||g_n+s x0-v^Ty||^2+y^TKy+eta||z^Ty-E x0||^2.       (Square)\n\nThis is an identity on the entire original formal space, with g_n free.\nK>=0 and eta>0 prove PSD. The initial norm is charged exactly once, by q.\nThe sign exclusions below prevent this PSD identity from being an upper\ncertificate. No PSD claim is substituted for the missing multiplier signs.\n\n## 3. General source identity, including its changed diagonal\n\nWrite delta0=z0-bE/a. The first ORIGINAL row of L^T is b e^T-a e0^T.\nConsequently, with Hsrc_j=s v_j+Fz_j,\n\n N0j=2b Hsrc_j-a(K0j+v0 v_j+eta z0 z_j+A0j)\n     =b s v_j+(bF-a eta delta0)z_j-aK0j-aA0j.\n\nSince v0=bs/a and z0=bE/a+delta0, direct subtraction yields, for EVERY j,\n\n Pj0-A0j=2Kj0+2eta delta0 z_j+N0j/a.\n\nAt j=0, A00=0; no exceptional diagonal term is omitted. Multiplying this\nENTIRE column by L^T gives\n\n N_:0=2L^TK_:0+2eta delta0 L^Tz+(L^TN_0:^T)/a.          (Source-field)\n\nThe term 2eta delta0 L^Tz is indispensable after a source release. Completing\nthe scalar square in N00, the linear delta0 terms cancel:\n\n N00=b^2 q/a-aK00-a eta delta0^2,\n mu0=2bq,\n r0:=N00-mu0=(1/a-a)q-aK00-a eta delta0^2.               (Source-total)\n\nThe incoming column total is N00, because (N^Te)0=2alpha0=0.\nThe fixed first clock moment of the outgoing row is\n\n Bsrc:=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)lambda0j=mu0-(Nt)0,\n Nt=2L^T(yclock+Vv+eta Zz-alpha),\n (Nt)0=-2a yclock0+2F delta0,\n Bsrc=2bq+2a yclock0-2F delta0.                             (Source-clock)\n\nFor the expression for (Nt)0, multiply by b e^T-a e0^T, use the exact sum\nmoments, v0=bs/a, z0=bE/a+delta0 and alpha0=0; the source-normalized constant\nterms cancel and -2a eta Z delta0=2F delta0 remains. Thus neither the old\ndiagonal nor the old source clock load can be reused after delta0 changes.\n\nFor completeness the incoming first clock moment also follows from the full\nfield, but is not fixed by the two prescribed skew moments alone:\n\n t^TN_:0=2ell^TK_:0+2eta delta0 ell^Tz+(ell^TN_0:^T)/a.\n\nIt contains the entire outgoing ell moment. We do not treat this as a free\nsource-clock budget or assign it an artificial value.\n\nAt ORIGINAL row2, h2=b and t2=1+b+g. Using\nsum_(j>0)N0j=-r0 in (Source-field) gives\n\n aN20+bN01+aN02=2a(L^TK)20+2a eta delta0(L^Tz)2-b r0.   (S3-general)\n\nAll three entries on the left are off diagonal and all three weights positive.\nThis derivation used no support restriction on A and no symmetry of L times\nan auxiliary cost matrix.\n\n## 4. Mechanism1: exact all-skew exclusion at the old source\n\nFor znew, delta0=0 and r0=(1/a-4a)q. Only d0 in the entire detail sum reaches\nsource0. Explicitly d0=(g,-b-g,b,0,...), and\nomega0=(3q-rho b(1-b/S))/g^2. All final-six columns have zero source coordinate.\nTherefore the full source column is Kclock_:0+omega0 g d0, and\n\n (L^TK)20\n =omega0 bg-rho b^2[1+(1-2b-g)/S]\n =3bq/g-rho b^2(1-b/S)/g-rho b^2[1+(1-2b-g)/S]\n >3bq/g-rho b^2(1+1/g). Finally q>1/(3S) gives\n625/S^2<(1875/S)q<q/100, since S>2^20>187500.\n\nIf every finite N_ij<=0, the left side of S3-old is nonpositive; the displayed\npositive value is a contradiction. The same proof works for any z with the\nsame two moments and z0=bE/a: no use of z1, gamma or the occupation tail was\nmade here. In particular adding A04 to the earlier sparse completion cannot\nrepair the literal metric/common-source normalization. This is the decisive\nfirst result; mechanism2 is the single prescribed source release below.\n\n## 5. To account\nfor EVERY original coefficient change, use znew for the old column and write\n\n DeltaP=F(znew xi^T+xi znew^T)+Qgap xi xi^T,\n Deltadvec=-Qgap xi, Deltarvec=-(Qgap/a)xi,\n DeltaAcan=Qgap(e xi^T-xi e^T)/n\n       +Qgap(tmean-1/a)(xi cvec^T-cvec xi^T)/Ve,\n DeltaN_ij=(DeltaP+DeltaAcan)_ij\n                  +h_i sum_(u>i)(DeltaP+DeltaAcan)_uj. (Released-capacity)\n\nFor the lower rrel bound use a<5/12, hence 1/a-5a>12/5-25/12=19/60.\nFor its upper bound use a>41/100, so\n1/a-5a<100/41-41/20=319/820<2/5, and a s^2<q/200.\nFor Brel, |yclock0|<(7/5)bq, a<5/12 and Qgap>(99/100)q give\nBrel>2bq(1-7a/5)+(198/100)q>(7/6+99/50)q>3q.\nIts upper bound uses yclock0<0, Qgap<q and 2b+2<5. The lower clock capacity follows\nfrom b rrel<3q/4; the upper follows from Delta>20 and rrel>19q/60.\n\nBoth original source coordinates have been retained in the source-pair check.\nFor zrel,\n\n Hsrc0=q/a+s^2,\n Hsrc1=-bq/a-(1/(a beta)-1)s^2,\n P00=3q+q/a^2+(2/a+1)s^2,\n P01=K01-bq/a^2-bs^2/(a^2 beta),\n P11=K11+g^2s^2/(a^2 beta^2)+b^2Qgap/a^2,\n N01=2b Hsrc1-a(P01+A01),\n N10=2g Hsrc0-gP00-beta(P01-A01).                       (Source-pair)\n\nThe product z0z1 is unchanged, so P01 is unchanged; neither Hsrc0 nor Hsrc1 is.\nEliminating A01 yields\n\n beta N01+a N10\n =-gb(1-a)q/a-3agq-2a beta K01\n                         +[2-a(1+3beta)]Qgap.             (Pair-cut)\n\nThe first three terms are the fully expanded baseline pair cut, less than -q\nby Appendix A equation(7.1) and its displayed coefficient proof. This proof\nuses the exact retained K01; it is included in this joint draft. Passing this weighted test does\nnot assign signs separately to N01 and N10.\n\nTo avoid silently fixing the second source row, its remaining capacities and\nclock are explicit as well:\n\n N11=2g Hsrc1-g(P01+A01)-beta P11,\n mu1=2gq, (N^Te)1=0,\n outgoing row1 total=N11-2gq, incoming column1 total=N11,\n (Nt)1=-2g yclock0-2beta yclock1+2Qgap/a^2-2Qgap(2g-1)/a,\n sum_(j!=1)(t_j-t1)lambda1j=t1*(2gq)-(Nt)1. Therefore\n\n S3-rel <(3/4-99/100+1/25)q=-q/5.\n\nThe old three-entry obstruction is thus genuinely removed by this release.\nThe second result below is a different necessary test of the complete source\nflow, not reuse of S3-old with changed coordinates.\n\n## 6. Both complete global costs and the terminal corrections\n\nFor any original real column x, let Gamma_x,ij=(x_i-x_j)^2 and\nT_x=sum_(i,j)Gamma_x,ij N_ij. Complete row and column sums give\n\n T_x=(x^2)^Tmu+2alpha^T(x^2)-2(Lx)^T(P+A)x.\n\nFor x=t and x=w, use At=rvec, Lw=e and e^T(P+A)=2s v^T+2Fz^T to obtain\n\n T_t=(t^2)^Tmu+2alpha^T(t^2)\n                       -4ell^T(yclock+Vv+eta Zz-alpha),\n T_w=(w^2)^Tmu+2alpha^T(w^2)-4s v^Tw-4F z^Tw.           (Global-costs)\n\nEvery neutral global C cancels in these formulas. Every symmetric detail with\nzero e and t moments cancels as appropriate. Therefore the\nsame explicit estimates give 1<rlast<7 and\n\n g rlast<Blast<Delta rlast, Dlast>0.\n\nThe terminal-six symmetric costs C2,C3 and both trace cuts in Appendix A\nSection8 also keep their displayed formulas with z replaced by zrel. Their\nnew common-term bounds are respectively16delta^2,28delta^2,648delta^2 and\n288delta^2, all less1/100. The clock/detail/rank-one parts are unchanged,\nincluding both crossing details. Thus these selected tail screens still pass.\nNone of these tests constitutes a compatible simultaneous sign completion.\n\n## 7. A source first-moment inequality with no clock relaxation\n\nSuppose every outgoing source multiplier lambda0j=-N0j, j>0, is nonnegative.\nIts exact total is rrel and its exact first clock load is Brel from Section5.\nFor each i>0 define\n\n Lambda_i=lambda0i+h_i sum_(j>i)lambda0j.\n\nBecause all physical h_i>1 and all t_j-1>=t_i-1 for j>=i,\n\n Lambda_i <= h_i sum_(j>=i)lambda0j\n          <= h_i Brel/(t_i-1).                        (Clock-load)\n\nThis proves the proposed screen directly from the original row. Earlier\nsource edges contribute nonnegative amounts to Brel and may be omitted only\nin the displayed upper-bound step. Neither the total nor Brel is optimized\nor replaced by a freely chosen clock value.\n\nThe full incoming field with delta0=-E now gives, for every i>0,\n\n N_i0=2(L^TK)_i0-2F(L^Tzrel)_i-Lambda_i/a.               (Incoming)\n\nFor any index3<=i<l-1 the source-reaching detail d0 is entirely before i and\nall other details have zero source coordinate. Set\nU_i=1+sum_(j>i)h_j, using the entire original suffix. Then exactly\n\n (L^TK)_i0=-rho b h_i U_i/S.                            (All-metric-terms)\n\nThis includes all of Knew: it is not a dropped-detail approximation.\nAt such an i, the early source corrections in u and xi are before i; both\nterminal correction pairs have zero sum and are after i. Product telescoping\nsum_(j>i)h_jw_j=w_(i+1)+s therefore gives\n\n (L^Tzrel)_i=E gamma h_i[(2-h_i)w_i+s]/(1+s).             (Tail-field)\n\nAll final-two corrections are included by their exact zero suffix sum. This\nformula is used only BEFORE both of those indices, never at the anchors.\n\n## 8. Moving ORIGINAL level-one query and an absolute threshold\n\nNow require the single absolute threshold m>=M=2^64. Let r be the unique\ninteger with 2^r<=m<2^(r+1), and choose i=2^r+1. This is an ORIGINAL query,\nnot a new schedule or a renamed clock. We have r>=64, i<=m+1 and\n3<i<2^(m-1)<l-1. Thus all formulas of Section7 apply, the actual alpha bracket\nis later, and the early source patch and final anchors do not overlap i.\nThe integer i+1=2^r+2 is divisible by2 exactly once and pop(i)=2, so\n\n h_i=g=2-eps, w_i=-beta^i/c^2.\n\nInsert (Clock-load), (All-metric-terms), and (Tail-field) in (Incoming).\nSince 2Qgap gamma/(1+s)=q, the resulting complete bound is\n\n (a/h_i)N_i0 >= a q[eps beta^i/c^2-s]\n                   -2a rho b U_i/S-Brel/(t_i-1).       (Exact-demand)\n\nThis retains the whole negative clock-metric term and the physical remaining\nsuffix U_i. We estimate each term with elementary absolute inequalities.\n\nFirst, Bernoulli's inequality gives beta^i>1-i/(4m)>=3/4-1/(4m)>1/2.\nTogether with a>2/5 and c^2<25/4,\n\n a q eps beta^i/c^2 > q/(125m).                        (Positive-demand)\n\nSecond, the actual prefix before query i consists of H_r, the bridge\n1+a c^r, and the next first step b. With vword=a/(c-2)>4/5, the finite mass\nidentity in Appendix A gives\n\n t_i-1=S_r+1+a c^r+b\n      =(1-vword)2^r+(vword+a)c^r+b > c^r.\n\nNo prefix has its own enclosing deformation parameter. Because c>12/5 and\n(6/5)^4=1296/625>2,\n\n c^r>2^r 2^(r/4)>m 2^(r/4-1)>=2^15 m.\n\nSince Brel<5q and 5000<2^15, this proves\n\n Brel/(t_i-1)<q/(1000m).                              (Budget-cost)\n\nThird, U_i<S: the omitted prefix including h0 has mass greater1. As\n2ab<3, rho<125/S^2, q>1/(3S), and S>2^(m-1),\n\n 2a rho b U_i/S<3rho< (1125/S)q<2250*2^(-m)q\n                                      <q/(1000m).     (Metric-cost)\n\nFor the last inequality, m<=2^(m/2) for all integers m>=4; induction proves\nit from m=4 since (m+1)/m<=5/4<sqrt(2). When m>=64,\n2^(m/2)>=2^32>2250000, so 2^m>2250000 m. This establishes the stated bound\nwithout numerical evaluation of the actual word or metric.\n\nFourth, the finite product bound s<2^(-4m^2) and a<1/2 give\n\n a q s<q/(1000m).                                    (Product-cost)\n\nIndeed 500m<2^(9+m/2)<2^(4m^2) for m>=64, using m<=2^(m/2).\nAll bounds are strict and valid for every integer m>=M.\n\nSubtracting these THREE costs from (Positive-demand) in (Exact-demand) yields\n\n (a/h_i)N_i0 > [1/125-3/1000]q/m=q/(200m),\n\nwhich is (Moving-cut). Thus the complete nonnegative outgoing source row\nforces a forbidden positive incoming source entry. No choice of A01, any\nother source edge, a remote anchor, or a global neutral skew avoids this\nconclusion for the fixed released law and fixed Knew. This is the decisive\nsecond result. The promised two mechanisms are exhausted.\n\n## 9. Audit, provenance and remaining interface\n\nThe first mechanism was evaluated on the FULL source-column relation before\nthe source release. The release was the literal TASK formula, not an optimized\ncolumn. Its exact sum/clock constraints, PSD polynomial, changed skew moments,\nall finite coefficient changes, first two source coordinates, row/column\ncapacities, source clock load, both global costs and terminal corrections have\nbeen retained explicitly. The new moving query uses the same enclosing m,\nactual binary level, actual prefix mass and original incoming/outgoing edges.\nThe metric term in (Exact-demand) was bounded only after its full expression\nwas derived. Original coordinates, products, and actual alpha bracket\n\nFix the SAME enclosing integer m>=M0 in every child, and put\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(r+1)=(H_r,1+a c^r,H_r), h=H_m,\n    n=2^m-1, l=n-1, S_r=sum H_r, S=S_m, D=1+2S,\n    q=17/(16D), pi=sqrt(q), b=1+a, g=1+beta.\n\nThe original queried indices are0,...,l, and n is the unqueried terminal.\ne is the all-ones column in R^n; e_i denotes its standard coordinate columns.\nL=Ltri has diagonal1 and L_ij=h_j for j<i, zero for j>i. Set\n\n    t=L e, t_i=1+sum_(u<i)h_u, T=t_l=S-a,\n    Delta=T-1=S-b, Utotal=S+1, ell=L t.\n\nHere L is a matrix after smoothness normalization, not the original smoothness\nconstant. Utotal is a scalar; U below is a six-by-four detail matrix.\n\nUseful elementary enclosures are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1, 41/100<a<5/12,\n    7/5<b<17/12, 199/100<g<2, 17/5<H:=1+a c^2<7/2.\n\nFor the lower bound on a, sqrt(2)<99/70 gives\n(70/169)(6143/6145)>41/100. The upper bound uses sqrt(2)>7/5.\nFor H=1+(1+sqrt(2))(1-eps^2), use sqrt(2)>141/100 and eps<1/100.\nAll remaining bounds follow directly from eps<=1/6144.\n\nPut vword=a/(c-2). Summing the two children gives, by substitution,\n\n    1+S_r=(1-vword)2^r+vword c^r.\n\nThe identity c(c-2)=1+2(2+sqrt(2))eps+(1+sqrt(2))^2 eps^2\nimplies c(c-2)<1+7eps+6eps^2. Thus\nvword>(1-eps)/(1+7eps+6eps^2)>1-8eps=1-2/m>4/5,\nwhere the second comparison follows by multiplying positive denominators.\nAlso c(c-2)>1>beta, so vword<1. Consequently\n\n    2^m-1<S<c^m<2^(2m), 1/(4S)<q<1/S,\n    2^(-2m-2)<q<2^(-m), pi<2^(-m/2)<1/1000,\n    17/(48S)<q<17/(32S), D/S<201/100. Counting multiples of2^r shows that the sum of these levels\nin the first i steps is i-pop(i). Hence\n\n    w_i=product_(u<i)(1-h_u)=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-pop(i)),\n    s=product_(i<n)(h_i-1)=beta^n c^(-m)>0,\n    w_n=-s, |w_i|<=beta^i.\n\nPalindrome reversal gives the same formula for unsigned suffix products.\nDefine k_i=h_i product_(u>i)(1-h_u). Finite product telescoping gives\n\n    e^T k=1+s, L^T k=h, t^T k=S,\n    |k_i|<=2, k_i w_i=s h_i/(h_i-1), L w=e. At the source and final pair these identities give\n\n    w0=1,w1=-a,w2=a beta,w4=a beta^3,\n    w_l=s/a, w_(l-1)=-s/(a beta),\n    k0=bs/a,k1=-gs/(a beta),k_l=b. For the canonical skew change,\n\n    DeltaP=eta(zsrc dz^T+dz zsrc^T+dz dz^T),\n    Deltadvec=-F dz, Deltarvec=-(F/a)dz,\n    DeltaAcan=F(e dz^T-dz e^T)/n\n       +F(tmean-1/a)(dz cvec^T-cvec dz^T)/Ve,\n    DeltaN_ij=(DeltaP+DeltaAcan)_ij\n                       +h_i sum_(u>i)(DeltaP+DeltaAcan)_uj. Write\nQ_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\ny_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2, and Gpre=(g_0,...,g_l).\nThen y=e x0-L Gpre, x_n=-s x0+k^T y, and\nQ_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>. The change of original coordinates is\ninvertible. Flow (5.4)--(5.5) cancels all sampled function values. Expanding,\nusing Pe-Ae=2s v+2Fz and q=s^2+eta E^2, gives the FULL identity\n\n    q||x0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n       =||g_n+s x0-v^T y||^2+y^T Knew y\n                              +eta||z^T y-E x0||^2.        (5.7)\n\nThe initial cost q is paid once, and g_n remains a free formal gradient.\nThe right side is PSD. It is an upper certificate only if ALL lambda signs\nalso hold; no positivity claim about the polynomial substitutes for them.\n\n## 6. {"id": "37660b4102b12fe7", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-03T05:14:32.763800+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max3r86 JOINT UNVERIFIED: ENTIRE balanced-cap outward-kink family has all-start bound (1+21/(4m))/D for every same m>=1536; one precisely scoped separated-cap original-root tail escape has exact maximal score and uniform bound <204/(343D)<3/(5D).", "evidence": "# Independent caps with one common outward curvature kink\n\n**JOINT UNVERIFIED. No verifier call, accepted fact, or minimax resolution.**\n\nProject gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; invocation86 after ended85;\ncompleted ceiling86 / attempted87; one-invocation cap1. Assignment guidance\n01add6113f777746; plan d3bb77837e54072e. No renewal is inferred.\n\nThis report contains two substantive mechanisms, in the authorized order.\nFirst it derives an all-start bound for the ENTIRE balanced-cap region.\nSecond it pays one explicitly defined separated-cap original-root transfer\nwhose complete physical suffix stays in the smaller-cap tail. The latter\nis an itinerary result, not a bound for the whole separated-cap family.\n\nAll mathematical ingredients are proved together below. The earlier odd\ndraft 8a5a6aef2db4f411 and its public analytical audit c2c80ed9020960f5\nwere read for awareness. Neither is used as an accepted premise. The\ncurrent TASK forbids fact_submit, so the entire proof remains UNVERIFIED.\n\n## 1. Precise family, same-m word, and candidate conclusions\n\nFix ONE integer m>=1536. At every child depth retain\n\n    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, b=c-2, ell=1+a,\n    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,\n    S=S_m, D=1+2S, n=2^m-1.\n\nHere 0<=j<m in the word recursion, and d_m is an auxiliary scale, not\nan additional step. Write h_i, 0<=i<n, for the physical chronological\nentries of H_m. Every symbol in this report is local; for example the\nword H_j, mass A_j, ratio b, and ray functions do not redefine unrelated\nsymbols in the project glossary.\n\nFix 0<kappa<=1 and two finite caps B_+>=kappa, B_->=kappa. Put\n\n    v=1-kappa, u_sigma=v+B_sigma, sigma in {+1,-1},\n    Bmax=max(B_+,B_-), Bmin=min(B_+,B_-),\n    G_sigma(r)=min(B_sigma,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,\n    g(sigma*r)=sigma*G_sigma(r), g(0)=0,\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, F_sigma(r)=f(sigma*r).\n\nThe signs + and - in subscripts mean +1 and -1. The common core has\nradius1 and curvature kappa on BOTH sides. Caps are the only independent\nray parameters. This is one fixed function for the entire word.\n\nLet Psi_j(s) be the actual output of the complete H_j starting at s, using\nthis same f. Set p(x)=|g(x)|, including p(0)=0. For a full nonzero start s,\nwrite R=|s| and x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i). The terminal is x_n=Psi_m(s).\nThe true original distance to the unique minimizer will be R, on either ray.\n\n**Candidate balanced-cap theorem.** If\n\n    Bmin/Bmax >= b=c-2,\n\nthen, for EVERY permitted kappa, pair of caps, and nonzero start s,\n\n    2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <= (1+21/(4m))/D <17/(16D).       (1.1)\n\nNo function- or start-dependent eventual threshold appears: m>=1536\nsuffices. The first mechanism establishes (1.1) in Sections2-12.\n\n**Candidate separated-state theorem.** Without the balanced assumption,\nfix one ray with larger cap B and the opposite ray with smaller cap K,\nwhere kappa<=K<B. Set J=m-1, A=A_J, d=d_J, T=S_J=A-1. Consider exactly\nthe histories for which the entire left H_J and its root source stay\non the B tail, the original root crosses to the K ray, and the entire\nright H_J including its terminal stays on the K tail. Such a history\nexists precisely for original radii\n\n    v+AB <= R <= (A+d)B-AK-v.                         (1.2)\n\nThe interval is nonempty precisely when dB-AK>=2v; this condition\nitself implies K/B<b. On this state the exact terminal and payoff are\ngiven in Section13, and uniformly\n\n    2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <204/(343D)<3/(5D).              (1.3)\n\nThis is one paid escape into the smaller tail, with no return across\nzero in the suffix. It does not assert (1.1) for arbitrary separated\ncaps or arbitrary itineraries. A concrete nonempty member of this\nstate with a genuine outward kink is also given in Section13.\n\nNeither theorem is a bound on the full finite-dimensional smooth-convex\nsupremum W_n(H_m). In particular neither supplies the full-class\nhypothesis in accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201. The original minimax\nproblem in PROBLEM.md remains OPEN, and W=2R retains its original\nmeaning; the plain R used here is explicitly the original radius.\n\n## 2. Both exact ray energies and legality of the fixed objective\n\nFor EACH sigma separately the derivative and twice-energy are\n\n    G_sigma(r)=kappa*r,                     0<=r<=1,\n                 r-v,                      1<=r<=u_sigma,\n                 B_sigma,                  r>=u_sigma;\n\n    2F_sigma(r)=kappa*r^2,                  0<=r<=1,\n                 (r-v)^2+kappa*v,           1<=r<=u_sigma,\n                 2B_sigma*r-Ctail_sigma,   r>=u_sigma,\n    Ctail_sigma=B_sigma^2+v*(2B_sigma-kappa).         (2.1)\n\nIndeed the negative-ray substitution t=-z in the defining integral\ngives f(-r)=integral_0^r G_-(z)dz; hence its cap area must use B_-, not\nB_+. Integrating the core and middle gives the first two expressions.\nAt u_sigma the energy is B_sigma^2+kappa*v; adding\n2B_sigma*(r-u_sigma) gives the displayed Ctail_sigma. At1 both\nexpressions equal kappa. Values and first derivatives agree at both\nknots. The middle may collapse when B_sigma=kappa, separately on either\nray. If kappa=1, v=0 and the whole law is an asymmetric Huber law.\n\nAcross the entire real line g is continuous, nondecreasing, and has\npiecewise slopes kappa,1,0. Partition an arbitrary interval at its\nknots and at0; the increment of g is between zero and that interval's\nlength. Thus g is globally 1-Lipschitz. Its integral is finite,\ndifferentiable, convex, and 1-smooth. Since every G_sigma(r)>0 for r>0,\n0 is the unique minimizer, with f(0)=0. No evenness is assumed.\n\nThe entire actual transcript is smooth-convex legal. For x<y, put\nDelta=g(y)-g(x), so 0<=Delta<=y-x. On [x,y],\n\n    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),\n    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).\n\nBoth right sides integrate to Delta^2/2. This proves both ordered\ninterpolation rows, and therefore for every ordered pair of actual\nqueries, terminal, or minimizer,\n\n    f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.\n\nRepeated positions have identical jets. Their scalar Gram matrices\nare positive semidefinite because the associated quadratic forms\nare squares of real linear combinations. This is an actual function\nconstruction, not independent prescriptions for two child transcripts.\n\nFor each ray, throughout its domain,\n\n    0<=G_sigma(r)<=min(r,B_sigma),\n    G_sigma(r)>=kappa*min(r,1).                       (2.2)\n\nIf p=G_sigma(r), then G_sigma(t)<=min(t,p) on [0,r]. Integrating gives\n\n    2F_sigma(r)<=2r*p-p^2.                            (2.3)\n\nThere is also the strictly scoped conversion\n\n    2F_sigma(r)<=p^2/kappa  when r<=u_sigma.           (2.4)\n\nIt is equality in the core; in the middle the difference is\nv*(p^2-kappa^2)/kappa>=0. It is NOT asserted farther along a tail.\n\nA strict crossing means consecutive nonzero iterates have opposite\nsigns. A zero hit stays zero. In a segment with no strict crossing\nand no zero hit, all points stay on one ray and radii decrease. If\nthe segment has step mass M, original radius R, terminal radius r,\nand terminal gradient p on that same ray, monotonicity gives\n\n    R>=r+M*p,\n    2F_sigma(r)<=2R*p-(1+2M)*p^2<=R^2/(1+2M).        (2.5)\n\nThe last inequality is completion of the square. In particular all\nfull-word noncrossing histories have score<=1/D, independent of cap\nbalance. Multiplying f by any advertised smoothness L_adv>0 and using\nsteps h_i/L_adv leaves the actual points and normalized score unchanged.\n\n## 3. Quantitative masses and literal suffixes of the original word\n\nFor m>=1536 the following bounds follow directly:\n\n    0<epsilon<1/1000, beta>999/1000,\n    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,\n    2/5<a<1/2, 0<a<b<3/7<beta, ca=beta<1.          (3.1)\n\nFor c, use 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and\n(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7. Then\na>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5 and a<1/(12/5)<1/2.\nThe identity lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda give\nc*(c-2)>1>beta, proving b>a. The other comparisons follow from\nb<3/7 and beta>999/1000.\n\nLet eta=a/b, so 0<eta<1. The word masses obey\n\n    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, A_0=1,\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j.                         (3.2)\n\nSubstitution verifies the formula and its initial value. Consequently\n\n    A_j>d_j/b for every j>=0,\n    A_j<c^j for j>=1,\n    D<2c^m=2d_m/a.                                  (3.3)\n\nFor L_j=S_j/d_j and j>=1,\n\n    L_j>7/5.                                         (3.4)\n\nAt j=1 this follows from L_1=(1+a)/beta>7/5. The recurrence\nL_(j+1)=(2L_j+1+1/d_j)/c and c<17/7 preserve the strict bound,\nsince (19/5)/(17/7)>7/5.\n\nThe useful finite margins are\n\n    S>1000, d_(m-1)>9, d_(m-1)>1/epsilon,\n    d_m>2/a, d_m^2>D, D/S<201/100.                  (3.5)\n\nIndeed d_(m-1)>2^m/5. At integer m=8, 2^m>20m, and doubling\npreserves this inequality on increasing m by1. Thus\nd_(m-1)>4m=1/epsilon; for m>=1536 this also gives the bounds9\nand1000, using S>=1+d_(m-1). Since 2/a<5, the bound d_m>2/a\nand then d_m^2>D follow from (3.3). Finally D/S=2+1/S.\n\nA physical level-k bridge in a complete H_j has a chronological\nprefix containing its full immediate left H_k, hence prefix mass\nat least S_k. Its physical suffix is exactly\n\n    H_k, (1+d_(l_1)),H_(l_1), ...,\n         (1+d_(l_t)),H_(l_t),\n    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1.                              (3.6)\n\nAscend its binary word tree: each ancestor reached through its left\nchild contributes its bridge and complete right child; each reached\nthrough its right child contributes nothing. This proves (3.6),\nincluding empty suffixes and skipped ancestor levels. All later\narguments use this physical suffix, not a selected subsequence.\n\n## 4. Exact SIGNED interval images, including unavailable pieces\n\nFix h=1+d with d>=0. For a source on ray sigma, express its signed\noutput relative to that source sign as T_sigma(r)=r-hG_sigma(r).\nThen\n\n    T_sigma(r)=(1-h*kappa)*r,           0<=r<=1,\n                 h*v-d*r,             1<=r<=u_sigma,\n                 r-h*B_sigma,         r>=u_sigma.    (4.1)\n\nAdjacent formulas agree at the knots. Define [z]_+=max(z,0).\nFor an entire source interval 0<=r<=R the exact greatest positive\nsame-ray output and opposite-ray output are respectively\n\n    Stay_sigma(R)=max([1-h*kappa]_+*min(R,1),\n                      [R-h*B_sigma]_+),\n    Cross_sigma(R)=max([h*kappa-1]_+*min(R,1),\n                       [d*min(R,u_sigma)-h*v]_+).     (4.2)\n\nTo prove these expressions, first maximize each affine piece. On\nthe core its signed maximum and minimum are its appropriate endpoint\nor zero. On a present middle piece, T_sigma is nonincreasing: its\ngreatest positive value is already the core endpoint at1, and its\nmost negative value occurs at min(R,u_sigma). On a present tail,\nT_sigma is increasing: its positive maximum is the endpoint at R,\nand its most negative value is at u_sigma, already represented by\nthe middle endpoint. The origin always supplies zero.\n\nThere are two possible formal pieces that are not present. If R<1,\n\n    dR-hv-(h*kappa-1)R=hv*(R-1)<=0,\n\nso the formal middle crossing term is dominated by the actual core\ncrossing term. If R<u_sigma, the formal tail stay R-hB_sigma is\nat most the actual endpoint R-hG_sigma(R), hence cannot exceed the\ntrue stay maximum. If that formal value is positive, the actual\npre-cap endpoint is positive and already accounted for by the core\nendpoint. This proves both maxima without adding unattained branches.\n\nFor an input interval [-P,Q], where P,Q>=0, the exact output is\n[-Pnext,Qnext] with\n\n    Qnext=max(Stay_+(Q),Cross_-(P)),\n    Pnext=max(Stay_-(P),Cross_+(Q)).                   (4.3)\n\nThe maxima are attained on the two compact source intervals. The\nwhole step map is continuous and fixes0, so its image is exactly\nthat interval, with no holes. Inductively the image of any interval\ncontaining0 under an entire word is an interval containing0. In\ngeneral Pnext and Qnext differ: oddness and a symmetric radius\nrecurrence are not used.\n\nEquations(4.1)-(4.3) cover R=0, R=1, R=u_sigma, B_sigma=kappa,\nv=0, d=0, h*kappa=1, and zero outputs because the adjacent affine\nformulas and positive parts agree. For d=0 a positive pre-cap\nmiddle stay can equal1-kappa; the actual hierarchy has d_j>0.\n\nFor a STRICT crossing from actual radius r on ray sigma, with\np=G_sigma(r) and opposite-ray landing z>0,\n\n    z=(1+d)p-r<=d*p.                                 (4.4)\n\nThis uses p<=r and is independent of the destination cap. Its\ndestination gradient satisfies G_(-sigma)(z)<=z, not necessarily\nG_sigma(z); this distinction is retained below.\n\nFor a positive same-ray output from a strictly pre-cap source\nr<u_sigma, one necessarily has h*kappa<1, the output lies in\nthe core, and it is at most both\n\n    (1-h*kappa)*r and (1-h*kappa)*min(r,1).            (4.5)\n\nIn the core this is (4.1). In the middle write the output as\n1-h*kappa-d*(r-1), and use r>=1. A cap-knot source is instead\nassigned to the saturated case; its two output formulas agree.\n\nIf all steps of H_j have h*kappa<=1, the closed core [-1,1]\nis absorbing, regardless of the caps, with full product\n\n    Pcore_j=product_(h in H_j)(1-kappa*h)\n       <=exp(-kappa*S_j).                            (4.6)\n\nThe factors are nonnegative and 1-z<=exp(-z); no physical step\nor terminal-only entry is replaced by an extra factor.\n\n## 5. First exterior crossing: payment from the ORIGINAL radius\n\nConsider the first strict crossing in a complete-word trajectory,\nwith exterior source r>1 on a definite ray sigma. Put\np=G_sigma(r), let k be its physical level, P its chronological\nprefix mass from the original start of that word, and z its\nlanding radius. Earlier radii decrease on this same source ray,\nand their gradients are at least p. Exterior sources have r>=v+p:\nequality in the middle, inequality in the tail. Thus\n\n    R>=r+P*p>=v+(1+P)p>=v+A_k*p,\n    z<=d_k*p-v.                                      (5.1)\n\nThe last inequality is the actual update, not a new starting\ncondition for a child. It uses no property of the destination cap.\nSince A_k>d_k/b and R-v>=A_kp>0,\n\n    z <=(d_k/A_k)*(R-v)-v\n       < b*(R-v)-v=:Lland(R).                        (5.2)\n\nIn particular when v>0, strict crossing requires p>v/d_k and\n\n    R>v*Qprice, Qprice=(c-1)/(c-2)>10/3.             (5.3)\n\nThe inequality Qprice>10/3 follows from b<3/7. In a nonempty\ncomplete word whose steps preserve the core, a first crossing\nmust have exterior source; any earlier core entry is absorbing.\nSuch a word has v>0 because all its steps exceed1. These facts\njustify (5.3) whenever it is invoked for a core-preserving child.\n\nWhenever the original denominator is needed below, R in (5.1)\nis explicitly the start of H_m or its original left prefix,\nwhich has that same start. Applying (5.3) to a right child is\nused only to rule out a crossing there, never to reset the\nfull-word denominator.\n\n## 6. Joint signed gradient grid and crossing capture: balanced caps\n\nIn this section assume Bmin>=b*Bmax. Since b>a,\n\n    a*Bmax < Bmin <= B_sigma on either ray.           (6.1)\n\nFor EVERY j>=1 through m prove jointly:\n\n    p(Psi_j(s))<=a*c^(-q)*Bmax\n      for every integer q>=0 and |s|<=d_j*c^(-q)*Bmax; (6.2)\n\n    any strict crossing within a complete H_j\n      implies p(Psi_j(s))<=a*Bmax.                   (6.3)\n\nThe function and both caps stay fixed at every q. The grid bounds\nthe actual terminal gradient on its actual sign; it is not a radial\nmap for an odd extension and does not replace either cap by w.\n\nFirst give the derivation of capture at any depth using only grid\nclaims at proper-child depths. A level-k crossing has landing\nradius<=d_k*Bmax by (4.4). Its immediate full right H_k therefore\nhas terminal gradient<=aBmax if k>=1, by its q=0 grid. If k=0,\nthe empty child leaves a landing whose destination gradient is\nat most its radius<=aBmax. At any subsequent retained ancestor\nof level l in (3.6), (6.1) puts the incoming point strictly\nbefore the cap of its actual ray. If that bridge crosses, its\nlanding is at most d_l*aBmax<=d_lBmax, and the full following\nH_l restores the bound by its grid. If it has a positive\nsame-ray stay, (4.5) puts that stay in the core, with no gradient\nincrease. It also gives kappa*(1+d_l)<1, so the subsequent\nsmaller steps of H_l preserve the core and cannot increase\nits gradient. Zero is absorbing. Every suffix step is covered\nby (3.6); every grid depth is less than the depth of the entire\nword whose capture is being established.\n\nFor the base j=1, put w=c^(-q)Bmax and R=beta*w=d_1*w. Unlike\nthe odd proof, the smaller-cap ray MAY already be saturated.\nThere are three cases at the single step h=ell=1+a.\n\n* A crossing lands at radius<=a*p(s)<=a|s|<=a*beta*w<a*w.\n  Its destination gradient is no greater than that radius.\n* A positive pre-cap stay has output gradient at most\n  kappa*[1-ell*kappa]_+*min(R,1)<=R/(4ell)<a*R<a*w.\n  Here 4aell>4*(2/5)*(7/5)>1.\n* A positive saturated stay on ray sigma has radius at most\n\n      beta*w-ell*B_sigma <= [beta-ell*b]*w <a*w.       (6.4)\n\n  The first inequality uses B_sigma>=bBmax>=bw. For the strict\n  second inequality, ell*b-(beta-a)=b-a>0, since beta=ac.\n  The output gradient is at most its radius, including if that\n  output itself is still saturated. If the displayed upper\n  radius is negative, this stay case is simply impossible.\n\nZero gives zero. This proves (6.2) at j=1, and the preceding\nproper-child suffix argument proves (6.3) there.\n\nNow assume both claims through j>=1. For the parent take any\nactual start with radius R<=c*d_j*w, w=c^(-q)Bmax. Let y be\nthe radius of its actual left-child output on its actual ray\nsigma, and z the radius after the parent bridge. If y=0 the\nrest is zero. Partition the nonzero bridge as follows.\n\n(a) Strict crossing. If q=0, G_sigma(y)<=Bmax=w. If q>=1, the\nleft child starts within d_j*c^(-(q-1))*Bmax, so its grid gives\nG_sigma(y)<=a*c*w=beta*w<=w. The bridge lands at radius<=d_j*w.\nThe complete right-child grid gives terminal gradient<=a*w.\n\n(b) Positive saturated same-ray stay, including y=u_sigma.\nThe left child could not have crossed, because its capture\nwould imply G_sigma(y)<=aBmax<B_sigma, contradicting saturation.\nIt could not have hit zero. Thus all its points remain on\nthe current ray, with radii decreasing to y>=u_sigma; all\nits queried gradients are B_sigma. Consequently\n\n    y=R-S_j*B_sigma,\n    z=R-(A_j+d_j)*B_sigma\n       <=c*d_j*w-(A_j+d_j)*B_sigma <d_j*w.          (6.5)\n\nTo verify the last payment, use (3.3) and balance:\n\n    (A_j+d_j)*B_sigma\n       >=(A_j+d_j)*b*Bmax\n       >(1+b)*d_j*Bmax\n       >=(c-1)*d_j*w.\n\nThen the full right H_j uses its q-th grid. A prior crossing\nis ruled out before asserting the all-tail prefix; no tail\nassumption is inferred from the endpoint alone.\n\n(c) Positive pre-cap same-ray stay, with no crossing in the\nleft child. By (4.5), kappa*(1+d_j)<1, and the right child\nstays in the core. Put t=kappa*d_j and L=L_j=S_j/d_j.\nThe noncrossing left child gives\n\n    R>=y+S_j*G_sigma(y)>=(1+kappa*S_j)*y,\n\nwhere G_sigma(y)>=kappa*y is used only at this pre-cap source.\nCombining (4.5)-(4.6), R<=c*d_j*w, and 0<t<1 gives\n\n    p_out<=kappa*exp(-Lt)*(1-kappa-t)*R/(1+Lt)\n       <=c*t*(1-t)*exp(-Lt)/(1+Lt)*w\n       <=c*t*(1-t)/(1+Lt)^2*w\n       <=c/[4*(1+L)]*w<a*w.                         (6.6)\n\nThe third line uses exp(Lt)>=1+Lt; the fourth follows from\n\n    (1+Lt)^2-4*(1+L)*t*(1-t)=(1-(L+2)t)^2.\n\nFinally c/[4*(1+L)]<85/336<2/5<a by (3.1),(3.4).\n\n(d) Positive pre-cap same-ray stay, with a crossing in the\nleft child. Again kappa*(1+d_j)<1, so both children preserve\ntheir cores. The first crossing in that original left child\nhas exterior source and pays R>vQprice by (5.3), using the\nactual parent start. Here 0<t<v. The parent bridge output\nis at most 1-kappa-t=v-t, and its full right child yields\n\n    p_out<=kappa*(v-t)*exp(-Lt).\n\nSince R<=c*d_j*w and R>vQprice, w>vQprice/(c*d_j), giving\n\n    p_out/w<c*t*(v-t)*exp(-Lt)/(vQprice)\n       <=c*v/(4Qprice)<=c/(4Qprice)<a.              (6.7)\n\nWe used t*(v-t)<=v^2/4 and\nc/(4Qprice)<51/280<2/5<a. All divisions by v are in the\ncore-preserving case v>0.\n\nA zero bridge is already settled. These are all bridge\noutcomes and all first-child crossing possibilities. Thus\n(6.2) holds at j+1. The proper-child suffix argument then\ngives (6.3) at j+1. This is a noncircular joint induction.\n\nIn particular every crossing history ends strictly before\nits ACTUAL terminal-side cap knot, since\n\n    p(x_terminal)<=aBmax<Bmin<=B_terminal-side.       (6.8)\n\nThe balanced ratio is used in (6.4),(6.5),(6.8), not silently\nextended to a cap ratio below b.\n\n## 7. Global gradient control and the asymmetric Huber reference\n\nFor 0<R<=d_jBmax choose an integer q>=0 with\n\n    d_j*c^(-(q+1))*Bmax<R<=d_j*c^(-q)*Bmax.\n\nAn exact grid boundary is the upper endpoint. For every actual\nstart |s|<=R, (6.2) gives terminal gradient\n<=a*c^(-q)Bmax<beta*R/d_j. For R>d_jBmax, a crossing history\nuses (6.3) and aBmax<beta*R/d_j. A noncrossing history on\nits own ray, with terminal radius r and gradient p, has\n\n    |s|>=r+S_j*p>=A_j*p,\n    p<=R/A_j<b*R/d_j<beta*R/d_j.\n\nA zero hit gives p=0. Thus at all j>=1 and R>0,\n\n    p(Psi_j(s))<=beta*R/d_j for every |s|<=R.        (7.1)\n\nNow define the asymmetric unit-curvature Huber objective J\nwhose signed derivative on ray sigma is sigma*min(r,B_sigma).\nWe first assume Bmin>=1 and Bmin/Bmax>=b. This is exactly the\npresent family with kappa=1, common knot1, v=0, and the same\ntwo caps. The already derived grid and capture apply directly.\nOn a crossing history its terminal is pre-cap on its actual\nside by (6.8), so its twice-energy is p_terminal^2. Equation\n(7.1) and d_m^2>D give its score<=beta^2/d_m^2<1/D. Every\nnoncrossing history has score<=1/D by (2.5). Hence\n\n    2J(Psi_m^J(s))<=s^2/D.                          (7.2)\n\nFor arbitrary positive balanced caps, scale BOTH caps by their\ncommon divisor Bmin: write x=Bmin*y and\n\n    J(x)=Bmin^2*J_tilde(x/Bmin),\n\nwhere J_tilde has caps B_sigma/Bmin>=1. The signed GD recursion\nin y uses the identical physical word; the original radius and\nenergy scale by Bmin and Bmin^2. Thus (7.2) holds for every\npositive pair of balanced caps. This scale change is only a\nproof of the auxiliary Huber reference, not a replacement of\nthe original kink function or its core knot.\n\nFor this J, the exact signed interval update has ray maxima\n\n    Stay_sigma^J(R)=[R-hB_sigma]_+,\n    Cross_sigma^J(R)=d*min(R,B_sigma), h=1+d.         (7.3)\n\nInside the Huber core the signed output is -dr. On its tail\nit is r-hB_sigma. The largest crossing is the core endpoint\nat min(R,B_sigma), and the largest positive stay is the tail\nendpoint at R, if positive. These give (7.3) even for absent\ntails. The pair update is (4.3) with these two functions. Both\nfunctions, hence both pair coordinates, are nondecreasing in\nthe relevant nonnegative radii.\n\nSuppose now that an actual kink history has EVERY queried\npoint and its terminal at radius>1. Start the auxiliary J\ninterval at [-R,R], with R=|s|, and propagate its exact two\nsigned endpoints through the original physical word. Inductively\nthe actual signed kink point lies within this Huber interval.\nFor if its next step crosses from side sigma, (4.4),(2.2) give\nlanding<=d*min(r,B_sigma), controlled by that side's crossing\nterm in (7.3). If it stays on its side with positive output,\na pre-cap middle source would land in the core by (4.5),\ncontrary to the exterior-history assumption. Hence this stay\nis saturated and equals r-hB_sigma, controlled by the same-side\nstay term. Coordinate monotonicity proves the induction.\n\nLet the actual terminal side be sigma_n. The corresponding\nsigned endpoint of the final Huber interval has radius at\nleast |x_n|. It is attained by an ACTUAL Huber trajectory\nstarting at some s_* in [-R,R], because the complete map is\ncontinuous on that compact interval. Both ray energies are\nnondecreasing in radius, and G_sigma<=min(r,B_sigma) gives\nF_sigma(r)<=J(sigma*r). Therefore\n\n    2f(x_n)<=2J(Psi_m^J(s_*))<=s_*^2/D<=R^2/D.       (7.4)\n\nThe selected endpoint is on the actual terminal side. No\nevenness, oddness, single symmetric envelope, or artificial\ndenominator is used. The comparison imposes no inner-reversal\ncondition: its premise is the actual history remaining exterior.\n\n## 8. All starts with large curvature mass\n\nAssume kappa*S>=12 and balanced caps. Noncrossing histories\nuse (2.5). Crossing histories end strictly pre-cap on the\nactual terminal side by (6.8). Thus (2.4),(7.1) give\n\n    D*2f(x_n)/R^2<=D*beta^2/(kappa*d_m^2)\n       =(D/S)*(beta*S/d_m)^2/(kappa*S)\n       <(201/100)*(17/7)^2/12\n       =58089/58800<1.                              (8.1)\n\nHere beta*S/d_m<c follows from A_m<c^m=d_m/a.\nNo limitation on previous sign changes, tail visits, or core\nreturns is imposed. The terminal-side energy conversion is\nlegitimate solely because strict pre-cap capture was proved.\n\n## 9. No inner reversal and the residual parameter band\n\nAssume first kappa*(1+d_(m-1))<=1. All physical steps preserve\nthe closed core. Noncrossing histories use (2.5). Crossing\nhistories that never enter that core use (7.4). For a crossing\nhistory with any core entry, the first crossing has exterior\nsource and pays R>vQprice; the terminal stays in the core on\nwhichever side it has reached, so 2f(x_n)<=kappa. Hence\n\n    score:=2f(x_n)/R^2<kappa/(v^2*Qprice^2).           (9.1)\n\nThis includes a first core entry at the actual terminal.\nPut d=d_(m-1) and t_c=c*(c-2). The increasing function\nz/(1-z)^2 on (0,1), evaluated at kappa<=1/(1+d), gives\nkappa/v^2<=(1+d)/d^2. With D<2c^m and\n(c-1)^2=t_c+1, obtain\n\n    D*kappa/(v^2*Qprice^2)\n       <[2*t_c^2/(beta*(t_c+1))]*(1+1/d).           (9.2)\n\nFor example 2c^m/d=2c/a=2c^2/beta gives this expression\nby direct substitution. The identity lambda^2=2lambda+1 yields\n\n    t_c=1+2*(lambda+1)*epsilon+lambda^2*epsilon^2\n       <1+8epsilon.\n\nIndeed lambda<5/2 and epsilon<1/1000 bound its coefficient\nabove1 by 7+(25/4)/1000<8. Since t_c>1 and d>1/epsilon,\nthe right side of (9.2) is less than\n\n    (1+8epsilon)^2*(1+epsilon)/(1-epsilon).\n\nThe exact difference\n(1-epsilon)*(1+19epsilon)-(1+8epsilon)^2\n=2epsilon-83epsilon^2 is positive. Therefore the last\nquantity is less than (1+19epsilon)*(1+epsilon)<=1+21epsilon,\nusing 19epsilon<=1. This proves (1.1) in this parameter region.\nAlso 21/(4m)<=21/6144<1/16, proving its strict final envelope.\n\nThe remaining parameters satisfy kappa*S<12 and\nkappa*(1+d_(m-1))>1. Write d=d_(m-1). From (3.3),\n\n    kappa*S>S/(1+d)>[c*d/b-1]/(1+d)>5.              (9.3)\n\nFor the final inequality, c/b>17/3 and d>9 imply\n(c/b-5)d>(2/3)*9=6. On 12/5<c<17/7,\n\n    b/c^2<3/40:\n\nthe polynomial 3c^2-40c+80 decreases there and is27/49>0\nat17/7. Since d_m<b(S+1),\n\n    kappa*(1+d_(m-2))\n       <12*[1/S+(3/40)*(1+1/S)]\n       =9/10+129/(10S)<1.                           (9.4)\n\nHere S>1000. Set J=m-1. The ORIGINAL root is consequently\nthe sole physical step that can reverse a core input; both\ncomplete H_J children individually preserve that core. In\nthis whole residual band,\n\n    5<kappa*S<12,\n    v>49/50, D*kappa<603/25<25.                     (9.5)\n\nIndeed kappa<12/S<1/50 and D/S<201/100. The equality\nkappa*S=12 belongs to Section8; equality at the largest\ncore-nonreversal threshold belongs to the first region here.\n\n## 10. Root source in the core, including an exterior root landing\n\nIn the residual band put y=|Psi_J(s)|. If y=0 the terminal\nis zero. Suppose 0<y<=1. Regardless of its sign the root\ngradient has magnitude kappa*y and the root reverses, with\nactual opposite-ray landing z=q*y, where\n\n    t=kappa*d_J, q=kappa*(1+d_J)-1=t-v>0,\n    L=S_J/d_J>7/5.\n\nThe child core nonreversal in (9.4) implies\n\n    v<t<=c*v, q<(3/2)*v, q<(3/5)*t.                (10.1)\n\nFor the last two bounds use c<5/2, q<= (c-1)v,\nand q/t<=1-1/c<3/5. The root landing may exceed1.\n\nThe complete right H_J cannot strictly cross. Such a crossing\nwould have an exterior first source because this child\npreserves the core, and (5.3) would require its OWN starting\nradius z>vQprice. But z<=q<3v/2<vQprice. Thus it stays on\nthe root's destination ray until possibly hitting zero.\nIt must enter the core by its actual terminal: otherwise\nall its queried gradients are at least kappa by (2.2),\ngiving terminal radius<=z-kappa*S_J<=q-Lt<0, impossible.\n\nFor a bound that retains the entire return and suffix, let\n\n    C_h(r)=r-h*kappa*min(r,1)\n\nfor each actual right-child step. Since 0<=h*kappa<=1,\nC_h is nonnegative and nondecreasing on [0,infinity).\nAlong the noncrossing actual child, (2.2) gives\nr_next<=C_h(r); monotonicity bounds its terminal by the\nfull chronological composition of these C_h maps from z.\n\nFor r>0 define Bfun(r)=log r for r<=1 and r-1 for r>=1.\nFor alpha in [0,1] and a positive output,\n\n    Bfun(r-alpha*min(r,1))<=Bfun(r)-alpha.           (10.2)\n\nInside the core this is log(1-alpha)<=-alpha. Outside\nwith outside output it is equality; an outside-to-core\ntransition uses log w<=w-1 at w=r-alpha. Zero stays zero.\nSince Bfun(z)<Lt (if z>1 use z-1<q<Lt), summing over\nEVERY actual child step makes the final comparison radius\nless than1 and at most Kfun(z)*exp(-Lt), where\n\n    Kfun(z)=z for 0<=z<=1; exp(z-1) for z>=1.\n\nFor 0<=y<=1,\n\n    Kfun(q*y)<=y*Kfun(q).                            (10.3)\n\nIf q<=1 this is equality. If q>1 and qy<=1, it uses\nq<=exp(q-1). If qy>=1, differentiate exp(qy-1)/y:\nits derivative has the sign of qy-1, so its maximum on\n[1/q,1] is exp(q-1). These cases prove (10.3). Hence\n\n    |x_n|<=y*Z, Z=Kfun(q)*exp(-Lt).                 (10.4)\n\nUniformly Z<1/4. If q<=1, Lt>7q/3 from (10.1), so\nZ<q*exp(-7q/3)<=3/(7e)<1/4, with e=exp(1)>2.\nThe maximum follows by differentiating w*exp(-7w/3).\nIf q>1, Z<exp(-1-4q/3)<exp(-7/3)<1/4 because\nexp(7/3)>e^2>4.\n\nPay the ORIGINAL R separately. If the original left child\ndid not cross, all its radii decreased on its original ray\nto y, whose gradient is kappa*y. Thus\nR>=(1+kappa*S_J)*y=(1+Lt)*y. By (9.5),\nLt>(7/5)*(49/50)>1, so y/R<1/2. If the left child did\ncross, its first source is exterior and (5.3) pays the\noriginal R>vQprice>49/15>3; as y<=1, now y/R<1/3.\nThe terminal is in the common core, so (9.5),(10.4) give\n\n    D*2f(x_n)/R^2<25/64<1/2  in the noncrossing-left case,\n    D*2f(x_n)/R^2<25/144<1/2 in the crossing-left case. (10.5)\n\nBoth actual signs, both destination caps, an exterior root\nlanding, and entry into the core only at the actual terminal\nare included. The argument uses no even objective values.\n\n## 11. Original-radius landing price in the residual band\n\nFor the first strict crossing of the FULL trajectory with\nexterior source, (5.2) is\n\n    z<Lland(R)=b*(R-v)-v.\n\nDefine\n\n    Rcrit=v+(1+v)/b, Lland(Rcrit)=1.                 (11.1)\n\nAny landing z>=1 forces R>Rcrit, including the tie z=1,\nbecause (5.2) is strict. In the residual band b<3/7 and\nv>49/50 imply\n\n    Rcrit>v+(7/3)*(1+v)\n       =(10/3)v+7/3>28/5,\n    D*kappa/Rcrit^2<(603/25)/(28/5)^2=603/784<1.     (11.2)\n\nIf R<=Rcrit and every step AFTER this first crossing\npreserves the core, its landing is below1 and all later\nradii are at most z. Also\n\n    Lland(R)/R=b-(c-1)*v/R\n\nincreases with R>0 and equals1/Rcrit at Rcrit. Hence\n\n    |x_n|/R<=1/Rcrit.                               (11.3)\n\nIf R>Rcrit and a later core entry is absorbing, then\n|x_n|<=1 also gives |x_n|/R<1/Rcrit. No assertion that\nthe original root preserves a core input is made here;\nits location relative to each entry is checked next.\n\n## 12. Exterior root source: exhaustive balanced-region closure\n\nRemain in the residual band, and suppose y=|Psi_J(s)|>1.\nThe original left child never entered the closed core,\nbecause it preserves that core and ends outside. Thus\nevery point through the root source is exterior. Zero\nhits are settled; histories with no strict crossing use\n(2.5). Consider the remaining histories.\n\nCase A: the original left child had a crossing. Its first\ncrossing is the full trajectory's first; both source and\nlanding are exterior because a core landing would stay\ncore through the child endpoint. Thus (11.1) pays\nR>Rcrit. If the root output or any later point enters the\ncore, every remaining step is in the right H_J and\npreserves it. The actual terminal energy is at most\nkappa, and (11.2) gives score<603/(784D). If no such\nentry occurs, the entire original history, all queries\nand the terminal, is exterior; apply the signed asymmetric\nHuber comparison (7.4). Repeated exterior crossings on\neither side are included.\n\nCase B: the original left child had no crossing. The first\ncrossing is at the root or inside the right child. Its\nsource is exterior: at the root this is y>1; in the right\nchild an earlier core entry would have been absorbing\nand would forbid the crossing. Every step after this first\ncrossing belongs to the core-preserving right H_J.\n\nIf R<=Rcrit, (11.3) gives a core terminal with\n|x_n|/R<=1/Rcrit, so score<603/(784D). If R>Rcrit\nand a point from the first crossing onward enters the\ncore, it stays there and gives the same bound. If R>Rcrit\nand there is no such entry, the full history is exterior:\nthe original left child was exterior, and any earlier\nright-child core entry would have forbidden the first\ncrossing.
