{"id": "a40ecd591138eb2c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-03T05:16:04.672423+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max4 round92 JOINT UNVERIFIED: full unsaturated-terminal original-row interface and nu-corrected affine PSD region derived; frozen tail-source rank-two paid family excluded for ALL theta,xi by exact original source cut mu0-P00<-3a/(4S). General unsaturated architectures and original target remain OPEN.", "evidence": "# Unsaturated terminal mass: full original coefficient interface and a source-capacity obstruction\n\nStatus: JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max4, continuation invocation92, guidance\n52e9d99015702be8, plan7000802aea7e2692. This is one joint analytical draft.\nThe current TASK grants zero formal calls; no new statement in this report is\na verifier-accepted fact. Original target OPEN. W=2R throughout.\n\n## 1. Result and scope\n\nThe general terminal-square and original-row flow interface is derived below\nwithout imposing q=pi^2. The proposed constant-gradient Gram is correct when\nall optimum-to-query multipliers vanish. When those multipliers are allowed,\nthe two additional terms are displayed explicitly in (3.1).\n\nAt q0=1/D, nonnegative multipliers and the actual tight Huber trajectory force\nthose optimum incoming rows to vanish. The constant-gradient restriction then\nforces V+1=D*pi and pi^2<=q0. For the specified terminal distribution supported\nonly at l=n-1, this fixes pi=b/(S+b), strictly below sqrt(q0). It leaves real\nfreedom in the mixed initial-point/gradient coefficients; this freedom is not\npresent under saturation q=pi^2.\n\nThe ONE literal paid family tested at q=17/(16D) is\n\n M_trial = ||g_n-pi*x0+r^T g||^2\n         + delta ||x0-u^T g||^2 + gamma ||dsrc^T g||^2\n         + kappa ||x0+theta*dsrc^T g||^2 + xi ||dtail^T g||^2,       (1.1)\n\nwhere theta is any real number, xi>=0, and all columns and coefficients are\nspecified in Section5. Its paid block has rank at most two, genuine mixed x0\nterms, zero-sum gradient columns, and an adjustable terminal-three detail.\nThat detail can pay the elementary last-query column deficit of the baseline.\n\nNevertheless the ENTIRE family is excluded by an exact original source cut:\n\n a * sum_(j=1)^l lambda_(0,j) = mu0-P00\n  = -2ab*k + a^2*b^2/T^2 - gamma/b^2 + kappa\n                              - (kappa/b^2)(theta+b)^2\n  < -3a/(4S) < 0.                                            (1.2)\n\nHere P=sym(N Ltri), mu_i=lambda_(i,star), and k=(S-ab)/((S+b)T).\nEvery weight on the left must be nonnegative. Equation (1.2) holds for every\ntheta, every xi>=0, every compatible skew completion, and every enclosing\nm>=1536. It is not the failure of a particular chosen array.\n\nThe whole unit-quadratic polynomial separately forces theta=0 or theta=-2.\nBoth survive all linear flow/skew compatibility equations and both fail (1.2).\nThe theta=-2 case explicitly spends the loose target in mixed terms. Thus the\nfailure is not an artifact of retaining only the q0 certificate search.\n\nThis excludes the displayed family, not all unsaturated pi, not all tail-source\ncertificates, and not the hierarchy itself. No primal lower bound or full W\nupper bound follows. The missing hypothesis of accepted931b9653ce0f7201 remains\nunproved.\n\n## 2. Original rows and the general full coefficient interface\n\nNormalize smoothness to1 and the optimum to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let h be\nany positive word of length n>=2, let l=n-1, and retain the original formal\nvectors x0,g0,...,g_l,g_n. The final vector g_n is not queried in the updates:\n\n x_i=x0-sum_(u<i)h_u*g_u,   0<=i<=n.\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.\n\nAll distinct ordered rows involving star, queries, and terminal are available.\nIn the selected architecture only incoming-terminal multipliers are set to\nzero. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, parts(b),(c), licenses that selection for\ntight certificates and gives the exact terminal-square interface. It does\nNOT license deleting either orientation of any queried-prefix row.\n\nWrite\n\n lambda_(n,star)=2pi, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n alpha_i>=0, pi>=0, pi+sum_i alpha_i=1,\n lambda_(i,n)=lambda_(star,n)=0.\n\nInitially retain BOTH optimum directions:\n\n mu_i=lambda_(i,star)>=0,  nu_i=lambda_(star,i)>=0.\n\nLet e be the n-dimensional all-ones column, e_i its coordinate columns, and\nLtri the n-by-n matrix with diagonal1, entry h_j in row i,column j when j<i,\nand zero above the diagonal. Write g=(g0,...,g_l)^T; expressions involving its\ncolumns mean the corresponding sums of Euclidean vectors and inner products.\nSet\n\n y=e*x0-Ltri*g,  zeta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\n t=Ltri*e,  t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j,  S=sum_i h_i, D=1+2S.\n\nDirect expansion gives\n\n Q_ij=zeta_i-zeta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>,\n Q_(i,star)=zeta_i-<g_i,y_i>,  Q_(star,i)=-zeta_i.\n\nDefine an n-by-n real matrix N from the original prefix multipliers by\n\n N_ij=-lambda_(i,j) for i!=j,\n N_ii=mu_i+sum_(j!=i)lambda_(i,j).\n\nExact cancellation of every f_i is equivalent to\n\n Ne=mu,             N^T e=2alpha+nu.                       (2.1)\n\nIndeed the coefficient of f_i before subtracting terminal flow is prefix\noutflow minus prefix inflow, namely (N^T e)_i-nu_i; the terminal outgoing\nrow contributes -2alpha_i. There is no unexamined reversed flow convention.\nAfter this cancellation the prefix interpolation sum is\n\n 2sum_i alpha_i*zeta_i - sum_ij N_ij <g_i,y_j>.\n\nDefine\n\n r=h-Ltri^T*alpha,\n r_i=h_i*(pi+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j)-alpha_i,\n V=e^T r=S-alpha^T t.                                     (2.2)\n\nThese identities follow by expanding column i of Ltri^T alpha and using\nsumalpha=1-pi. Consequently\n\n x_n-sum_i alpha_i*y_i = pi*x0-r^T g.\n\nExpanding the outgoing terminal rows now gives the COMPLETE identity\n\n M=q||x0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\n  =||g_n-pi*x0+r^T g||^2\n   +(q-pi^2)||x0||^2\n   +2<x0,(pi*r-mu/2)^T g>\n   +g^T [sym(N Ltri)-r r^T] g.                            (2.3)\n\nHere sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. No gradient equality, rank condition, or actual-history\nrestriction was used in obtaining (2.3). Since g_n is a free formal vector,\nM is PSD exactly when the entire prefix block in (2.3) is PSD.\n\nFor completeness this gives a full coefficient recovery interface. Suppose a\nproposed residual is\n\n (q-pi^2)||x0||^2+2<x0,z^T g>+g^T K g,\n\nwhere K is symmetric. Define\n\n P=K+r r^T, mu=2pi*r-2z, w=Ltri^(-1)e.\n\nFor a chosen nu>=0, all coefficient matches hold exactly when a skew matrix\nA satisfies\n\n A^T=-A,\n A e=E:=P e-Ltri^T(2alpha+nu),\n A w=F:=mu-P w,\n N=(P+A)Ltri^(-1).                                       (2.4)\n\nOne must also impose mu>=0 and N_ij<=0 for EVERY i!=j. These are genuine\nadditional sign conditions, not consequences of PSD or of the moments.\nThe complete original array is then\n\n lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij (distinct queries),\n lambda_(i,star)=mu_i, lambda_(star,i)=nu_i,\n lambda_(n,star)=2pi, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n all incoming-terminal multipliers zero.                  (2.5)\n\nThere is an explicit entry formula, so (2.4) hides no coordinate deletion:\n\n N_ij=P_ij+A_ij\n       -h_j sum_(v>j)(P_iv+A_iv) product_(j<u<v)(1-h_u).   (2.6)\n\nThe inverse used here has diagonal1 and, for v>j, entry\n-h_j product_(j<u<v)(1-h_u). Multiplication by Ltri proves it by the finite\nproduct telescoping identity; entries above its diagonal vanish.\n\nBecause w0=1 and w1=1-h0!=1, e,w are independent. The necessary and sufficient\nlinear compatibility conditions for the two skew moments in (2.4) are\n\n e^T E=0,  w^T F=0,  e^T F=-w^T E.                        (2.7)\n\nAn explicit sufficiency construction is useful. Put\n\n wbar=e^T w/n, cvec=w-wbar*e,\n fvec=F-wbar*E+(E^T w/n)e.\n Acan=(E e^T-e E^T)/n\n          +(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/(cvec^T cvec).        (2.8)\n\nThe conditions imply e^T fvec=cvec^T fvec=0. Multiplying (2.8) by e and w\ntherefore gives exactly E and F. Every compatible skew is Acan+C with\nC^T=-C, Ce=Cw=0. Formula (2.6) specifies all coefficients even for such a\ncompletion. Nonnegativity of the recovered array remains a separate problem.\n\n## 3. Constant-gradient screen and optimum incoming rows\n\nRestrict only the queried formal gradients to a common vector G, keeping x0\nand g_n independent. From (2.1),\n\n sum_i mu_i=2(1-pi)+sum_i nu_i,\n e^T N Ltri e=2alpha^T t+nu^T t.\n\nThe residual after the terminal square has the exact two-vector Gram\n\n [ q-pi^2,  pi(V+1)-1-(sum nu)/2;\n   pi(V+1)-1-(sum nu)/2, D-(V+1)^2+nu^T t ].              (3.1)\n\nThus the suggested Gram is correct with nu=0. Under nonnegative multipliers,\nits absence of the sum-nu term forces EVERY nu_i=0. In the general cone it is\nincorrect to discard those optimum rows without this assumption or an argument.\n\nFor nu=0 write Y=V+1. The exact PSD region of this 2-by-2 restriction is\n\n q>=1/D,  0<=pi<=sqrt(q),\n (qY-pi)^2 <= (q-pi^2)(Dq-1),                            (3.2)\n\nintersected with pi<=1 and the actual alpha moment constraints. To verify this,\nthe two diagonal conditions are q-pi^2>=0 and D-Y^2>=0. The determinant is\nqD-1+2pi Y-qY^2-Dpi^2. Its product by q equals\n(q-pi^2)(Dq-1)-(qY-pi)^2. Necessity of q>=1/D also follows by evaluating this\nGram on (D,1): the value is D(qD-1)-(Dpi-Y)^2. Conversely, for q>=1/D and\nq-pi^2>0, the determinant condition forces the lower diagonal to be\nnonnegative. If q-pi^2=0, (3.2) gives Y=1/pi and the lower diagonal is\nD-1/q>=0. This proves the boundary cases as well as the interior.\n\nFor the fixed hierarchy below t0=1 and t_l=T=S-a. As alpha ranges over\nnonnegative columns of mass1-pi, its moment range gives\n\n b+pi*T <= Y <= S+pi,  b=1+a.                            (3.3)\n\nThis is a necessary affine screen only. It is not a full-space certificate.\n\nAt q0=1/D the vanishing of nu is actually forced, rather than optional.\nUse the legal scalar Huber function f(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=1 and\nf(x)=|x|-1/2 otherwise, starting at x0=D. All queried points and the terminal\nlie in the positive affine branch; all their gradients equal1. Every finite\nordered row and every query/terminal-to-optimum row is zero. The remaining\nrows are Q_(star,i)=-(D-t_i)<0. The objective gap q0*x0^2-2f_n is zero.\nConsequently M=-sum_i nu_i(D-t_i). PSD and nu>=0 force every nu_i=0.\nAlso the terminal square vanishes on this tight trajectory, so\n\n Y=D*pi, pi^2<=1/D.                                      (3.4)\n\nThe same conclusion follows directly from (3.2). Equations (3.3),(3.4) imply\npi>=b/(S+b), and also pi<=1/2. Thus concentrating alpha at the latest query\nselects the smallest possible pi in this q0 affine screen.\n\nFor q>q0, no general vanishing claim for nu is made. The same Huber trajectory\nonly implies the necessary budget\n\n sum_i nu_i(D-t_i) <= D^2(q-q0).\n\n## 4. Frozen tail source and the genuine unsaturated freedom\n\nFix the specified enclosing m>=1536, epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\nc=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, and\n\n H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), h=H_m,\n n=2^m-1, l=n-1, S=sum h, D=1+2S,\n b=1+a, T=S-a, Ctot=S+b, Ctot+T=D.\n\nEvery child uses this SAME enclosing m. Put\n\n alpha=(1-pi)e_l, pi=b/Ctot, q0=1/D,\n delta=q0-pi^2=S(S-2ab)/(D*Ctot^2)>0.                     (4.1)\n\nThen r_i=pi*h_i for i<l, r_l=a+pi, and V=a+pi*T=D*pi-1.\nAll coefficients in (4.1) are fixed; there is no alternative alpha search.\n\nIt is useful to state the COMPLETE q0 flow constraints, beyond the affine\nscreen. Write the residual prefix matrix as [delta,z^T;z,K]. Its constant\nrestriction vanishes at (x0,g)=(D,e). PSD of the full residual then forces\nz^T e=-delta D and K e=-D z: a zero quadratic value for a PSD form forces its\nbilinear product with every direction to vanish, as follows by varying a real\nscalar in that direction. Equivalently every possible PSD residual at q0 has\n\n z=-delta*u, e^T u=D,\n K=delta*u*u^T+K0, K0>=0, K0 e=0.                        (4.2)\n\nThe Schur complement proves K0>=0; the displayed moments prove K0 e=0.\nConversely (4.2) makes the residual PSD with exactly that affine null direction.\nIts remaining complete flow interface is\n\n mu=2pi*r+2delta*u>=0, nu=0,\n P=r r^T+delta*u*u^T+K0,\n A e=P e-2Ltri^T alpha,\n A w=mu-P w,\n N=(P+A)Ltri^(-1), N_ij<=0 for all i!=j.                 (4.3)\n\nCompatibility (2.7), and every entry formula (2.6), remain required.\nThere is no imposed support restriction on finite multipliers in (4.3).\n\nUnder saturation q=pi^2, the residual x0 diagonal is zero. Full PSD then\nforces its entire x0 mixed column to vanish, fixing mu=2pi*r. In contrast,\n(4.2),(4.3) permit the additional vector 2delta*u in mu, with e^T u=D,\nand a full PSD metric K0 on the e-orthogonal gradient directions. This is\nreal coefficient freedom. It neither follows from nor is excluded by the\nsaturated common-column report ba770750b559361d, read in full for this round.\nThat report's original Q orientation agrees with (2.1)-(2.3); its special\nmetric and common-column constraints have not been imported as necessities.\n\n## 5. ONE explicit paid rank-two family\n\nDefine the physical column v by v_i=h_i for i<l and v_l=1, so e^T v=T. Freeze\n\n u=(D/T)v, r=pi*v+a*e_l,\n s=beta^(2^m-1)*c^(-m), gamma=a^2/T^2-s^2,\n q=17/(16D), kappa=q-q0=1/(16D),\n dsrc=(e0-e1)/b.\n\nThe last three physical steps are (b,gbar,b), where gbar=1+beta. On their\nORIGINAL indices define\n\n dtail=a*gbar*e_(l-2)+(1-a*beta)e_(l-1)-b*e_l.             (5.1)\n\nBoth columns have zero sum. The only family in this mechanism is (1.1), for\nall theta real and xi>=0. In the requested paid-block coordinates this means\n\n ell=kappa*theta*dsrc,\n R=kappa*theta^2*dsrc*dsrc^T+xi*dtail*dtail^T.              (5.2)\n\nThus sumell=0, Re=0, and\n\n [kappa,ell^T;ell,R]\n =kappa*(1,theta*dsrc)^T*(1,theta*dsrc)\n                   +xi*(0,dtail)^T*(0,dtail)\n\nis literally PSD of rank at most two. No feasible but unspecified matrix R is\nused. In particular theta=-2 uses a nonzero mixed block. The tail column is\nincluded from the outset to allow last-query diagonal repair without changing\nthe source coordinates; it is not a second post-failure family.\n\nAll baseline signs can be proved at the stipulated threshold. The inequalities\nsqrt(2)<10/7 and epsilon<=1/6144 give\n\n a>(7/17)(6143/6145)>2/5, while a<1/2; hence 1<b<3/2.\n\nEvery h_i>1, so S>2^m-1>64. Formula (4.1) proves delta>0.\nTo prove gamma>0, set vword=a/(c-2). Since c>1+sqrt(2) and a<1/c,\n0<vword<1. Summing the two children, or substituting in their defining\nrecursion, gives\n\n 1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j.\n\nSince c>2 and j>0 this is less c^j, hence T<S<c^m.\nAlso n>=8m for m>=6: it holds at6 and the inequality is preserved when m\nincreases by1. Put x=1/(4m). Since (1-x)^(-1)>1+x and the binomial expansion\ngives (1+x)^(4m)>2, one has beta^(4m)<1/2 and beta^n<1/4. Therefore\ns*T<beta^n<1/4<a, proving gamma>0. No recurrence or numerical calculation was\nexecuted; these are symbolic finite inequalities.\n\nConsequently (1.1) is a PSD polynomial for every theta and xi>=0. Whether\nit equals a nonnegative combination of the required original rows is the\nseparate issue answered negatively below.\n\n## 6. Full constant and unit-quadratic polynomial checks\n\nFor a common queried gradient G, e^T u=D and both difference columns vanish.\nThe complete trial polynomial restricts to\n\n ||g_n-pi*x0+V*G||^2+delta||x0-D*G||^2+kappa||x0||^2.     (6.1)\n\nThis is exactly the nu=0 affine Gram (3.1) at q=q0+kappa, with the specified\npi and V. Its terminal square still contains the original free g_n.\nOn the actual Huber trajectory x0=D*G,g_n=G, (6.1) is kappa D^2||G||^2,\nwhich matches the original objective polynomial. No child is restarted.\n\nThe optimum incoming restriction is also forced within THIS paid family,\neven though q>q0. Coefficient matching gives\n\n z=-delta*u+kappa*theta*dsrc,\n mu=2pi*r+2delta*u-2kappa*theta*dsrc.                      (6.2)\n\nIts sum is 2pi*(Dpi-1)+2delta D=2(1-pi). The sum of (2.1) therefore gives\nsum nu=0; nonnegativity forces nu=0. This is a family-specific consequence,\nnot a deletion from the unrestricted loose-target cone.\n\nFor the full unit-curvature quadratic f(x)=||x||^2/2, let\nw_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j). Then Ltri*w=e and g_i=w_i*x0. The hierarchy has\nsigned full product w_n=-s. To see its magnitude, note that the level at i\nis the exponent of2 dividing i+1. Its total across i=0,...,2^m-2 is\nsum_(j=1)^(m-1)(2^(m-j)-1)=2^m-m-1=n-m. Multiplying the factors\na*c^level=beta*c^(level-1) gives beta^n*c^(-m)=s.\n\nAt the original endpoints,\n\n w0=1, w1=-a,\n w_l=s/a, w_(l-1)=-s/(a*beta), w_(l-2)=s/(a^2*beta).\n\nThese follow from the literal last factors -a,-beta,-a. They prove\n\n dsrc^T w=1, dtail^T w=0,\n u^T w=D/T, r^T w=pi+s.                                 (6.3)\n\nFor u use sum_(i<l)h_i*w_i=1-w_l; for r use r=pi*v+a*e_l.\nThe terminal square in (1.1) consequently vanishes on the ENTIRE actual\nunit-quadratic trajectory. Every original Q_ij, including optimum and terminal\nrows, is zero on that trajectory. A certificate identity must therefore have\nvalue (q-s^2)||x0||^2.\n\nThe baseline has exactly the correct q0 value, because\n\n delta*(1-D/T)^2+gamma=q0-s^2.                            (6.4)\n\nIndeed 1-D/T=-Ctot/T and\nCtot^2-D*b^2+D*a^2=T^2; multiplying (6.4) by D*T^2 proves it.\nThe paid tail term vanishes, and the paid source square contributes\nkappa*(1+theta)^2||x0||^2. Thus full polynomial matching forces\n\n theta*(theta+2)=0, namely theta=0 or theta=-2.            (6.5)\n\nThis is a necessary identity of the original polynomial, not a test of only\nan aggregate moment. It does not decide any individual multiplier sign.\n\n## 7. Complete linear flow constraints for the literal family\n\nFor (1.1), write\n\n Gamma=gamma+kappa*theta^2,\n P=r r^T+delta*u*u^T+Gamma*dsrc*dsrc^T+xi*dtail*dtail^T,\n mu=2pi*r+2delta*u-2kappa*theta*dsrc, nu=0.                (7.1)\n\nThe actual coefficient equations are exactly (2.4)-(2.7) with these P,mu and\nthe specified alpha. These formulas, followed by (2.6),(2.5), recover every\noriginal coefficient for any compatible skew; there are no omitted rows.\n\nOne can check ALL scalar compatibility conditions, rather than assume them.\nFirst\n\n e^T E=V^2+delta D^2-2(1-pi)T=0,\n\nbecause V=Dpi-1, delta=1/D-pi^2 and (1-pi)T=S-V. Next (6.3),(6.4) give\n\n w^T F=pi^2-s^2+delta*(2D/T-D^2/T^2)-gamma\n                                  -kappa*(theta^2+2theta)\n       =-kappa*theta*(theta+2).\n\nFinally P is symmetric and Ltri*w=e, so\n\n e^T F+w^T E=e^T mu-2alpha^T Ltri*w=0.\n\nThus for BOTH allowed values theta=0,-2, every compatibility equation holds\nfor every xi>=0. Formula (2.8) provides an actual real skew completion;\nadding an arbitrary neutral skew does not alter the cuts below. This proves\nthat the scoped failure is specifically nonnegative original multipliers,\nnot a silently inconsistent choice of skew moments.\n\nFor explicit optimum-row signs set\n\n k=(S-ab)/(Ctot*T)=(1-pi*b)/T>0.\n\nSince r=pi*v+a*e_l and u=(D/T)v, (7.1) simplifies to\n\n mu=2k*v+2pi*a*e_l-2kappa*theta*dsrc.                    (7.2)\n\nThe equality uses pi^2*T/D+delta=(1-pi*b)/D. At theta=0 all mu entries are\npositive. At theta=-2 only source0 and source1 change:\n\n mu0=2kb+4kappa/b, mu1=2k*gbar-4kappa/b,\n mu_i=2k*h_i for 1<i<l, mu_l=2k+2pi*a.\n\nOne has k>1/(2S): Ctot*T<S(S+3/2), S-ab>S-3/4, and S>3 suffice. Hence\nmu1>1/S-1/(8S)>0, because 4kappa/b=1/(4Db)<1/(8S).\nAll optimum outgoing signs pass at both unit-compatible theta values.\nThe terminal outgoing signs also pass: 0<pi<1 and alpha=(1-pi)e_l.\nThe forced optimum incoming coefficients are zero, and all incoming terminal\ncoefficients remain zero as selected at the start.\n\n## 8. Original tail tests, including the repair actually afforded by xi\n\nThe last column of Ltri is e_l. Consequently the exact coefficient identity\nP=sym(N Ltri) gives\n\n Pll=Nll.\n\nThe original last column and row capacities therefore require\n\n Pll-2(1-pi)=sum_(i<l)lambda_(i,l)>=0,\n Pll-mu_l=sum_(j<l)lambda_(l,j)>=0.                       (8.1)\n\nThe source paid square has no support at the last three indices, so\n\n Pll=Pll0+xi*b^2,\n Pll0=(a+pi)^2+delta*D^2/T^2.                            (8.2)\n\nDefine J=pi^2+delta*D^2/T^2. There is the exact identity\n\n J=2k-a^2/T^2.                                          (8.3)\n\nFor verification, put everything over Ctot^2*T^2. The numerator of J-2k is\nb^2*T^2+D*S(S-2ab)-2Ctot*T(S-ab)=-a^2*Ctot^2.\nUsing (8.3) in (8.2) gives\n\n Pll0-mu_l=a^2*(1-T^(-2))>0.\n\nThus the last row capacity passes already, whereas the baseline last COLUMN\ncapacity fails. In detail, pi<b/S<3/(2S)<1/32 and delta<1/D give\n\n Pll0<(17/32)^2+D/T^2<1/2.\n\nFor the last inequality, D/T^2<3/S: after replacing a by1/2 it follows from\nS^2-4S+3/4>0, valid at S>64. Also 3/S<3/64, and\n(17/32)^2+3/64=337/1024<1/2. On the other hand 2(1-pi)>31/16.\nTherefore the exact positive tail price is\n\n xi >= [2(1-pi)-Pll0]/b^2.                               (8.4)\n\nThe family explicitly permits such xi; this elementary tail mismatch has\nbeen repaired within the chosen family, not ignored or confused with an\nobstruction to every unsaturated certificate.\n\nThe next original tail pair also has a complete orientation check. Put\np=l-1. Since h_p=gbar,\n\n N_(p,l)+N_(l,p)=2P_(p,l)-gbar*Pll\n   =gbar*[2k-a^2*(1+T^(-2))]\n                  -xi*b*[2(1-a*beta)+gbar*b].            (8.5)\n\nThe left side must be nonpositive. Its right side is strictly negative for\nall xi>=0: k<1/T<2/S, so 2k<4/S<1/16<a^2, and 1-a*beta>0. Hence this\nlast-pair necessary condition passes. It does not assert the individual signs.\nThese tests retain both orientations of each original row and the actual\nlast-three products; no terminal node has been substituted for a query.\n\n## 9. Decisive original source cut for the ENTIRE family\n\nThe first physical step is h0=b=1+a. Column0 of Ltri has entry1 in row0 and\nentry b in every later row. Since P=sym(N Ltri), its first diagonal obeys\n\n P00=N00+b*sum_(j>0)N0j\n     =mu0+a*sum_(j>0)N0j.\n\nThus any nonnegative original multiplier array must satisfy the nonnegative\ncut\n\n mu0-P00=a*sum_(j=1)^l lambda_(0,j)>=0.                   (9.1)\n\nEvery original queried destination is retained. There is no assumption about\nsupport or choice of the completing skew, and mu0 is the ACTUAL outgoing\noptimum multiplier fixed by the x0 mixed coefficients.\n\nIn (7.1), dtail0=0, r0=pi*b, u0=Db/T, and dsrc0=1/b. Therefore\n\n mu0=2kb-2kappa*theta/b,\n P00=b^2*J+(gamma+kappa*theta^2)/b^2.\n\nUsing (8.3) and completing the theta square gives exactly\n\n mu0-P00\n =-2ab*k+a^2*b^2/T^2-gamma/b^2\n                   -kappa*(theta^2+2btheta)/b^2\n =-2ab*k+a^2*b^2/T^2-gamma/b^2+kappa\n                   -(kappa/b^2)(theta+b)^2.              (9.2)\n\nIt is independent of xi. It is also independent of ALL remaining neutral\nskew freedom. The paid x0 block can improve this source deficit by at most\nkappa, regardless of theta.\n\nHere is an explicit bound uniform at the stipulated enclosing threshold.\nWe proved k>1/(2S), b>1, gamma>0, S>64, 2/5<a<1/2. Also T>S/2. Hence\n\n -2ab*k < -a/S,\n a^2*b^2/T^2 < a*9/(2S^2) < a/(8S),\n kappa=1/(16D)<1/(32S)<a/(8S).\n\nThe second comparison uses S>36; the third uses a>1/4. Dropping only the two\nnonpositive square terms in (9.2) gives\n\n mu0-P00 < -3a/(4S) <0.                                 (9.3)\n\nEquations (9.1),(9.3) contradict one another for EVERY theta real and EVERY\nxi>=0. In particular both theta values that pass the whole unit-quadratic\nidentity are excluded, even after paying (8.4) and even though all their\nlinear compatibility equations and optimum signs pass.\n\nThis is an exact nonnegative cut against the complete explicitly chosen\nrank-two paid family. No enumeration, optimizer, numerical witness, or\nparticular-skew failure was used. There is no need to guess and test additional\narrays after this cut. No third family has been opened.\n\n## 10. What remains and the status of the original problem\n\nThe reusable candidate interfaces are (2.1)-(2.8), the nu-corrected affine\nGram (3.1), its exact nu=0 region (3.2), and the q0 unsaturated form (4.2)-(4.3).\nThey are part of this JOINT UNVERIFIED draft, not accepted building blocks.\nThey can guide a future authorized verification or a new architecture.\n\nThe failure identifies a precise local deficiency. For this u, the baseline\nsource coefficient P00 exceeds the available mu0 by order1/S. The loose\npayment kappa is too small to fix it with the frozen dsrc column. Arbitrarily\nlarge PSD energy on the chosen last-three column does not change P00 or mu0.\nA future different mixed column or different u could change this cut. This\nreport does not assert that either repair succeeds, and does not attempt one.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used at its exact original-row/terminal-reduction\nscope. Result and scope\n\nBoth specified new attachment families fail as sufficient conic/PSD identities\nfor the FULL H4 endpoint at d=1. Neither failure is an actual-history\ncounterexample to that endpoint inequality.\n\nMechanism1 transfers actual initial-mixture mass from g3,g11 to rL,rR.\nIts changed initial-x terms are canceled by the explicitly included ORIGINAL\nauxiliary normal rows. It is a negative value of the complete\nquadratic, not a negative coefficient considered in isolation.\n\nThe original primitive-row array C_rows is fixed in both families; no claim\nabout arbitrary redistributions of that array is made. The final original\nrow weights are all specified and nonnegative. An entrywise formula specifies\nevery coefficient of the complete formal matrix, including the zero x row\nafter exact cancellation and the unequal E,J auxiliary denominators.\n\nBoth mandatory actual histories are retained in Sections9-10 with their\nactual right H3, all physical reserves, and all radial/normal terms. Their\nfull endpoint gaps remain strictly positive. No child is reset.\n\nAccepted fact1e0bf3f41457d8e4 proves the actual H3 inequality for every\ncompact convex body containing0 and every d>=1, and its finite-word\nrepresentation and full-class seven-step conclusions have precisely their\nstated scope. We use its H2 identities and actual H3 law. We neither reprove\nthe representation theorem nor claim that the accepted H3 law implies H4.\n\nH4 at d=1 remains OPEN. H4 for all d>=1 remains OPEN. All-depth propagation,\nany uniform (1+C0/m)/D upper bound, and the original minimax target remain\nOPEN. No all-depth mechanism or third attachment family is opened.\n\n## 2. Fixed word, all samples, and accepted inputs\n\nFix ONE integer m>=1536 and keep it in every child. Put\n\n epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,\n H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), S_j=sum H_j.\n\nLocal abbreviations, identical to the source, are\n\n A=1+a, B=1+beta, t=S2=2A+B, C=1+t,\n k=beta*c=d_2, K=beta*c^2=d_3, ell=C+k,\n s=S3=2C+k-1=t+ell, C3=1+s=2C+k,\n b=1+K, h=s+b=C3+K, S=S4=2s+b.\n\nHere S is the LOCAL H4 mass; the enclosing full word still has mass S_m\nand n=2^m-1. No replacement of either mass is intended. The actual H4 is\n\n (A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A,1+K,A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A).        (2.1)\n\nFor one nonempty compact convex body D0 containing0 in a positive finite\nEuclidean dimension, let P be projection onto D0 and\n\n F(X)=max_(g in D0)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2),\n U_d(X)=max_(g in D0)(2<X,g>-d||g||^2), d>0.\n\nThe actual samples obey x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i, g_i=P(x_i), 0<=i<15;\ng15=P(x15) is the terminal gradient and supplies no physical step. Put\n\n x=x0, u=x3, z=x4, v=x7, w=x8, uR=x11, zR=x12, y=x15.\n\nThus z=u-(1+k)g3, w=v-b*g7, zR=uR-(1+k)g11. At d=1 use\n\n J=1+2t, D=1+2s, M=1+2S=D+2h,\n eta=M-2s=D+2b, E=eta+2t,\n M=E+2ell, D=J+2ell, E-J=2h.                 (2.2)\n\nThe ACTUAL auxiliary maximizers on this SAME body are\n\n p0=P(x/M), rL=P(z/E), rR=P(zR/J),\n q=P(y)=g15, qL=P(v/eta), rO=P(w/D).\n\nThe sample inputs belonging to labels p0,rL,rR,qL,rO are respectively\nx/M,z/E,zR/J,v/eta,w/D. They are not extra GD steps.\n\nFor ANY two physical or auxiliary labels use\n\n Q_(i,j)=<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j><=0,                (2.3)\n\nwhere X_j is that label's sample input and G_j its projection. This is\nthe exact projection specialization of smooth-convex interpolation in\naccepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4, PartB(2.3), derived there from its predecessor\n1252b8823bb5a290. In particular Q_(i,p0)=<x/M-p0,G_i-p0>.\n\nFor a physical word of mass T from X to Y its primitive energy is\n\n V=||X||^2-||Y+T*P(Y)||^2.\n\nAccepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4 PartB(2.4),(3.3)-(3.6),(4.2)-(4.5)\nsupplies the exact primitive, H2, join, and maximization identities used\nbelow. The off-root identities are algebraic and do not assume primitive\npositivity at H3 or H4. PartB(6.3) supplies the full actual H3 U law.\n\n## 3. The complete base identity and every base row\n\nWe restate the full source(4.8), including its row and square coefficients,\nand check it from the accepted identities. Set C1=1+A and\n\n kap1=(3C1+beta)/(C1-beta),\n R1=(C1+beta)[C1^2-beta(2C1+beta)]/(C1-beta),\n deltaA=2-A^2,\n e0=deltaA, e1=R1(2/B-1),\n e2=kap1*deltaA+R1(2/A-1).\n\nAll three e_i are positive by accepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4 PartB(3.4)-(3.7).\nOn each H2 block of four gradients the complete base multiplier list is\n\n Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=2(1+A), Lambda_12=2R1/B,\n Lambda_21=(kap1-1)A+2R1/B,\n Lambda_13=(kap1-1)beta, Lambda_31=kap1-1,\n Lambda_23=2kap1+(kap1-1)A+2R1/A,\n Lambda_32=2kap1(1+A)+2R1/A.                 (3.1)\n\nAll other H2 entries are zero. For offsets o=0,4,8,12 define\n\n C_o=-sum_(i!=j in {0,1,2,3})Lambda_ij Q_(o+i,o+j),\n P_o=sum_(j=0)^2 e_j||g_(o+j)-g_(o+j+1)||^2,\n V_o=C_o+P_o.\n\nUse C00=C_0,C01=C_4,C10=C_8,C11=C_12, and analogous P,V labels.\nPut\n\n kap=(3C+k)/(C-k), kapO=(3C3+K)/(C3-K),\n d_i=(C+k)[k(2C+k)-C^2]/(C-k),\n d_o=(C3+K)[K(2C3+K)-C3^2]/(C3-K),\n Bterm=kapO*d_i+d_o.                         (3.2)\n\nHere d_i,d_o are the source's defect coefficients, not sequence elements\nd_j. They are strictly positive. To check the signs set u0=sqrt(2)-1:\n\n k-u0*C=epsilon[u0+4u0^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda*epsilon]>0.\n\nIndeed u0>2/5,lambda<5/2,epsilon<1/8 give a positive bracket.\nAlso K/C3=c*(k/C)/(2+k/C)>u0, since c>lambda and the map\nr -> c*r/(2+r) is strictly increasing with lambda*u0/(2+u0)=u0.\nThe bounds C>11/2,k<5/2,C3>13,K<25/4 ensure both denominators in(3.2)\nare positive. These bounds follow directly from epsilon<=1/6144,\nbeta>9/10,c<5/2,a>1/3,a<u0<1/2. In particular Bterm>0.\n\nThe three joins are\n\n E_i=sum_(j=4)^6 h_j Q_(j,3)+Q_(7,3)+k Q_(3,7)\n                                      +sum_(j=4)^6 h_j Q_(j,7),\n E_r=sum_(j=12)^14 h_j Q_(j,11)+Q_(15,11)+k Q_(11,15)\n                                      +sum_(j=12)^14 h_j Q_(j,15),\n E_o=sum_(j=8)^14 h_j Q_(j,7)+Q_(15,7)+K Q_(7,15)\n                                      +sum_(j=8)^14 h_j Q_(j,15).\n\nDefine the COMPLETE nonnegative row expression and all physical squares\n\n C_rows=C00+kap*C01+kapO*C10+kapO*kap*C11\n       -(kap-1)E_i-kapO*(kap-1)E_r-(kapO-1)E_o,\n P_sq=P00+kap*P01+kapO*P10+kapO*kap*P11.       (3.3)\n\nFor absolute specificity this is also an entry rule for the base array.\nStart with zero on every ordered pair. For each offset o add rho_o*Lambda_ij\nto the coefficient of -Q_(o+i,o+j), where\nrho_0=1,rho_4=kap,rho_8=kapO,rho_12=kapO*kap. For the three tuples\n\n (p,n,I,e,lambdaJoin)=\n (3,7,{4,5,6},k,kap-1),\n (11,15,{12,13,14},k,kapO*(kap-1)),\n (7,15,{8,...,14},K,kapO-1),\n\nadd lambdaJoin*h_j on (j,p) and(j,n) for each j in I, lambdaJoin on(n,p),\nand lambdaJoin*e on(p,n). Add coincident entries. This completely defines\nevery base ordered-row coefficient, including all overlaps, with no negative\nentries and no implicit feasibility variables.\n\nApplying accepted PartB(3.3) to the two inner joins and then the outer join,\nwith actual capacities C,C and then C3,C3, gives exactly\n\n V4=C_rows+P_sq+Fdef,\n Fdef=-d_i||g3||^2+(d_i-d_o)||g7||^2\n                -kapO*d_i||g11||^2+Bterm||q||^2.            (3.4)\n\nThe four diagonal defect coefficients sum to0. This application keeps the\nsigned off-root term from the accepted identity; it does not invoke an\nexact-root composition law on a perturbed bridge.\n\nLet\n\n Hvec=sum_(i=0)^14 h_i*g_i=x-y,\n Vmix=Hvec+(1+S)q,\n Wstar=M*p0-Vmix,\n Nstar=2<x-M*p0,Wstar>,\n Qbase=||Wstar||^2+P_sq+Fdef.                 (3.5)\n\nThe canonical mixture Vmix/M is convex since its nonnegative weights sum\nto M. The exact maximization identity accepted PartB(4.2),(4.5), applied\nto the WHOLE H4 at d=1, is\n\n M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]=V4+||Wstar||^2+Nstar\n               =C_rows+Nstar+Qbase.          (3.6)\n\nThis is precisely source(4.8). Its derivation accounts for the complete\nmatched source identity: the left and outer auxiliary terms, the signed\nradial differences and the entire actual right H3 combine to this full\nword identity. No term is assumed nonnegative merely because it disappeared\nby exact algebraic cancellation. In particular (3.6) is not an inequality\nobtained by throwing away the source Xi or the right child.\n\n## 4. Exact sibling and local normals\n\nWrite TL=x-z=sum_(i<4)h_i*g_i and TR=x-zR=sum_(i<12)h_i*g_i. Also put\n\n L01=A*g4+B*g5+A*g6, L10=A*g8+B*g9+A*g10,\n Udiff=u-uR=(1+k)g3+L01+b*g7+L10,\n Zdiff=z-zR=L01+b*g7+L10+(1+k)g11.            (4.1)\n\nThe actual nonnegative combinations, with their ordered orientations, are\n\n Ng=-Q_(11,3)-Q_(3,11)\n   =<Udiff,g3-g11>-||g3-g11||^2,\n\n Na=-E Q_(rR,rL)-J Q_(rL,rR)\n   =<Zdiff,rL-rR>-E||rL||^2-J||rR||^2\n                                      +(E+J)<rL,rR>,\n\n Nc=-Q_(rR,11)-J Q_(11,rR)\n   =k||g11||^2+(J-k)<g11,rR>-J||rR||^2.       (4.2)\n\nThe weights E and J in Na are different: Na is the mass-balanced sum\n<z-E*rL,rL-rR>+<zR-J*rR,rR-rL>. Its x terms cancel. The unweighted\nsum instead contains(1/E-1/J)<x,rL-rR>; it is not used here.\n\nSet the literal sibling coefficients\n\n                   theta=d_i/k, omega=d_i,                (4.3)\n\nand allow tau>=0 multiplying Nc. All three are nonnegative and theta,omega\nare strictly positive. The obstructions below hold independently of tau;\nin fact they also hold if theta,omega are any nonnegative numbers.\n\n## 5. Mechanism1: changed physical-to-auxiliary initial attachments\n\nThis family was fixed in plan1bbce25036dd1237 before its coefficient\nderivation. Choose 0<=wL,wR<=1+k and put\n\n DeltaA=wL(rL-g3)+wR(rR-g11),\n VmixA=Vmix+DeltaA, WA=Wstar-DeltaA,\n NA=2<x-M*p0,WA>.\n\nThe numerator VmixA gives weights h3-wL,h11-wR at g3,g11, the unchanged\nother physical weights, weights wL,wR at rL,rR, and1+S at q. They are\nnonnegative and sum to M, so VmixA/M is a feasible convex mixture and\nNA is a nonnegative sum of original initial-maximizer normal rows.\n\nDefine the two additional ACTUAL normal quantities\n\n AL=<z-E*rL,rL-g3>=-E Q_(3,rL)>=0,\n AR=<zR-J*rR,rR-g11>=-J Q_(11,rR)>=0.          (5.1)\n\nThe changed initial normal satisfies Nstar=NA+2<x-M*p0,DeltaA>.\nExtract2wL*AL+2wR*AR from this last term. Using z=x-TL,zR=x-TR,\nthe entire x coefficient cancels, leaving the EXACT quadratic\n\n Q1=Qbase\n    +2wL<TL+E*rL-M*p0,rL-g3>\n    +2wR<TR+J*rR-M*p0,rR-g11>\n    -omega*Ng-theta*Na-tau*Nc.               (5.2)\n\nThus the full endpoint equality is\n\n M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]\n   =C_rows+NA+2wL*AL+2wR*AR\n                      +omega*Ng+theta*Na+tau*Nc+Q1.         (5.3)\n\nEvery term outside Q1 is nonnegative on an actual history. A PSD Q1 on\nall independent formal gradients would therefore be sufficient for H4.\nThere is no assumption that the changed square is still ||Wstar||^2:\nequivalently, writing aL=TL+E*rL-Vmix and aR=TR+J*rR-Vmix gives\n\n Q1=||WA||^2+P_sq+Fdef-||DeltaA||^2\n              +2wL<aL,rL-g3>+2wR<aR,rR-g11>\n              -omega*Ng-theta*Na-tau*Nc.                   (5.4)\n\nThe -||DeltaA||^2 is part of the full polynomial. Equations(5.2),(5.4)\nagree by expanding WA=Wstar-DeltaA; neither conceals an auxiliary step.\n\nTwo diagnostic necessary conditions explicitly replace the old frozen\ncalculation. On the mandatory formal assignment g0=...=g15=p0=G!=0,\nrL=rR=0, we have TL=ell*G,TR=(h+ell)*G and\n\n Q1=[2wL(M-ell)+2wR(M-h-ell)-tau*k]||G||^2.    (5.5)\n\nA candidate that pays this required direction must satisfy\n\n             2wL(M-ell)+2wR(M-h-ell)>=tau*k.               (5.6)\n\nThe new terms in(5.5) are retained; the old value -tau*k cannot be reused\nas the new obstruction. This constraint allows positive tau: for example\nwL=1+k,wR=0,tau=(1+k)(M-ell)/k makes(5.5) strictly positive.\n\nWith p0 and every physical gradient set to0, the complete auxiliary block is\n\n E(2wL+theta)||rL||^2+J(2wR+theta+tau)||rR||^2\n                                  -theta(E+J)<rL,rR>.     (5.7)\n\nIts diagonal entries are positive and its determinant condition is\n\n (2wL+theta)(2wR+theta+tau)\n                         >=theta^2*(E+J)^2/(4EJ).          (5.8)\n\nThis retains the actual denominator mismatch E-J=2h. Neither(5.6) nor\n(5.8) is asserted sufficient; the obstruction below holds even if both\nare imposed as extra restrictions on the family.\n\nFor every parameter choice take the FORMAL scalar-direction assignment\n\n p0=rL=rR=g0=...=g14=G!=0, q=(1+v0)G.\n\nBoth attachment differences in(5.2) vanish. Ng,Na,Nc also vanish. The\nonly physical square that changes is the last H2 adjacent square, and\nWstar=-(1+S)v0*G. Consequently the WHOLE Q1 is\n\n Q1=[2Bterm*v0+Kterm*v0^2]||G||^2,\n Kterm=(1+S)^2+kapO*kap*e2+Bterm>0.           (5.9)\n\nTaking v0=-Bterm/Kterm gives\n\n                    Q1=-Bterm^2||G||^2/Kterm<0.            (5.10)\n\nThis excludes all wL,wR,tau in mechanism1, including any paying the old\ntest and the auxiliary block. The failure specifically calls for a change\nthat can affect the actual terminal-gradient contact. It does not prove\nan actual H4 trajectory violates the endpoint law.\n\n## 6. Mechanism2: all physical-to-terminal initial transfers\n\nAfter the decisive(5.10), plan2331dae962e6ea64 fixed the second and last\nfamily. Retain mechanism1 and choose fifteen nonnegative parameters t_i,\n0<=i<15, subject to\n\n       t_i+wL*1_(i=3)+wR*1_(i=11)<=h_i.                   (6.1)\n\nThe symbols t_i are attachment masses; scalar t=S2 remains unchanged.\nDefine\n\n Tsum=sum_(i<15)t_i, DeltaT=sum_(i<15)t_i(q-g_i),\n Delta=DeltaA+DeltaT, Vmix2=Vmix+Delta,\n W2=Wstar-Delta, N2=2<x-M*p0,W2>.\n\nThe new mixture weights are\n\n mu_i=h_i-t_i-wL*1_(i=3)-wR*1_(i=11), 0<=i<15,\n mu_q=1+S+Tsum, mu_L=wL, mu_R=wR.             (6.2)\n\nThey are nonnegative and sum exactly to M. In particular terminal mass\nhas increased by precisely Tsum, rather than being silently left at1+S.\nFor each physical i<15 include the ORIGINAL terminal normal\n\n                  NT_i=<y-q,q-g_i>=-Q_(i,15)>=0.          (6.3)\n\nSince y=x-Hvec,\n\n 2<x-M*p0,DeltaT>-2sum t_i*NT_i\n                      =2<Hvec+q-M*p0,DeltaT>.             (6.4)\n\nThis displays the entire changed x coefficient and its cancellation.\nThe full second-family equality is\n\n M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]\n  =C_rows+N2+2wL*AL+2wR*AR+2sum t_i*NT_i\n                         +omega*Ng+theta*Na+tau*Nc+Q2,\n\n             Q2=Q1+2<Hvec+q-M*p0,DeltaT>.                 (6.5)\n\nNo maximizer has been replaced by an arbitrary feasible gradient. The\npositive normal rows in(6.5) use the actual p0,rL,rR,q on the one body.\nThe candidate PSD test treats their formal vector coordinates independently,\nas a sufficient identity must do before invoking those row inequalities.\n\nFor comparison with a literal new-mixture square, the whole expression is\n\n Q2=||W2||^2+P_sq+Fdef-||Delta||^2\n    +2wL<TL+E*rL-Vmix,rL-g3>\n    +2wR<TR+J*rR-Vmix,rR-g11>\n    -2S<q,DeltaT>-omega*Ng-theta*Na-tau*Nc.                (6.6)\n\nThis keeps all cross terms of the two transfers through -||Delta||^2.\nIt is algebraically identical to(6.5); no unpriced square is discarded.\n\nHere is the COMPLETE final ordered-row list in a single convention:\nevery entry below is a coefficient of -Q_(i,j). Start with the explicit\nbase array from Section3, then add\n\n (i,p0): 2M*mu_i, 0<=i<15;\n (15,p0): 2M*mu_q;\n (rL,p0): 2M*wL; (rR,p0): 2M*wR;\n (3,rL): 2wL*E;\n (11,rR): J*(2wR+tau); (rR,11): tau;\n (11,3): omega; (3,11): omega;\n (rR,rL): theta*E; (rL,rR): theta*J;\n (i,15): 2t_i, 0<=i<15.                     (6.7)\n\nCoincident base and added pairs are summed; all other entries are zero.\nAll coefficients are nonnegative by(6.1)-(6.2). Formula(6.7) also gives\nthe first family on setting every t_i=0. It specifies the actual factor M\nin the initial rows and the unequal factors E,J in the auxiliary rows.\nNo unspecified row redistribution or hidden signed multiplier is present.\n\n## 7. Every formal quadratic coefficient\n\nThis section gives one entry formula for the entire matrix, rather than\nonly a selected principal restriction. Let the nineteen independent vector\ncoordinates have labels I={p,0,1,...,15,L,R}, where p means p0,L means rL,\nR means rR, and15 means q. Let e^a be the nineteen-dimensional unit column\nof label a; these columns are coefficient bookkeeping, not physical vectors.\nFor columns a,b define sym(a,b)=(a*b^T+b*a^T)/2. (1.2)\n\nThis proves the requested non-strict bound with the frozen coefficient\nb=c-2. On the smaller ray |g|<=K always; this does not mean that\nthe terminal is before its cap. No geometric amplitude grid is needed.\n\n**Candidate conclusion 2: every smaller-tail terminal is paid.**\nFor every permitted function and original nonzero start, if\n\n    x_n=sigma_S*r_n, r_n>=u_(sigma_S)=v+K,\n\nthen\n\n    2f(x_n)/R^2 <= 1/D.                              (1.3)\n\nThus the requested form (1+C0/m)/D holds with the absolute C0=0\nand explicit threshold m>=1536. More specifically:\n\n* If this history has no strict crossing, its score is at most1/D.\n* If it has any strict crossing, it has EXACTLY ONE, from the\n  original larger ray into the smaller ray, and its score is\n  strictly less than2/(3D).\n\nThe last assertion covers arbitrary source positions before that\ncrossing, arbitrary physical crossing levels, and all literal\nprefixes and suffixes. It does not assume that the larger-ray\nprefix is on its tail, or that the crossing occurs at the root.\n\nThe whole separated-cap family is NOT covered by (1.3): crossed\nhistories terminating on the larger ray, or strictly before the\nsmaller cap knot, still need a full original-radius objective bound.\nThis report also supplies no upper bound on the unrestricted\nfinite-dimensional supremum W_n(H_m). The original minimax target\nis OPEN; W_n=2R_n is unchanged.\n\n## 2. Actual objective, exact energies, and physical transcript\n\nFor each ray separately, the derivative is\n\n    G_sigma(r)=kappa*r,              0<=r<=1,\n               r-v,                 1<=r<=u_sigma,\n               B_sigma,             r>=u_sigma.\n\nThe middle may collapse when B_sigma=kappa. Direct integration gives\n\n    2F_sigma(r)=kappa*r^2,                        0<=r<=1,\n                (r-v)^2+kappa*v,                 1<=r<=u_sigma,\n                2B_sigma*r-Ctail_sigma,          r>=u_sigma,\n    Ctail_sigma=B_sigma^2+v*(2B_sigma-kappa).      (2.1)\n\nFor negative x, substituting t=-z in the integral gives\nf(-r)=integral_0^r G_-(z)dz; hence the energy uses the ACTUAL\nterminal-side cap, not the cap of an earlier source. Values and\nderivatives agree at1 and at the ray's own u_sigma. In particular\n\n    2F_(sigma_S)(r)=2K*r-[K^2+v*(2K-kappa)]\n       for r>=v+K.                               (2.2)\n\nThe signed derivative g is continuous and nondecreasing, with\npiecewise slopes kappa,1,0. Partitioning any real interval at\nits finitely many knots proves that its derivative increment lies\nbetween zero and the interval length. Thus g is globally\n1-Lipschitz, f is finite, differentiable, convex, and 1-smooth.\nEvery G_sigma(r)>0 for r>0, so0 is its unique minimizer and f(0)=0.\nFor every ray and radius,\n\n    0<=G_sigma(r)<=min(r,B_sigma).                 (2.3)\n\nEvery point used below is generated by the original GD recursion on\nthis one fixed f. In particular every query and the true terminal\nhave compatible function values and derivatives. For an explicit\ninterpolation check, if x<y and Delta=g(y)-g(x), then\n0<=Delta<=y-x and\n\n    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),\n    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)),  x<=t<=y.\n\nIntegration bounds both corresponding increments below by\nDelta^2/2. Equivalently, every ordered pair of actual points i,j\nobeys\n\n    f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.\n\nThis also includes the minimizer. Repeated points have identical\njets; scalar Gram matrices are positive semidefinite because\ntheir quadratic forms are squares. No separate children or\nunrealized terminal jets have been prescribed.\n\n## 3. Same-m mass estimates and physical tree interfaces\n\nThe only numerical bounds needed in the two arguments are\n\n    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,\n    0<a<b<3/7<1/2,\n    a<5/12,\n    A_j>d_j/b for every 0<=j<=m.                  (3.1)\n\nHere is a direct derivation. Since epsilon<1/1000,\nsqrt(2)>7/5, sqrt(2)<17/12, and\n(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7, the bounds on c follow.\nThe identity lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda give\nc*(c-2)>1>beta, hence a=beta/c<b. Since c>12/5\nand beta<1, a<5/12. Also b=c-2<3/7<1/2.\nSet eta=a/b. Then0<eta<1. The literal word masses satisfy\n\n    A_0=1, A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j.                     (3.2)\n\nThe last expression has the correct initial value; substituting\nit in the recurrence uses 2eta+a=c*eta. It is strictly larger\nthan eta*c^j=d_j/b. In particular at j=0 it gives a<b,\nand for j>=1 one has A_j>=A_1=2+a>2.\n\nEvery physical level-k bridge in H_j is the middle entry of\nan actual subword (H_k,1+d_k,H_k). Its full chronological\nprefix and suffix within H_j therefore have masses at least\nS_k. Immediately after the bridge lies its entire right H_k.\nThe rest of the suffix is exactly\n\n    H_k, (1+d_(l_1)),H_(l_1), ...,\n         (1+d_(l_t)),H_(l_t),\n    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1.                          (3.3)\n\nTo see this, ascend the word tree from the bridge occurrence.\nEach ancestor reached through a left child contributes its\nbridge and right child; each reached through a right child\ncontributes no remaining entries. This includes an empty\nancestor list, skipped levels, and H_0 being empty.\n\nIf a level-k bridge lies within H_l, then k<l. Writing M,N\nfor its prefix and suffix masses WITHIN that H_l gives\n\n    M,N>=S_k,\n    S_l=M+(1+d_k)+N.                             (3.4)\n\nThese are identities for physical entries, not recurrences\nevaluated by a program or factors appended after the terminal.\n\n## 4. Exact signed endpoint map and elementary trajectory payments\n\nFor completeness, retain both caps in the exact one-step interval\nmap. At a source on ray sigma, with h=1+d, d>=0, the signed\noutput relative to that source sign is\n\n    T_sigma(r)=r-hG_sigma(r)\n              =(1-h*kappa)r,          0<=r<=1,\n                h*v-d*r,              1<=r<=u_sigma,\n                r-h*B_sigma,          r>=u_sigma.\n\nWriting [z]_+=max(z,0), its same-ray and opposite-ray maxima\nover the actual source interval0<=r<=R are\n\n    Stay_sigma(R)=max([1-h*kappa]_+*min(R,1),\n                      [R-h*B_sigma]_+),\n    Cross_sigma(R)=max([h*kappa-1]_+*min(R,1),\n                       [d*min(R,u_sigma)-h*v]_+).  (4.1)\n\nEach affine piece has extrema at its endpoints. The middle\npiece decreases, so its positive maximum is already supplied\nat the core endpoint, while its most negative value is at\nmin(R,u_sigma). The tail increases, so it supplies the final\nsame-side endpoint and the cap-knot crossing endpoint.\nIf R<1, the formal middle crossing value is dominated by the\ncore crossing value because their difference is h*v*(R-1)<=0.\nIf R<u_sigma, the formal tail stay is no larger than the\nactual endpoint T_sigma(R), and if positive that actual stay\nis already covered by the core endpoint. Thus absent pieces\ndo not introduce unattained maxima.\n\nFor a signed interval [-P,Q], P,Q>=0, the exact image is\n[-Pnext,Qnext], where\n\n    Qnext=max(Stay_+(Q),Cross_-(P)),\n    Pnext=max(Stay_-(P),Cross_+(Q)).               (4.2)\n\nThe map is continuous and fixes0, so the attained extrema\ngive its entire interval image. This is a two-endpoint map,\nwith the two independently fixed caps. No odd radius-map\nsubstitution is made. The proofs below need only its actual\ntrajectory consequences; they do not execute this map.\n\nIf a strict crossing at a level-k bridge has source radius y,\nsource gradient magnitude p=G_sigma(y), and landing radius z,\nthen exactly\n\n    z=(1+d_k)p-y<=d_k*p.                         (4.3)\n\nThis uses p<=y from (2.3) and keeps the source and destination\nrays separate. In particular a small-to-large crossing has\nz<=d_k*K, while a large-to-small crossing need not have that\nbound with K in place of p.\n\nOn a segment with no strict crossing and a nonzero endpoint,\nthere is no zero hit. All points stay on one ray, all radii\ndecrease, and all gradient magnitudes decrease. If it starts\nat radius Z, ends at radius r with gradient magnitude p,\nand its full mass is U, then\n\n    Z>=r+U*p>=(1+U)*p.                           (4.4)\n\nThe second inequality uses p<=r. If r>=v+B_sigma, every point\nof this segment is on that same tail, so it has the stronger\nidentity Z=r+U*B_sigma.\n\nFor later noncrossing full histories, integrate\nG_sigma(t)<=min(t,p) for0<=t<=r to obtain\n\n    2F_sigma(r)<=2r*p-p^2.\n\nIf the whole H_m is noncrossing, R>=r+S_m*p, so\n\n    2f(x_n)<=2R*p-D*p^2<=R^2/D.                 (4.5)\n\nThe last step follows from D*(p-R/D)^2>=0. This applies on\neither ray and at either cap knot.\n\n## 5. Mechanism 1: larger-ray capture from the last crossing\n\nTake any complete H_j satisfying the premise of conclusion1.\nSince the terminal is nonzero, no earlier point is zero. Choose\nthe LAST strict crossing, of physical level k. As its suffix\nhas no further crossing and ends on the larger ray, this crossing\nis small-to-large. Its source gradient is at mostK, and by\n(4.3) its landing z satisfies z<=d_k*K.\n\nLet T be the mass of its ENTIRE remaining physical suffix.\nEvery point after the crossing stays on the larger ray; (4.4)\napplied to that suffix and its terminal gradient p_n yields\n\n    (1+T)*p_n<=z<=d_k*K.\n\nThe suffix contains its literal right H_k, so T>=S_k. Therefore\n\n    p_n<=d_k*K/(1+T)<=d_k*K/A_k<b*K.\n\nFor k=0 the right child is empty, A_0=1, d_0=a<b; the same\nformula holds without supplying a nonexistent contraction.\nAll ancestors in (3.3) are included in T. Earlier crossings,\nof any signs and levels, are irrelevant to this last-crossing\npayment; they have not been assumed absent.\n\nBecause b<1 and K<B, (1.1) also implies p_n<B, so this\nlarger-ray terminal is strictly before ITS OWN cap. This\ndoes not imply any comparable radius statement on the small\nray. In particular |g|<=K there allows an arbitrarily long\nlinear tail without a separate history payment.\n\nThis direct sign-sensitive physical-suffix proof establishes\nthe frozen state without an auxiliary amplitude induction.\nThere is no q=-1 issue, changed grid cap, replaced curvature,\nor use of c*(c-2)=1. In fact c*(c-2)>1 was retained in (3.1).\nNo unverified prior-child capture is an induction premise.\n\n## 6. Mechanism 2, first interface: a prefix-suffix mass product\n\nFor any physical level-k bridge within H_l, k<l, let M,N\nbe its prefix and suffix masses within that literal child.\nWe claim the strict inequality\n\n    (1+M)*(1+N)>d_l*d_k.                          (6.1)\n\nThis is a new intermediate derivation in the SAME joint\nunverified mechanism, not a separately accepted lemma.\n\nPut X=1+M, Y=1+N, and A=A_k. Equations(3.4) give\n\n    X,Y>=A, X+Y=A_l-d_k.\n\nThus (X-A)(Y-A)>=0 gives\n\n    X*Y>=A*(A_l-d_k-A).                          (6.2)\n\nThe sum condition also gives A<=(A_l-d_k)/2, hence\n\n    A_l-d_k-A >= (A_l-d_k)/2.\n\nSince l>=k+1, d_k<=d_l/c, and (3.1) gives A_l>d_l/b.\nIt follows that\n\n    A_l-d_k-A > d_l*(1/b-1/c)/2\n                > (3/4)*d_l > b*d_l.            (6.3)\n\nFor the middle strict inequality use b<1/2 and c>2, so\n1/b>2 and 1/c<1/2. For the final one use b<1/2<3/4.\nFinally A=A_k>d_k/b. Combining (6.2)-(6.3) proves\nX*Y>d_k*d_l. It includes k=0 and both extremal positions\nof the bridge within its containing word. No symmetry of\nthe actual iterates or equal prefix/suffix masses is needed.\n\n## 7. Every smaller-tail terminal with crossings has only one\n\nSuppose the actual H_m terminal lies on the smaller tail:\nx_n=sigma_S*r_n with r_n>=v+K. Suppose first that there\nis at least one crossing. Let its LAST crossing have level k,\nsource radius y on the larger ray, gradient magnitude p,\nand landing z on the smaller ray. Let T be its complete\nremaining suffix mass. There are no further crossings,\nso all subsequent radii decrease to r_n and all are at\nleast v+K. Therefore every subsequent queried gradient has\nmagnitudeK, and\n\n    z=r_n+T*K >= v+(1+T)*K >= A_k*K.\n\nTogether with (4.3), this gives the necessary inequality\n\n    p> K/b.                                      (7.1)\n\nIndeed d_k*p>=z>=A_k*K and A_k/d_k>1/b.\n\nAssume for contradiction that there is an earlier crossing.\nChoose the PENULTIMATE crossing. It is small-to-large;\nwrite l for its physical level and L for its landing radius.\nIts source cap is K, so (4.3) gives\n\n    L<=d_l*K.                                    (7.2)\n\nBetween these two consecutive crossings every iterate stays\non the larger ray. There are exactly two chronological cases.\n\n**Case A: the last crossing is after the entire immediate\nright H_l of the penultimate bridge has finished.**\nThis H_l has no crossing. Its endpoint gradient q satisfies\n(4.4), including l=0 where the child is empty:\n\n    q<=L/A_l<=d_l*K/A_l<b*K.\n\nAll later larger-ray points before the last crossing have\nnonincreasing radius and gradient. Thus its source p<=q<bK.\nThis contradicts (7.1), since b<1. This argument pays the\nentire child from the actual penultimate landing; it does\nnot reset an original denominator.\n\n**Case B: the last crossing lies inside that immediate\nright H_l.**\nIts level k is then smaller than l. Let M,N be the literal\nprefix and suffix masses of this bridge INSIDE this H_l.\nBefore the last crossing there is no intervening crossing,\nso (4.4) applied from L to its source gives\n\n    L>=y+M*p>=(1+M)*p.                           (7.3)\n\nAfter the last crossing every remaining original iterate,\nin particular the endpoint of this H_l, lies on the smaller\ntail. Denote this child's endpoint radius by r_child.\nIt satisfies r_child>=r_n>=v+K>=K, and the N intervening\nmass has constant gradient magnitudeK. Consequently\n\n    z=r_child+N*K >=(1+N)*K.\n\nCombine this with z<=d_k*p, (7.3), and (7.2). All factors\nare positive, so\n\n    (1+M)*(1+N)*K <=d_k*(1+M)*p\n                   <=d_k*L<=d_k*d_l*K.\n\nDivision byK>0 contradicts (6.1).\n\nBoth cases are impossible. Thus there cannot be a\npenultimate crossing. The last crossing is the FIRST and\nONLY strict crossing of the original trajectory, and its\ninitial ray must be the larger ray. All original earlier\nqueries stay on that ray with decreasing radii. This is a\nderived property of every smaller-tail terminal history,\nnot an itinerary assumption imported from the prior root\nexample. In particular a start on the small ray cannot\nleave and return to a smaller-tail terminal.\n\n## 8. Original-radius payment at every possible crossing level\n\nContinue with a smaller-tail terminal history that has\ncrossed. Section7 permits use of a FIRST-crossing price,\nnow with its full charge explicitly from the original R.\nLet P,T be the total physical masses before and after its\nunique level-k crossing. Let y,p,z be its actual source\nradius, source gradient, and landing as in Section7. Then\n\n    P,T>=S_k,\n    S_m=P+(1+d_k)+T,\n    R>=y+P*p,\n    z=(1+d_k)*p-y,\n    r_n=z-T*K.                                   (8.1)\n\nThe prefix inequality follows by summing EVERY original\nearlier step: its larger-ray gradient is at least p. No\nall-tail assumption about the larger-ray prefix is used.\n\nBy (7.1), p>K/b>K>=kappa. A core source would have\np<=kappa, so y>1. On the larger ray's exterior middle\ny=v+p, and on its tail y>=v+p. Thus\n\n    y>=v+p,\n    R>=v+(P+1)*p,\n    r_n<=d_k*p-v-T*K.                            (8.2)\n\nAt the actual smaller-tail terminal use the exact energy\n(2.2), retaining its intercept before any bound:\n\n    2f(x_n)=2K*((1+d_k)*p-y-T*K)\n                -[K^2+v*(2K-kappa)].             (8.3)\n\nReplacing y by its lower bound v+p gives\n\n    2f(x_n)<=N0,\n    N0=2d_k*p*K-(2T+1)*K^2-4v*K+v*kappa.         (8.4)\n\nThe left side is strictly positive because r_n>=v+K>0;\ntherefore N0>0 as well. As K>=kappa and v>=0,\n-4vK+v*kappa<=0. Using the positive denominator lower\nbounds in (8.2), then discarding this nonpositive numerator\nterm, yields\n\n    2f(x_n)/R^2\n      <= N0/[v+(P+1)*p]^2\n      <= [2d_k*p*K-(2T+1)*K^2]/[(P+1)^2*p^2].    (8.5)\n\nAll denominator reductions apply to positive numerators.\nWith t=K/p>0, completion of the square\n\n    2d_k*t-(2T+1)*t^2\n       =d_k^2/(2T+1)\n         -(2T+1)*(t-d_k/(2T+1))^2\n\ngives the paid position-sensitive bound\n\n    2f(x_n)/R^2 <= d_k^2/[(P+1)^2*(2T+1)].       (8.6)\n\nThe actual capB and source branch constrain feasible p,\nbut enlarging that feasible range in this square bound\nonly increases the upper bound. The source function,\nstart, and queries have not been changed.\n\n## 9. Uniform same-m bound including the leaf case\n\nSet X=P+1, Y=T+1, A=A_k, d=d_k. They satisfy\n\n    X,Y>=A,\n    D=2X+2Y+2d-1.\n\nMultiplying (8.6) by D gives at most\n\n    d^2*(2X+2Y+2d-1)/[X^2*(2Y-1)].              (9.1)\n\nFor fixed X this decreases as Y increases, since its\nY-dependent factor is 1+(2X+2d)/(2Y-1), with positive\ndenominator. Thus replace Y by A. For fixed A the\nremaining X-dependent factor is\n\n    [2X+(2A+2d-1)]/X^2,\n\nwhich decreases on X>0 because it is2/X plus a positive\nconstant divided by X^2. Replacing X by A therefore gives\n\n    D*2f(x_n)/R^2\n      <= d^2*(4A+2d-1)/[A^2*(2A-1)].             (9.2)\n\nThis is a bound using the complete original D, not a\nconversion from a child denominator after discarding\nancestors. It allows every bridge position simultaneously.\n\nIf k>=1, then A>2 and q=d/A<b<3/7. Hence\n\n    d^2*(4A+2d-1)/[A^2*(2A-1)]\n       =q^2*(4+2q-1/A)/(2-1/A)\n       < (9/49)*(4+6/7)/(3/2)\n       =204/343<3/5.                             (9.3)\n\nIf k=0, then A=1 and d=a<5/12, so instead\n\n    d^2*(4A+2d-1)/[A^2*(2A-1)]\n       =a^2*(3+2a)\n       <(25/144)*(23/6)=575/864<2/3.             (9.4)\n\nThis treats the empty H_0 and its possibly first or last\nphysical leaf explicitly. Together (9.3)-(9.4) prove\n\n    2f(x_n)/R^2<2/(3D)\n\nfor every crossing smaller-tail terminal history. Adding\nthe noncrossing estimate (4.5) proves conclusion2 for the\nENTIRE specified terminal class, with C0=0 and m>=1536.\n\nNeither a first exterior crossing estimate nor an interior\nchild-input bound was charged directly to R before Section7\nproved that all earlier crossings are absent. This resolves\nthe original-denominator issue rather than assuming it.\n\n## 10. Boundary checks and scope of the new interfaces\n\nThe preceding arguments include kappa=1, where v=0; there\nis no division by v. Either cap may equal kappa where\nallowed (in particular K=kappa), and the middle piece on\nthat ray may be empty. A smaller terminal at exactly\nr_n=v+K belongs to the tail class and has both matching\nenergy expressions. Every subsequent radius after the last\ncrossing is at least this threshold, so the constant-K\nsuffix identities still hold at equality. A radius1 is in\nthe closed core, but (7.1) strictly excludes it as the\nsource of a crossing with a smaller-tail terminal.\n\nA zero hit is incompatible with a nonzero larger-ray\nterminal or a smaller-tail terminal, so omitting zero in\nthe trajectory-payment arguments loses no relevant case.\nThe allowed initial ray is arbitrary in conclusion1 and\nin conclusion2. The unique-crossing argument proves the\ninitial larger ray when crossings occur in conclusion2.\n\nThe selected bridges and the child containing the final\ntwo crossings are actual physical occurrences in H_m.\nAll suffix mass statements include every remaining query\nand the true terminal; none adds a terminal-only factor\nor pretends that a skipped ancestor is present. Strict\nA_k>d_k/b depends on the same enclosing m, never on a\nreplacement child value of epsilon or beta.\n\nThe inequality K/B<b is part of the frozen assigned\nfamily, but these two particular arguments do not need\nits full strength once K is designated the smaller cap.\nThis observation is only a scope check; no third family\nor additional mechanism is developed.\n\nThe first mechanism now provides the desired two-ray\ngradient state after crossings: p<bK on the larger ray,\np<=K on the smaller. The second pays all smaller-tail\nterminals with their ACTUAL energy, without the invalid\nreplacement 2F_small<=p_terminal^2/kappa on that tail.\n\nThe following interfaces are still absent for a theorem\ncovering ALL starts in the separated-cap family:\n\n1. A gradient or objective estimate charged to original R\n   for crossed histories ending on the larger ray. Capture\n   gives p<bK and ensures a pre-cap endpoint there, but\n   K/R is not bounded by this statement at the required\n   full-word scale. A uniform original-radius bound such\n   as the balanced proof's global-gradient estimate has\n   not been established here.\n2. A corresponding estimate for crossed histories ending\n   strictly before the smaller cap knot (in its core or\n   middle). Their tail payment does not apply. The prior\n   balanced grid and its cap-ratio-paid saturated steps\n   cannot be imported at a separated cap ratio.\n3. Even after those scalar interfaces, the full\n   finite-dimensional W_n(H_m) upper bound required by\n   accepted931b9653ce0f7201 would remain a separate\n   theorem-strength requirement.\n\nNo failure or counterexample to either frozen candidate\nhas been found in this derivation. It proves neither\nthat all separated caps are harmless nor that the\noriginal minimax conjecture is false. The conclusion\nis precisely the two unverified scoped results above.\n\n## 11. {"id": "81cec34e1a158b2b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-03T05:48:17.208357+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max95 COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED: every legal initializer of the frozen full-depth Gray itinerary has true-distance score<343/(333D_m)<17/(16D_m), uniformly m>=1536. The prescribed pair alone suffices, via lambda_(N/4-1,N-1)=2sigma_n/(sigma_(N/4-1)+sigma_n). Exact perspective with zero-boundary feasibility caveat, full polar geometry, both dual signs, inherited pair constants and exact dyadic prefix sums included. This is NOT a full-function upper or minimax resolution.", "evidence": "# All initializers of the frozen Gray itinerary: a two-vertex upper certificate\n\n**JOINT UNVERIFIED.** Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`, worker max,\ninvocation95. Governing guidance `59d1537ae33f6ddd`; plan\n`8ce881a97f71d8a8`. This is one self-contained analytical proof attempt,\nnot a verifier-accepted fact. The current allocation explicitly permits\nzero formal calls/fact_submit. The original minimax target remains OPEN.\n\n## 1. Result, normalization, and exact scope\n\nFor the precise full-depth geometry defined below, the proposed result is:\nfor every integer m>=1536 and EVERY initializer realizing the prescribed\noriginal vertex itinerary, its actual terminal closest-minimum score obeys\n\n    2 Phi(z_n) / dist(z_0,argmin Phi)^2\n       < 343/(333 D_m) < 17/(16 D_m),\n    D_m=1+2 sum_(k<n) h_k.                              (1)\n\nThere is no existence assertion for a legal initializer. The estimate\nalready holds in the relaxation retaining only the ORIGINAL vertices\n\n    i=2^(m-2)-1=N/4-1,   n=N-1,\n\nboth ordered normals between them, and their two zero-vertex normals.\nTheir Gram matrix AND chronological prefix constants are inherited from\nthe complete original trajectory, not recomputed on a two-step word.\nA single positive multiplier, on the original orientation i->n, supplies\nthe certificate. All other pair and zero constraints remain present but\nhave multiplier zero. The proof retains the negative cross inner product,\nboth radial factors, and every tiny Walsh character.\n\nThis bounds one fixed body WITH one prescribed itinerary. It is neither\nan upper bound for every trajectory on that body nor a full-function upper\nbound for H_m. It cannot be combined with a benchmark comparison to claim\nminimax separation. The original W=2R normalization is the factor 2 in (1).\nFor advertised smoothness L>0, use L Phi and steps h_k/L; the same points\nand score result.\n\nThe all-initializer perspective interface and its weak dual are also derived\nbelow. There is a necessary qualification to the proposed interface: its\nzero point is feasible even when the legal-initializer set is empty.\nTherefore literal equality to a supremum over legal initializers requires\nnonemptiness, or the convention of adjoining the zero score. No conic\nstrong duality or unproved feasibility is used.\n\nOne mechanism suffices. The conditional three-vertex mechanism is not\nopened. A relative-potential change cannot escape (1) while satisfying all\nfrozen original rows; general geometry remains open.\n\n## 2. Frozen objects and complete Gram kernel\n\nLet m>=1536 be an integer and put\n\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_l=1+a c^l, h_k=H_(nu_2(k+1)), 0<=k<n.\n\nHere nu_2 is the exponent of 2 in a positive integer. H_l denotes a\nsingle level-l physical step, whereas H_m in the task denotes the entire\nrecursively concatenated word. Define its depth-j sum, with the SAME m,\n\n    S_j=sum_(k<2^j-1) H_(nu_2(k+1))\n       =a c^j/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))2^j-1.                 (2)\n\nAt each level l<j there are 2^(j-l-1) steps; summing their 1 and a c^l\nparts as finite geometric series proves (2). No recurrence is executed.\n\nSet Kmix=N-1-m and eta=1/(N^2 Kmix). For each nonzero binary character\nb in F_2^m, chi_b(u)=(-1)^(b dot u). Let b^(k)=N-2^k, 0<=k<m.\nAssign positive character amplitudes omega_b by\n\n    omega_(b^(k))^2=2^k/(N-1),\n    omega_b^2=eta for every other nonzero b.\n\nFor each binary label u put\n\n    Wframe(u)=((omega_b chi_b(u))_(b!=0),1/sqrt(3)),\n    Gray(k)=k xor floor(k/2), w_k=Wframe(Gray(k)),\n    T=4/3+1/N^2.\n\nXor and popcount mean the binary exclusive-or and number of 1 bits.\nThe character identity chi_(b^(l))(Gray(k))=(-1)^bit_l(k) follows by\ncancelling the successive Gray bits from l through m-1. The sum of all\ncharacters is N on equal labels and zero otherwise: pair labels in any\ncoordinate in which they differ. Subtracting the constant and distinguished\ncharacters gives the EXACT kernel\n\n    B_kl=<w_k,w_l>\n      =1/3-eta+sum_(j<m)(2^j/(N-1)-eta)\n                       (-1)^(bit_j(k)+bit_j(l))\n                     +eta N 1_(k=l)\n      =T-2(k xor l)/(N-1)+2eta popcount(k xor l)\n                     -eta N+eta N 1_(k=l).           (3)\n\nIn particular B_kk=T. The mixed characters are present in every formula.\n\nThe exact adjacent level energy and inner product are\n\n    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta(N/2-l-1),\n    B_l^adj=T-2E_l.\n\nLet rho_l be the positive root of\n\n    rho_l^2+(H_l-2)(B_l^adj/T)rho_l+1-H_l=0.\n\nThese are the prescribed diagnostic roots. Put\n\n    x=r_0=rho_0(1-4/N), y=r_1=rho_1(1-4/N),\n    r_l=1 for l>=2,\n    sigma_0=1, sigma_(k+1)=sigma_k r_(nu_2(k+1)),\n    v_k=sigma_k w_k, D0=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}.          (4)\n\nNo high-level root replaces r_l=1. Exact low-level counts give\n\n    sigma_k=x^ceil(k/2)y^floor((k+1)/4).             (5)\n\nAll character amplitudes and all sigma_k are positive. The full Walsh\nmatrix, including its constant vertical row, is invertible by the same\northogonality identity. Thus the N vectors v_k are a basis of R^N. Let V\nbe the matrix with these vectors as columns and G=V^T V, so G is positive\ndefinite. These independence statements do not omit small characters.\n\n## 3. Legal potentials, actual values, and the complete minimum set\n\nDefine\n\n    P_k=sum_(l<k)h_l v_l, A_kl=<P_k,v_l>,\n    C_kl=A_kl-A_kk+G_kl-G_kk,\n    K_k=A_kk+G_kk, c_n=A_nn+G_nn/2.\n\nFor an initializer z_0, write fpot_k=<z_0,v_k>. These are inner-product\npotentials, NOT sampled function values. Conversely every real vector\nfpot is realized uniquely by\n\n    z_0=V G^(-1) fpot.                              (6)\n\nConsider the fixed function\n\n    Phi(z)=max_(v in D0){<z,v>-||v||^2/2}.\n\nCompleting the square gives the unique maximizer projection_D0(z).\nA proposed maximizer v satisfies\n\n    <z-v,w-v><=0 for every w in D0.                 (7)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance along a segment\nfrom v to w. Conversely expanding ||z-w||^2 shows (7) makes v the unique\nnearest point. It suffices to check generating vertices, by linearity.\nAdding (7) for two projections proves their firm nonexpansiveness and\nhence their 1-Lipschitz property. The two affine maxima bound the remainder\nPhi(z')-Phi(z)-<projection_D0(z),z'-z> between 0 and ||z'-z||^2.\nThus Phi is finite, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, with gradient\nprojection_D0. These elementary facts also appear in accepted fact\n`1e0bf3f41457d8e4`, Part A Section 2; no stronger assertion from that fact\nis required here.\n\nAt the prescribed points z_k=z_0-P_k, (7) is EXACTLY\n\n    fpot_l-fpot_k<=C_kl for every original ordered pair k,l,\n    fpot_k>=K_k for every original k.               (8)\n\nFor the second line use w=0. Therefore (8) is both necessary and sufficient\nfor all v_k, including the terminal v_n, to be the actual projections.\nUnder (8), z_(k+1)=z_k-h_k grad Phi(z_k) for all k<n, and\n\n    Phi(z_k)=fpot_k-A_kk-G_kk/2,\n    Phi(z_n)=fpot_n-c_n>=G_nn/2>0.                 (9)\n\nThere is no physical h_n. The complete minimum set is\n\n    Kpolar=cone{v_k}^polar={z:V^T z<=0}, min Phi=0.  (10)\n\nA polar point makes all defining expressions nonpositive, while v=0\nsupplies zero. If <z,v_k>>0, a sufficiently small positive multiple of\nv_k belongs to D0 and has positive defining expression; this proves the\nconverse. This is the full polar cone, not a selected vertical slice.\n\n## 4. Exact closest-minimum geometry, perspective, and zero boundary\n\nFor ANY fpot, let z_0 be (6) and write Rtrue=dist(z_0,Kpolar). Changing\nvariables d=V^T(z_0-zstar) in the distance minimization gives\n\n    Rtrue^2=r(fpot):=min_(d>=fpot) d^T G^(-1)d.       (11)\n\nIndeed zstar is polar iff V^T zstar=fpot-d<=0, and the vector difference\nis V G^(-1)d with precisely that squared norm. The positive definite\nquadratic is coercive, so its minimum over the closed nonempty orthant\ntranslate exists and is unique.\n\nFor completeness its geometry follows without a duality theorem. At its\nminimizer d*, let alpha=G^(-1)d*. Coordinate variations give alpha_k>=0;\nif d*_k>fpot_k, both variation signs are allowed and alpha_k=0. Thus\n\n    alpha>=0, d*>=fpot, alpha_k(d*_k-fpot_k)=0.\n\nPut p=V alpha and zstar=z_0-p. Then p belongs to cone{v_k}, zstar belongs\nto its polar, and <p,zstar>=0. For any polar y,\n\n    ||z_0-y||^2=||p||^2+||zstar-y||^2-2<p,y>\n               >=||p||^2.\n\nThis proves the full closest-minimum assertion directly. In particular a\nlegal fpot has positive radius: fpot_0>=K_0=G_00>0 excludes polarity.\n\nConsider the finite convex program\n\n    maximize 2F_n-2tau c_n\n    tau>=0,\n    F_l-F_k<=tau C_kl for ALL original ordered pairs,\n    F_k>=tau K_k,\n    d>=F, d^T G^(-1)d<=tau.                         (12)\n\nThe variables F are rescaled potentials. Convexity means the feasible set\nis convex and the maximand is linear. There is no solver use here.\n\nForward direction: for legal fpot set tau=1/r(fpot), F=tau fpot and\nchoose d=tau d*. Then d^T G^(-1)d=tau and the objective is exactly\n2(fpot_n-c_n)/r(fpot).\n\nReverse direction: given a feasible point with tau>0, fpot=F/tau obeys\nALL of (8), has the unique actual realization (6), and\n\n    r(fpot)<=d^T G^(-1)d/tau^2<=1/tau.\n\nBy (9) its terminal numerator is strictly positive. Hence its actual\nscore is AT LEAST the value of (12). Pointwise equality is not asserted\nwhen the supplied quadratic inequality is slack. At an optimum the two\nsuprema nonetheless coincide by the two inequalities just proved.\n\nZero boundary: tau=0 forces d=0 by positive definiteness. The inequalities\nd>=F and F>=0 force F=0, so the objective is zero. This point always\nexists, independently of itinerary feasibility. Thus (12) equals\n\n    sup({0} union {actual scores of legal initializers}).              (13)\n\nIf a legal initializer exists, its score is positive and (13) is exactly\nthe proposed supremum. If none exists, (12) has only its zero point, while\nthe conventional supremum of the empty legal set is not zero. Equivalently,\npositive-tau feasibility is precisely legal-initializer feasibility.\nThis qualification matters because full itinerary feasibility is not proved.\n\nThe maximum in (12) exists. Since K_0=G_00, feasibility gives\nF_0>=tau G_00, d_0>=F_0, and\n\nd^T G^(-1)d>=d_0^2/G_00>=tau^2 G_00.\n\nThe first inequality follows by applying Cauchy--Schwarz to\nV G^(-1)d and v_0. Consequently tau<=1/G_00 when tau>0. The quadratic\nconstraint bounds d. The lower bounds F_k>=tau K_k and upper bounds F<=d\nthen bound F. The feasible set is closed and bounded. If its maximum is\npositive, a positive-tau maximizer recovers an actual maximizer via (6).\n\n## 5. Weak-dual signs and complete-square certificate\n\nLet lambda_kl>=0 and nu_k>=0 be any fixed multipliers, and define\n\n    rho=2e_n+out(lambda)-in(lambda)+nu,\n    kappa=2c_n-sum lambda_kl C_kl+sum nu_k K_k.\n\nHere e_n is the coordinate vector, out_k=sum_l lambda_kl and\nin_k=sum_l lambda_lk. Suppose rho>=0 componentwise and kappa>0.\nFor every legal potential,\n\n    rho^T fpot-kappa\n       =2(fpot_n-c_n)\n        +sum lambda_kl [C_kl-(fpot_l-fpot_k)]\n        +sum nu_k(fpot_k-K_k)\n       >=2(fpot_n-c_n).                             (14)\n\nThis explicitly fixes the orientation and both signs. For its closest\npolar point zstar, g=V rho belongs to cone{v_k}, so\n\n    rho^T fpot=<z_0,g>\n       <=<z_0-zstar,g><=Rtrue ||g||.\n\nThus, writing b=||g|| and u=1/Rtrue,\n\n    2(fpot_n-c_n)/Rtrue^2\n       <=b u-kappa u^2\n        =b^2/(4kappa)-kappa(u-b/(2kappa))^2\n       <=rho^T G rho/(4kappa).                     (15)\n\nAlternatively, in (12), (14) becomes objective<=rho^T F-kappa tau,\nwhich is <=rho^T d-kappa tau<=b sqrt(tau)-kappa tau; complete the square\nin sqrt(tau). This also includes tau=0. No strong-duality assertion is\nneeded. These multipliers belong to itinerary normals, not the earlier\noriginal smooth-convex GD interpolation dual.\n\n## 6. The exact two-vertex interface and inherited prefix constants\n\nLet r=m-2, Lq=2^r=N/4, i=Lq-1, and put\n\n    theta=x^2 y, s=theta^(Lq/4)=sigma_i,\n    t=theta^(N/4)=sigma_n=s^4,\n    B=<w_i,w_n>=T-3N/[2(N-1)]+4eta-eta N.           (16)\n\nThe index xor is 3N/4 and its popcount is 2. The exact submatrix is\n\n    Gpair=[[s^2 T, s t B],[s t B,t^2 T]].             (17)\n\nIt is positive definite as a principal submatrix of G. In particular\nT+B>0. Also B<0 and\n\n    T+B=7/6+2/N^2-3/[2(N-1)]-eta(N-4)<7/6.         (18)\n\nThe last inequality uses N>=4, with 2/N^2<3/[2(N-1)].\n\nHere are closed finite dyadic formulas for all chronological constants;\nthey are symbolic identities, not instructions to run a recurrence.\nFor integers 2<=j<=m, let Pcal_j(z,w) be the following POLYNOMIAL,\nwith empty products equal to 1:\n\n [H_0 z+x H_1 z^2 w+x H_0 z^3 w]\n       product_(b=0)^(j-3)[1+w(theta z^4)^(2^b)]\n +sum_(l=2)^(j-1) H_l w^l(theta z^4)^(2^(l-2))\n       product_(b=0)^(j-l-2)[1+w(theta z^4)^(2^(l-1+b))].              (19)\n\nIts exact expansion is\n\n    Pcal_j(z,w)=sum_(k<2^j-1)h_k sigma_k z^(k+1)w^popcount(k).\n\nFor the three low positions k=4q,4q+1,4q+2 use respectively radii\n theta^q,x theta^q,x theta^q and popcounts pop(q),pop(q)+1,pop(q)+1.\nFor level l>=2 write k=(2q+1)2^l-1: its radius is\n theta^((2q+1)2^(l-2)) and popcount is l+pop(q). Expanding the binary\nproducts in (19) enumerates precisely these disjoint level classes.\nThis proves the formula without enumeration by a program.\n\nDefine the exact sums\n\n    U_j=Pcal_j(1,1),\n    M_j=(partial_z Pcal_j)(1,1)-U_j,\n    Z_j=(partial_w Pcal_j)(1,1).\n\nThus they sum h_k sigma_k, k h_k sigma_k, and popcount(k)h_k sigma_k,\nrespectively. A scalar alternative for the first is\n\n    U_j=(1-theta^(2^(j-2)))\n       [(H_0(1+x)+x H_1)/(1-theta)\n        +sum_(l=2)^(j-1) H_l theta^(2^(l-2))\n                                      /(1-theta^(2^(l-1)))].         (20)\n\nEvery denominator is positive. This follows by summing each level's\nfinite geometric progression in (19).\n\nPut U=U_r,M=M_r,Z=Z_r and\n\n    b_i=T-2(Lq-1)/(N-1)+2eta r-eta N,\n    b_n=T-2+2eta m-eta N,\n    J_i=b_i U+2M/(N-1)-2eta Z=<P_i,w_i>,\n    J_pre=b_n U+2M/(N-1)-2eta Z=<P_i,w_n>,\n    J_n=b_n U_m+2M_m/(N-1)-2eta Z_m=<P_n,w_n>.\n\nThese use k xor i=i-k for k<i and k xor n=n-k for k<n. In particular\n\n    A_ii=s J_i, A_in=t J_pre, A_nn=t J_n.             (21)\n\nTo make the reverse row equally explicit, define\n\n    E_i=sum_(q=0)^3 s^q[J_i+(-2q Lq/(N-1)+2eta popcount(q))U]\n       +H_r s T\n       +H_(r+1) s^2[T-2Lq/(N-1)+2eta-eta N]\n       +H_r s^3[T-4Lq/(N-1)+2eta-eta N].\n\nThen A_ni=s E_i. For each quarter interior k=qLq+j, j<Lq-1, the\nradius is s^q sigma_j and k xor i=(q+1)Lq-1-j. The three bridge\nindices are qLq-1, q=1,2,3. Their xor values against i are 0,Lq,2Lq.\nThe q=1 bridge is the actual diagonal and therefore contributes T,\nnot T-eta N. This proves the displayed expression including its\notherwise easily missed diagonal correction.\n\nAll pair data are now exact:\n\n    K_i=s J_i+s^2 T, K_n=t J_n+t^2 T,\n    c_n=t J_n+t^2 T/2,\n    C_in=t J_pre-s J_i+s t B-s^2 T,\n    C_ni=s E_i-t J_n+s t B-t^2 T.                   (22)\n\nWriting u=fpot_i,v=fpot_n, the two-dimensional potential domain is\n\n    u>=K_i, v>=K_n, v-u<=C_in, u-v<=C_ni. Its elementary\nactive-face cases are:\n\n* If u<=0 and v<=0, the minimum is 0 at d=(0,0).\n* If u>0 and v<=b u/p, it is u^2/p at d=(u,b u/p).\n* If v>0 and u<=b v/q, it is v^2/q at d=(b v/q,v).\n* Otherwise both faces are active: d=(u,v) and the displayed quadratic\n  is the value; its two coordinate derivatives are nonnegative.\n\nThese follow by setting free-coordinate derivatives to zero and checking\nthe remaining derivative sign. The positive terminal numerator in (23) is at least\nq, and the certificate below forces a positive radius on any feasible\npoint of (23), so the zero-distance case cannot cause a positive-score\nsingularity here.\n\nThis is a relaxation of the full initializer problem: retaining two\nhalfspaces enlarges the polar minimum set, so the pair distance is no\ngreater than Rtrue. The numerator and chronological constants stay\nunchanged. Therefore an upper bound for all of (23), with this smaller\ndistance, is an upper bound for every full legal initializer.\n\n## 7.
