Consequently n>=32m at our threshold.\nSince (1-epsilon)^(-1)>1+epsilon and the binomial expansion gives\n(1+epsilon)^(4m)>1+4m epsilon=2,\n\n beta^n<1/256, s S<1/256, D s^2<3/65536<1/64.             (2.3)\n\nThe last inequality uses D<3S<3c^m. These are symbolic inequalities;\nno parameter, recurrence, product, or trajectory was evaluated by a program.\n\nTwo scalar abbreviations used only in the first-family calculations are\n\n K0=(S-ab)/(Ctot T), J0=pi^2+delta D^2/T^2.\n\nDirect expansion over the denominator Ctot^2 T^2 gives\n\n J0=2K0-a^2/T^2,\n 1/(2S)<K0<2/S, J0>1/(2S).                               (2.4)\n\nFor the lower K0 bound,\n2S(S-ab)-Ctot T=S^2-(1+2ab)S+ab>0 since ab<3/4 and S>64.\nThe upper bound is K0<1/T<2/S. Therefore\nJ0>1/S-1/S^2>1/(2S), proving the last assertion.\n\n## 3. Original polynomial, flow, and complete coefficient coordinates\n\nNormalize smoothness to1 and the optimum to x_star=g_star=f_star=0.\nThe formal vectors x0,g_0,...,g_l,g_n remain independent. Write\n\n x_i=x0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nThe signed original interpolation rows satisfy Q_ij<=0 on every legal\ntrajectory. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies this orientation and\nthe exact terminal-reduction scope. Selecting incoming-terminal rows zero\ndoes NOT delete any original ordered queried-prefix row. The selected\nterminal multipliers are\n\n lambda_(n,star)=2pi, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n lambda_(i,n)=lambda_(star,n)=0.\n\nInitially keep both optimum directions, mu_i=lambda_(i,star) and\nnu_i=lambda_(star,i). Let e be the n-dimensional all-ones column and e_i\nits coordinate columns. Let Ltri have diagonal1, entry h_j in row i,column\nj for j<i, and zero above its diagonal. Set\n\n v=(h_0,...,h_(l-1),1)^T=Ltri^T e_l,\n t=Ltri e, t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, t_l=T,\n w=Ltri^(-1)e, y_i=x_i-g_i, zeta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nExpanding the original rows gives\nQ_ij=zeta_i-zeta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>,\nQ_(i,star)=zeta_i-<g_i,y_i>, Q_(star,i)=-zeta_i.\nDefine the full n-by-n matrix N by\n\n N_ij=-lambda_(i,j) for i!=j,\n N_ii=mu_i+sum_(j!=i)lambda_(i,j).\n\nCancellation of every queried value is exactly\n\n Ne=mu, N^T e=2alpha+nu.                                  (3.1)\n\nIndeed the queried value coefficient is prefix outflow minus prefix\ninflow minus 2alpha_i. This fixes the orientation of the column equation.\nLet\n\n r=h-Ltri^T alpha=pi v+a e_l,\n r_i=pi h_i (i<l), r_l=a+pi,\n V=e^T r=a+pi T=Dpi-1.\n\nThe identity x_n-sum alpha_i y_i=pi x0-r^T g and expansion of the terminal\nrows give the FULL formal-vector polynomial after value cancellation:\n\n M=q||x0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n  =||g_n-pi x0+r^T g||^2+(q-pi^2)||x0||^2\n    +2<x0,(pi r-mu/2)^T g>\n    +g^T[sym(N Ltri)-r r^T]g.                             (3.2)\n\nHere g denotes the column of queried formal gradient vectors and\nsym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. This derivation uses no actual-trajectory restriction.\n\nFor either chosen column u below, with e^T u=D, define\n\n dsrc=(e_0-e_1)/b,\n dtail=a gbar e_(l-2)+(1-a beta)e_(l-1)-b e_l,\n chi=1-u^T w, gamma=q0-s^2-delta chi^2.\n\nThe family, and no larger unknown metric, is\n\n M_u=||g_n-pi x0+r^T g||^2\n       +delta||x0-u^T g||^2+gamma||dsrc^T g||^2\n       +kappa||x0+theta dsrc^T g||^2\n       +xi||dtail^T g||^2,                               (3.3)\n\nwhere theta is real and xi>=0. Coefficient comparison in (3.2) forces\n\n mu=2pi r+2delta u-2kappa theta dsrc,\n P=r r^T+delta u u^T+(gamma+kappa theta^2)dsrc dsrc^T\n                        +xi dtail dtail^T.              (3.4)\n\nSince e^T mu=2pi V+2delta D=2(1-pi), summing (3.1) gives\nsum nu=0. Nonnegative optimum incoming multipliers therefore force every\nnu_i=0 within THESE families, including at q>q0. This is not a claim\nthat nu vanishes in the unrestricted loose-target certificate cone.\n\nEvery remaining original coefficient, including remote rows, is specified\nby the following necessary and sufficient equality interface:\n\n A^T=-A,\n Ae=E:=Pe-2Ltri^T alpha=V r+delta D u-2(1-pi)v,\n Aw=F:=mu-Pw,\n N=(P+A)Ltri^(-1).                                       (3.5)\n\nIt remains necessary that mu>=0 and N_ij<=0 for ALL i!=j. These signs\nare not implied by the moment equations or by (3.3) being PSD.\nThe complete entry formula is\n\n N_ij=P_ij+A_ij\n       -h_j sum_(vindex>j)(P_(i,vindex)+A_(i,vindex))\n                         product_(j<uindex<vindex)(1-h_uindex). (3.6)\n\nThe inverse has diagonal1 and entry\n-h_j product_(j<uindex<vindex)(1-h_uindex) below its diagonal in\nrow vindex,column j. Solving Ltri z=e_j proves this inverse: after the\nfirst nonzero column coordinate, the next coordinate is multiplied by\n1-h at each step. Thus (3.6) retains the original coordinates, rather than\nthe different older y-coordinate convention.\n\nFor any compatible skew, the original array is\nlambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct queries, lambda_(i,star)=mu_i,\nlambda_(star,i)=0, with the terminal coefficients specified above.\nNo row remains to be silently selected or deleted.\n\n## 4. Full constant-gradient and unit-quadratic checks; all skew compatibilities\n\nThe two detail columns have sum zero. In particular,\na gbar+1-a beta-b=0. On common queried gradients G the entire trial is\n\n ||g_n-pi x0+V G||^2+delta||x0-D G||^2+kappa||x0||^2.       (4.1)\n\nThis matches (3.2): the remaining affine Gram has entries\nq-pi^2, pi(V+1)-1, D-(V+1)^2, and V+1=Dpi.\nAt the actual positive-affine Huber history x0=D G,g_n=G, it equals\nkappa D^2||G||^2, the original objective slack. The initial radius is\nthe original one; there is no restart.\n\nFor f(x)=||x||^2/2 starting at x0, the actual queried gradients are w_i x0,\nwhere w_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j). The full signed product is w_n=-s.\nTo derive it, the exponent of c from the ruler levels equals n-m;\nequivalently sum_(j=1)^(m-1)(2^(m-j)-1)=n-m. Multiplying the n factors\n-a c^level gives -a^n c^(n-m)=-beta^n c^(-m).\nThe original last factors are -a,-beta,-a, so\n\n w_0=1, w_1=-a,\n w_l=s/a, w_(l-1)=-s/(a beta), w_(l-2)=s/(a^2 beta).\n\nConsequently\n\n e^T dsrc=e^T dtail=0,\n dsrc^T w=1, dtail^T w=0,\n v^T w=1, r^T w=pi+s.                                   (4.2)\n\nFor v, telescope sum_(i<l)h_i w_i=1-w_l and add w_l.\nFor dtail its product is\ns[gbar-(1-a beta)-b beta]/(a beta)=0.\n\nThe terminal square in (3.3) therefore vanishes on the entire actual\nunit-quadratic trajectory. Every original interpolation row also vanishes.\nThe trial value is\n\n delta chi^2+gamma+kappa(1+theta)^2\n   =q0-s^2+kappa(1+theta)^2.\n\nThe original polynomial value is q-s^2. Thus matching forces, for either\ncolumn and with the newly recomputed payment,\n\n theta(theta+2)=0, that is theta=0 or theta=-2.             (4.3)\n\nThis is independently rederived, not imported from the former u.\n\nThe necessary and sufficient linear conditions for a real skew A in (3.5)\nare e^T E=0, w^T F=0, e^T F=-w^T E. The vectors e,w are independent\nbecause w_0=1 and w_1=-a. With (4.2), the explicit second moment is\n\n F=(pi-s)r+delta(1+chi)u-[gamma+kappa theta(theta+2)]dsrc.  (4.4)\n\nDirect substitution gives\n\n e^T E=V^2+delta D^2-2(1-pi)T=0,\n w^T F=q0-s^2-delta chi^2-gamma-kappa theta(theta+2)\n      =-kappa theta(theta+2),\n e^T F+w^T E=e^T mu-2alpha^T Ltri w=0.                    (4.5)\n\nThus ALL linear compatibilities hold at both allowed theta values for every\nxi>=0. To exhibit sufficiency, set wbar=e^T w/n,\ncvec=w-wbar e, fvec=F-wbar E+(E^T w/n)e. Then\n\n Acan=(E e^T-e E^T)/n\n       +(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/(cvec^T cvec).           (4.6)\n\nThe three scalar identities give e^T fvec=cvec^T fvec=0, so multiplying\n(4.6) by e and w gives E and F. Every completion is Acan+B with\nB^T=-B and Be=Bw=0. The cuts below exclude them ALL.\n\nThe full clock column, sometimes lost by changing coordinates, is\n\n Nt=(P+A)e=2Pe-2Ltri^T alpha,\n Pe=V r+delta D u.                                       (4.7)\n\nAlthough the detail metrics vanish in Pe, they remain in P_ii, in every\nentry (3.6), and in all row-capacity comparisons. The mixed theta term\nremains in mu. In particular theta=-2 has not been replaced by an unmixed\nq0-plus-radius certificate.\n\n## 5. Mechanism 1: check the assigned u_hat and its positive payment\n\nFreeze exactly the assigned first law\n\n u_old=(D/T)v, Delta=b Ctot/T,\n u_hat=u_old-Delta(e_0-e_l).                              (5.1)\n\nIt has sum D and\n\n (u_hat)_0=b, (u_hat)_i=(D/T)h_i for 0<i<l,\n (u_hat)_l=Uhat:=D/T+Delta.\n\nThe source identity uses D-Ctot=T. Every coordinate is positive. Retaining\nthe last coordinate in (4.2) gives the EXACT product payment\n\n chi_hat=1-D/T+Delta(1-s/a)\n        =(Ctot/T)(a-b s/a),\n gamma_hat=q0-s^2-delta chi_hat^2.                        (5.2)\n\nThis is not the old a^2/T^2-s^2. The terminal correction b s/a is retained.\nBy (2.1)-(2.3), b s/a<15/1024<1/4<a, and\n0<chi_hat<(17/16)(1/2)=17/32. Since (17/32)^2<1/3,\n\n gamma_hat>q0(2/3-1/64)>q0/2, gamma_hat<q0.               (5.3)\n\nOnly now is PSD of (3.3) asserted: every displayed weight is nonnegative,\nand delta,kappa,gamma_hat are strictly positive.\n\nThe forced optimum coefficients can be written explicitly as\n\n mu=2K0 v+2pi a e_l-2delta Delta(e_0-e_l)\n                         -2kappa theta dsrc.             (5.4)\n\nIn particular mu_0=2q0 b-2kappa theta/b and\nmu_l=2K0+2pi a+2delta Delta. At theta=0 every mu_i>0.\nAt theta=-2 only index1 loses mass; there\nmu_1=2K0 gbar-4kappa/b>1/S-1/(8S)>0 by (2.4).\nAll terminal signs pass as 0<pi<1 and alpha=(1-pi)e_l.\n\n### Source and tail checks, with the full paid terms retained\n\nThe first column of Ltri is (1,b,...,b)^T. Therefore\n\n mu_0-P_00=a sum_(j>0)lambda_(0,j).\n\nFor any of our u with u_0=b this exact left side is\n\n C_source=q0(1-a^2)-gamma/b^2\n                  -kappa(theta^2+2b theta)/b^2.           (5.5)\n\nAt theta=0, gamma<q0 and (2.1) give\nC_source>q0(3/4-25/49)=47q0/196>q0/5.\nAt theta=-2 it increases by 4kappa a/b^2, so the same strict margin holds.\nThus the advertised source repair is real.\n\nEquation (4.7) at coordinate0 has (Pe)_0=b(1-pi), yielding the exact\nadditional source clock condition\n\n sum_(j>0)lambda_(0,j)(t_j-1)=mu_0.                       (5.6)\n\nThis includes the changed theta=-2 optimum coefficient; it has not been\nremoved from the row equations.\n\nAt the original last query, P_ll=N_ll. Hence the full tail capacities are\n\n P_ll-2(1-pi)=sum_(i<l)lambda_(i,l)>=0,\n P_ll-mu_l=sum_(j<l)lambda_(l,j)>=0,\n P_ll=(a+pi)^2+delta Uhat^2+xi b^2.                       (5.7)\n\nHere D/T<3, Delta<2, so Uhat<5. Therefore the xi=0 diagonal is\nless than (17/32)^2+25/(2S)<489/1024<1/2, whereas\n2(1-pi)>31/16. The last-column condition requires the nontrivial price\n\n xi>=[2(1-pi)-(a+pi)^2-delta Uhat^2]/b^2.                (5.8)\n\nNo smallness assumption about xi is permitted or used. The last-row\ncapacity already passes at xi=0 because\nP_ll-mu_l=a^2-pi^2+delta Uhat(Uhat-2)>0; Uhat>2 follows\nfrom D/T>2 and Delta>0. The adjacent tail-pair sum, with p=l-1, is exactly\n\n N_(p,l)+N_(l,p)=2P_(p,l)-gbar P_ll\n  =gbar[pi^2-a^2+delta Uhat(2D/T-Uhat)]\n       -xi b[2(1-a beta)+gbar b].                         (5.9)\n\nIts two individual signs are still required by (3.6), not inferred from\ntheir sum. The entire last-row clock is (1.3) with Uhat substituted into\nthe general formula in Section7. These formulas retain the original\nlast-three metric, both original orientations, and the actual terminal.\n\n## 6. Mechanism 1 decisive cut: a remote physical root\n\nHere derive (1.1) directly from the original array, not from any assumed\nskew support. Let k=2^(m-1)-1, H=sum_(j<k)h_j=sum H_(m-1),\nR=h_k=1+a c^(m-1), and z=1_[0,k). The original step immediately\nbefore k is b, and alpha_j=0 for every j<k. Since\n\n (Ltri z)_i=t_i for i<k, (Ltri z)_i=H for i>=k,\n\nexpanding z^T N Ltri z and using column sums (N^T e)_j=0 for j<k\ngives the exact prefix incoming identity\n\n C_prefix:=H sum_(j<k)mu_j-z^T P z\n          =sum_(i>=k,j<k)lambda_(i,j)(H-t_j).             (6.1)\n\nFor explicit cancellation, the internal prefix edges cancel when their\nrow and column contributions are collected. If I_cross and O_cross\ndenote total incoming and outgoing crossing weights, the prefix sum of\nmu is I_cross-O_cross, and z^T Pz is\nsum_(i>=k,j<k)lambda_(i,j)t_j-H O_cross. This proves (6.1).\n\nThe original k-th diagonal gives\n\n P_kk=mu_k+sum_(j<k)lambda_(k,j)\n                       -(R-1)sum_(j>k)lambda_(k,j).      (6.2)\n\nFor every j<k,\nH-t_j=sum_(r=j)^(k-1)h_r-1>=h_(k-1)-1=a.\nAdding a times mu_k-P_kk to (6.1) proves precisely the nonnegative\noriginal-row expansion (1.1), including all remote rows.\n\nFor the assigned u_hat, the source detail has z^T dsrc=0 and dsrc_k=0;\nthe last-three detail has z^T dtail=0 and dtail_k=0. Thus theta and xi\ncancel EXACTLY from (6.1),(6.2), however large their permitted values.\nThe original terminal weights and alpha are unchanged.\n\nThe scalar prefix sums for (5.1) are\n\n z^T r=pi H, z^T u_hat=(D/T)H-Delta,\n sum_(j<k)mu_j=2K0 H-2delta Delta,\n z^T Pz=J0 H^2-2delta(D/T)H Delta+delta Delta^2,\n mu_k=2K0 R, P_kk=J0 R^2.\n\nConsequently\n\n C_prefix=a^2 H^2/T^2\n             +(delta Ctot^2/T^2)(2bH-b^2).               (6.3)\n\nBecause H<S/2, T>S/2, and\ndelta Ctot^2=S(S-2ab)/D<S/2,\n\n C_prefix<1/4+3<4.                                      (6.4)\n\nThe bound is an upper bound on the entire prefix resource, not a decision\nthat it alone is sufficient. At the actual root,\n\n R>a c^(m-1)>(a/c)S>4S/25>S/8,\n mu_k<4, P_kk>S/128,                                    (6.5)\n\nusing (2.4), R<S, and S<c^m. Since S>4096, the quantity\n4-S/128 is negative and a>2/5. Combining (6.4),(6.5) gives\n\n C_root<4+(2/5)(4-S/128)=28/5-S/320<-S/640<0.            (6.6)\n\nThe last comparison uses S/640>4096/640>28/5. This contradicts (1.1)\nfor the entire first family, including both compatible theta values and\nevery xi meeting the mandatory tail price (5.8). It is independent of all\nneutral skew freedom, not a failure of a guessed coefficient array.\n\n## 7. Mechanism 2: the one frozen further redistribution and its payment\n\nThe negative term in (6.6) comes from the common-column diagonal at a\nremote large step. The sole additional law transfers ALL interior common\nmass to the original last query:\n\n u_new=u_hat-sum_(i=1)^(l-1)(u_hat)_i(e_i-e_l)\n      =b e_0+(D-b)e_l.                                   (7.1)\n\nThis law was recorded before evaluating its signs. It has no fitted\ncoefficient or unspecified metric, and retains the identical r,pi,alpha,\nq,dsrc,dtail,theta,xi from the first family. Write dlast=D-b=2S-a.\nThe exact full unit product is\n\n u_new^T w=b+dlast s/a,\n chi_new=-a-dlast s/a,\n gamma_new=q0-s^2-delta(a+dlast s/a)^2.                    (7.2)\n\nNo old payment is reused. Since dlast<2S and a>2/5,\ndlast s/a<5/256<1/32. Hence |chi_new|<17/32 and the same explicit\nargument as (5.3) gives\n\n q0/2<gamma_new<q0.                                      (7.3)\n\nThus (3.3) with this exact payment is PSD for every theta and xi>=0.\nEquations (3.4)-(3.6) and (4.1)-(4.7), derived jointly for either column,\ngive its full coefficient and compatibility equations. In particular the\nentire original unit-quadratic still forces theta=0,-2, and every linear\nskew compatibility holds at those two values.\n\nHere the optimum coefficients are\n\n mu_0=2q0 b-2kappa theta/b,\n mu_i=2pi^2 h_i-2kappa theta(dsrc)_i for 0<i<l,\n mu_l=2pi(a+pi)+2delta dlast.                             (7.4)\n\nAt theta=0 all are positive. At theta=-2,\nmu_1=2pi^2 gbar-4kappa/b<9/S^2-1/(18S)<0 for S>4096.\nThe bound uses pi<3/(2S), gbar<2, D<3S and b<3/2.\nSo theta=-2 already fails an ORIGINAL optimum outgoing row.\nThe decisive cut below covers every theta anyway.\n\nThe source formula (5.5), with gamma_new, again has margin>q0/5 at\ntheta=0,-2. This second law also genuinely removes the first obstruction.\nAt the same root its exact cut is\n\n C_root,new=pi^2[H^2+a(2R-R^2)]+delta(2bH-b^2)>0.         (7.5)\n\nTo prove the sign, use vword=a/(c-2)>2a and the positive term\n(1-vword)2^(m-1) in the mass formula. It gives\nH>2a c^(m-1)-1=2R-3>R since R>S/8>3. Thus\nH^2-aR^2>0, 2aR>0 and 2bH-b^2>0. This is a passed necessary\nscreen, not the claimed success of the new family.\n\nNow preserve the actual tail and clock equations. For any u with u_l=U\nin these two families, the last query has\n\n P_ll=(a+pi)^2+delta U^2+xi b^2,\n mu_l=2pi(a+pi)+2delta U,\n (Pe)_l=V(a+pi)+delta D U,\n (Nt)_l=2(Pe)_l-2(1-pi).                                 (7.6)\n\nBoth dsrc_l and its mixed contribution are zero, whereas dtail_l=-b.\nTherefore the full xi b^2 term remains in the last diagonal. The same\nformula (7.6) applies with U=Uhat or U=dlast without a change of coordinates.\n\nThe original last row has outgoing sum P_ll-mu_l. Since the actual\npenultimate physical step is gbar, every earlier clock difference obeys\nT-t_j>=gbar, with equality only at j=l-1. Expanding that row of Nt gives\n\n (Nt)_l=T mu_l+sum_(j<l)lambda_(l,j)(T-t_j).\n\nSubtract gbar times the last-row outgoing sum. This proves the literal\nnonnegative cut (1.3). Its full value, retaining every tail metric term, is\n\n C_last(U)=(1-beta)a^2+2b pi+gbar pi^2-2\n             +delta U[2(Ctot+gbar)-gbar U]-gbar xi b^2.   (7.7)\n\nFor the constant part, substitute V=a+pi T into (7.6):\n2(a+pi)(a+pi gbar)-gbar(a+pi)^2-2(1-pi)\n=(1-beta)a^2+2b pi+gbar pi^2-2.\nThus no large terminal common coefficient or tail metric is omitted.\n\nFor the frozen endpoint law U=dlast=D-b, the bracket in (7.7) is exactly\n\n 2(Ctot+gbar)-gbar(D-b)\n   =-2beta S+b(2+gbar)+gbar\n   <-(3/2)S+8<-S.                                       (7.8)\n\nThe constant part of (7.7) is negative: it is less than\n1/4+3/32+1/512-2<0 by (2.1),(2.2).\nFinally Dpi^2<27/(4S)<1/2, so delta>1/(2D), while\ndlast=D-b>D/2. Consequently delta dlast>1/4. Equations (7.7),(7.8)\ngive the strict uniform result\n\n C_last(dlast)<-S/4-gbar xi b^2<=-S/4<0.                 (7.9)\n\nThis contradicts the nonnegative sum (1.3), for every real theta and\nevery xi>=0, regardless of completing skew. The second family is decisively\nexcluded. It repaired the first root cut but placed too much common mass\nat a query whose nearest earlier clock is already gbar units away.\nAdding the included last-three PSD detail can only worsen (7.9).\n\n## 8. Status, dependencies, and bounded-round handoff\n\nBoth mechanisms are complete as scoped joint algebraic exclusions. No\nadditional common column or metric is opened. There is no assertion that\nthe displayed obstructions settle the feasibility of the unrestricted\noriginal certificate cone.\n\nThe useful new cut interfaces are (1.1)/(6.1)-(6.2) and (1.3)/(7.7).\nThey expose respectively the available incoming resource before a remote\nlarge query and the last query's weighted outgoing resource. A future\ndifferent law must satisfy both; passing either alone is insufficient.\nThese interfaces and the conclusions remain UNVERIFIED shared findings,\nnot premises licensed for use as fact-graph results.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used at its exact original interpolation and\nterminal-reduction scope. All new coefficient identities and inequalities\nare rederived in this one draft. The previous draft a40ecd591138eb2c was\nread in full as context, not used as a verified predecessor. The former\naccepted gate1e0bf3f41457d8e4 is not an all-depth premise.\n\nAccepted931b9653ce0f7201 has a CONDITIONAL full-function implication from\nW_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) for EVERY m>=1536 to an asymptotic benchmark\nseparation. This report supplies no such full-function bound. Its exact\nbenchmark scope therefore does not turn either family obstruction into a\nresolution of PROBLEM.md. The original radius and W=2R are unchanged.\n\nTASK, latest master_guidance, own local memory, shared findings/dead ends\nand relevant accepted facts were recalled. The actual\nH4 endpoint inequality is not refuted. Both mandatory actual histories\nare retained with the complete actual right H3 and all remainders; their\nfull endpoint gaps are positive. No child is reset.\n\nThe accepted predecessor is fact1e0bf3f41457d8e4. Its Part B proves the\nactual H3 U law for every d>=1 and supplies exact H2/off-root/remainder\nidentities. Its universality theorem is not reproved, and H3 is not used\nas if it were H4. No sufficient H4 certificate or recursive interface is\nobtained here. H4 at d=1, H4 at all d, all-depth propagation, and the\noriginal asymptotic minimax target remain OPEN. The original selected\nminimizer denominator and W=2R are unchanged.\n\n## 2. Fixed original word and all actual samples\n\nFix ONE integer m>=1536 throughout. Define\n\n epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,\n H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), S_j=sum H_j.\n\nUse these abbreviations, retaining the exact meanings in source finding\n11adbf577f491d4a:\n\n A=1+a, B=1+beta, t=S2=2A+B, C=1+t,\n k=beta*c=d_2, K=beta*c^2=d_3, ell=C+k,\n s=S3=2C+k-1=t+ell, C3=1+s=2C+k,\n b=1+K, h=s+b=C3+K, S=S4=2s+b.\n\nS is the LOCAL mass of H4, not the enclosing S_m. Its fifteen steps are\n\n (h0,...,h14)=(A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A,1+K,A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A).    (2.1)\n\nFor a fixed nonempty compact convex set D0 containing zero in a positive\nfinite-dimensional real Euclidean space, write P for projection onto D0,\n\n F(X)=max_(g in D0)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2),\n U_d(X)=max_(g in D0)(2<X,g>-d||g||^2), d>0.\n\nEvery physical query obeys x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i, g_i=P(x_i), i<15.\nThe actual terminal gradient is g15=P(x15); it supplies no GD step. Put\n\n x=x0, u=x3, z=x4, v=x7, w=x8, uR=x11, zR=x12, y=x15.\n\nThus z=u-(1+k)g3, w=v-b*g7, zR=uR-(1+k)g11. At d=1 set\n\n J=1+2t, D=1+2s, M=1+2S=D+2h,\n eta=M-2s=D+2b, E=eta+2t,\n M=E+2ell, D=J+2ell, E-J=2h.                          (2.2)\n\nThe auxiliary samples on the SAME D0 are ACTUAL maximizers\n\n p0=P(x/M), rL=P(z/E), rR=P(zR/J), q=P(y)=g15,\n qL=P(v/eta), rO=P(w/D).                              (2.3)\n\nTheir sample inputs are respectively x/M,z/E,zR/J,y,v/eta,w/D.\nNo extra physical steps or arbitrary feasible replacements are introduced.\nFor any sample labels i,j use the original ordered orientation\n\n Q_(i,j)=<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j><=0.                        (2.4)\n\nThis is the projection specialization in accepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4,\nPart B(2.3), citing its predecessor1252b8823bb5a290. A nonnegative row\ncoefficient below ALWAYS means a coefficient of -Q_(i,j).\n\n## 3. Complete base rows, reserves, and endpoint identity\n\nThe following identities are jointly reconstructed from accepted Part B,\nnot assumed true because they appeared in an earlier unverified finding.\nFor a word of mass T, input X, endpoint Y, its primitive energy is\nV=||X||^2-||Y+T*P(Y)||^2, by accepted Part B(2.4).\n\nSet\n\n C1=1+A, kap1=(3C1+beta)/(C1-beta),\n R1=(C1+beta)[C1^2-beta(2C1+beta)]/(C1-beta),\n deltaA=2-A^2,\n e0=deltaA, e1=R1(2/B-1), e2=kap1*deltaA+R1(2/A-1).\n\nThe eight H2 multipliers from accepted Part B(3.5)-(3.6) are\n\n Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=2(1+A), Lambda_12=2R1/B,\n Lambda_21=(kap1-1)A+2R1/B,\n Lambda_13=(kap1-1)beta, Lambda_31=kap1-1,\n Lambda_23=2kap1+(kap1-1)A+2R1/A,\n Lambda_32=2kap1(1+A)+2R1/A.                          (3.1)\n\nAll unlisted entries are zero. All listed entries and e0,e1,e2 are\npositive, as proved in accepted Part B(3.4)-(3.7).\nFor o in {0,4,8,12} let\n\n C_o=-sum_(i!=j in {0,1,2,3})Lambda_ij Q_(o+i,o+j),\n P_o=sum_(j=0)^2 e_j||g_(o+j)-g_(o+j+1)||^2,\n V_o=C_o+P_o.\n\nUse C00,C01,C10,C11 for offsets0,4,8,12, and analogous P,V labels. Set\n\n kap=(3C+k)/(C-k), kapO=(3C3+K)/(C3-K),\n d_i=(C+k)[k(2C+k)-C^2]/(C-k),\n d_o=(C3+K)[K(2C3+K)-C3^2]/(C3-K),\n Bterm=kapO*d_i+d_o, Di=(1+kapO)d_i.                  (3.2)\n\nThe defect d_i is not a sequence element d_j. Both d_i,d_o are strictly\npositive. To check, put u0=sqrt(2)-1 and use\n\n k-u0*C=epsilon[u0+4u0^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda*epsilon]>0.\n\nHere u0>2/5,lambda<5/2,epsilon<=1/6144. Also\nK/C3=c*(k/C)/(2+k/C)>u0, since the displayed rational map is increasing,\nc>lambda and lambda*u0/(2+u0)=u0. Factor\nk(2C+k)-C^2=(k-u0*C)(k+lambda*C), and similarly for K,C3.\nThe elementary bounds C>11/2,k<5/2,C3>13,K<25/4 imply C>k,C3>K.\nThus all denominators and the stated defects are positive.\n\nThe three ORIGINAL join sums are\n\n E_i=sum_(j=4)^6 h_j Q_(j,3)+Q_(7,3)+k Q_(3,7)\n                                      +sum_(j=4)^6 h_j Q_(j,7),\n E_r=sum_(j=12)^14 h_j Q_(j,11)+Q_(15,11)+k Q_(11,15)\n                                      +sum_(j=12)^14 h_j Q_(j,15),\n E_o=sum_(j=8)^14 h_j Q_(j,7)+Q_(15,7)+K Q_(7,15)\n                                      +sum_(j=8)^14 h_j Q_(j,15).\n\nDefine\n\n C_rows=C00+kap*C01+kapO*C10+kapO*kap*C11\n       -(kap-1)E_i-kapO*(kap-1)E_r-(kapO-1)E_o,\n P_sq=P00+kap*P01+kapO*P10+kapO*kap*P11.              (3.3)\n\nHere is the full base coefficient array, including every overlap. Begin\nwith zero at every ordered pair. For each o in{0,4,8,12}, add nu_o Lambda_ij\nat pair(o+i,o+j), with\n\n                 (nu_0,nu_4,nu_8,nu_12)=(1,kap,kapO,kapO*kap).\n\nFor each tuple (p,n,I,e,Lam) in\n\n (3,7,{4,5,6},k,kap-1),\n (11,15,{12,13,14},k,kapO*(kap-1)),\n (7,15,{8,...,14},K,kapO-1),\n\nadd Lam*h_j at(j,p) and(j,n) for each j in I, Lam at(n,p), and Lam*e\nat(p,n). Sum coincident entries. Every base entry is now explicitly given.\n\nApply accepted Part B(3.3) to both inner joins with capacities C,C, then\nto the outer join with capacities C3,C3. Its off-root coefficient is -d_i\nor -d_o. Substitution gives the EXACT full primitive identity\n\n V4=C_rows+P_sq+Fdef,\n Fdef=-d_i||g3||^2+(d_i-d_o)||g7||^2\n                      -kapO*d_i||g11||^2+Bterm||q||^2.   (3.4)\n\nFor clarity: the two child defects are\n-d_i(||g3||^2-||g7||^2) and\n-kapO*d_i(||g11||^2-||q||^2); the outer defect is\n-d_o(||g7||^2-||q||^2). This verifies every diagonal in(3.4).\nNo primitive positivity of H3 or H4 was assumed.\n\nLet\n\n Hvec=sum_(i<15)h_i*g_i=x-y,\n Vmix=Hvec+(1+S)q, Wstar=M*p0-Vmix,\n Nstar=2<x-M*p0,Wstar>, Qbase=||Wstar||^2+P_sq+Fdef.\n\nThe mixture Vmix/M has nonnegative weights summing to M. Applying accepted\nPart B(4.2),(4.5) to the WHOLE H4 at d=1 gives\n\n M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]=C_rows+Nstar+Qbase.                  (3.5)\n\nThis is a full endpoint equality, with the whole actual right H3 included.\nIt does not drop the matched radial terms: it reassembles them through the\naccepted exact primitive and maximization identities. At d=1 the terminal\nradial term is zero because q=P(y), not by a sign estimate.\n\n## 4. Complete retained attachment family and original added rows\n\nPut\n\n TL=sum_(i<4)h_i*g_i=x-z, TR=sum_(i<12)h_i*g_i=x-zR,\n L01=A*g4+B*g5+A*g6, L10=A*g8+B*g9+A*g10,\n Udiff=(1+k)g3+L01+b*g7+L10=u-uR,\n Zdiff=L01+b*g7+L10+(1+k)g11=z-zR.\n\nThe allocated nonnegative normals are\n\n Ng=-Q_(11,3)-Q_(3,11)=<Udiff,g3-g11>-||g3-g11||^2,\n Na=-E Q_(rR,rL)-J Q_(rL,rR)\n   =<Zdiff,rL-rR>-E||rL||^2-J||rR||^2+(E+J)<rL,rR>,\n Nc=-Q_(rR,11)-J Q_(11,rR)\n   =k||g11||^2+(J-k)<g11,rR>-J||rR||^2.              (4.1)\n\nNa uses UNEQUAL denominators E,J and cancels x exactly. Retain\n\n theta=d_i/k, omega=d_i, tau>=0,\n wL,wR>=0, t_i>=0 (0<=i<15),\n t_i+wL*1_(i=3)+wR*1_(i=11)<=h_i.                   (4.2)\n\nThe proofs of obstruction in fact hold for arbitrary nonnegative theta,\nomega, but their literal source values remain fixed in the actual family.\nWrite\n\n DeltaA=wL(rL-g3)+wR(rR-g11),\n DeltaT=sum_(i<15)t_i(q-g_i), Tsum=sum_(i<15)t_i,\n Delta=DeltaA+DeltaT, W2=Wstar-Delta,\n mu_i=h_i-t_i-wL*1_(i=3)-wR*1_(i=11),\n mu_q=1+S+Tsum, mu_L=wL, mu_R=wR,\n N2=2<x-M*p0,W2>.\n\nThese weights are nonnegative and sum to M. The other extracted original\nnormals are\n\n AL=<z-E*rL,rL-g3>=-E Q_(3,rL),\n AR=<zR-J*rR,rR-g11>=-J Q_(11,rR),\n NT_i=<y-q,q-g_i>=-Q_(i,15).                         (4.3)\n\nStarting with(3.5), replace Nstar by N2+2<x-M*p0,Delta>.\nSubtract2wL AL,2wR AR and2sum t_i NT_i. Since z=x-TL,zR=x-TR,\ny=x-Hvec, this cancels the ENTIRE x coefficient and gives\n\n Q2=Qbase\n    +2wL<TL+E*rL-M*p0,rL-g3>\n    +2wR<TR+J*rR-M*p0,rR-g11>\n    +2<Hvec+q-M*p0,DeltaT>-omega*Ng-theta*Na-tau*Nc,\n\n M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]\n  =C_rows+N2+2wL*AL+2wR*AR+2sum t_i*NT_i\n                            +omega*Ng+theta*Na+tau*Nc+Q2. (4.4)\n\nThis rederives source Q2 from the accepted endpoint identities rather than\nusing its unverified PSD claims. Every physical and auxiliary coordinate\nhas been retained. In particular expansion about the changed mixture has\n-||Delta||^2; changing the mixture does not simply change the square.\n\nFor both new mechanisms below, keep ALL these additional ordered rows:\n\n (i,p0):2M mu_i for i<15; (15,p0):2M mu_q;\n (rL,p0):2M wL; (rR,p0):2M wR;\n (3,rL):2wL E;\n (11,rR):J(2wR+tau); (rR,11):tau;\n (11,3):omega; (3,11):omega;\n (rR,rL):theta E; (rL,rR):theta J;\n (i,15):2t_i for i<15.                              (4.5)\n\nAdd these to the changed base arrays, summing coincident entries. All other\nadditional entries are zero. This gives every final ordered coefficient,\nincluding initial factor M and auxiliary factors E,J.\n\n## 5. Mechanism1: exact common-zeta return and its admissible interval\n\nDefine the three actual symmetric physical normals\n\n JL=-Q_(7,3)-Q_(3,7), JR=-Q_(15,11)-Q_(11,15),\n JO=-Q_(15,7)-Q_(7,15).                              (5.1)\n\nLet the corresponding displacements be\n\n DL=x3-x7=(1+k)g3+sum_(j=4)^6 h_j*g_j,\n DR=x11-x15=(1+k)g11+sum_(j=12)^14 h_j*g_j,\n DO=x7-x15=sum_(j=7)^14 h_j*g_j.\n\nAdding the two ORIGINAL orientations in each pair gives exactly\n\n JL=<DL,g3-g7>-||g3-g7||^2,\n JR=<DR,g11-q>-||g11-q||^2,\n JO=<DO,g7-q>-||g7-q||^2.                            (5.2)\n\nEach is nonnegative on an actual projection history. For zeta>=0 freeze\n\n rhoL=zeta*d_i/k, rhoR=kapO*rhoL, rhoO=zeta*d_o/K,\n Rz=rhoL*JL+rhoR*JR+rhoO*JO,\n Cz=C_rows-Rz, Qz=Q2+Rz.                            (5.3)\n\nReplacing C_rows,Q2 by Cz,Qz in(4.4) preserves the endpoint equality.\nThis really changes the original row allocation. It is not an additional\nnormal with a silently changed sign.\n\nHere are BOTH ordered coefficients at every affected original pair:\n\n pair        coefficient in Cz before the additions(4.5)\n (7,3)       kap-1-rhoL\n (3,7)       k(kap-1)-rhoL\n (15,11)     kapO(kap-1)-rhoR\n (11,15)     kapO*k(kap-1)+(kapO-1)(1+k)-rhoR\n (15,7)      kapO-1-rhoO\n (7,15)      K(kapO-1)-rhoO.                        (5.4)\n\nThe term(kapO-1)(1+k) at(11,15) is the ORIGINAL OUTER sum's j=11\ncontribution; it cannot be omitted. No H2 base pair coincides with any\nof these six pairs. Formula(3.3)'s full entry rule proves this rather\nthan presuming disjoint children. In the complete final list, add2t11\nat(11,15) and2t7 at(7,15), as prescribed by(4.5); the other four displayed\npairs receive no attachment addition.\n\nSince k>1,K>1, the reverse orientations (7,3),(15,11),(15,7) are the\nlimiting ones. The interval is EXACTLY\n\n 0<=zeta<=zeta_max,\n zeta_max=min{k(kap-1)/d_i, K(kapO-1)/d_o}\n         =min{2k/delta_i,2K/delta_o},\n delta_i=k(2C+k)-C^2, delta_o=K(2C3+K)-C3^2.         (5.5)\n\nThe forward right-inner overlap makes its bound weaker, not stronger.\nEven allowing the terminal additions to support a reduced forward row\ndoes not enlarge(5.5), because its three limiting reverse rows are unchanged.\nEvery other original row is untouched and nonnegative.\n\nLiteral zeta=1 is strictly allowed. Here is an analytic bound, with no\nnumerical evaluation. Put Cref=lambda^2=5+2u0 and C3ref=lambda^3=2Cref+lambda.\nThen\n\n 0<Cref-C=epsilon[1+4u0/(1+epsilon)]<3epsilon,\n k=lambda(1-epsilon^2), 0<lambda-k<3epsilon^2,\n 0<C3ref-C3<7epsilon,\n Kref=lambda^2, 0<K-Kref=Kref(epsilon-epsilon^2-epsilon^3)<6epsilon.\n\nAt(Cref,lambda) and(C3ref,Kref) the corresponding delta is zero. Therefore\n\n 0<delta_i\n   =(k-lambda)(k+lambda+2C)+(Cref-C)(Cref+C-2lambda)\n   <36epsilon<1<2k,\n\n 0<delta_o\n   =(K-Kref)(K+Kref+2C3)+(C3ref-C3)(C3ref+C3-2Kref)\n   <6epsilon*43+7epsilon*30=468epsilon<1<2K.         (5.6)\n\nWe used Cref<6,C3ref<15,Kref<6,K<25/4 and epsilon<=1/6144. In particular\nzeta_max>1. These are uniform symbolic inequalities at the original m;\nno fitted interval or recurrence replay is involved.\n\n## 6. Entire nineteen-coordinate matrix, rederived with returned rows\n\nThe independent formal labels are I={p,0,1,...,15,L,R}; p means p0,\nL means rL,R means rR and15 means q. A formal assignment gives an arbitrary\nEuclidean vector G_alpha to each of these NINETEEN labels. It is not an\nassertion that these vectors form a legal projection history. Let e^alpha\nbe the unit coefficient column at label alpha, and define\nsym(a,b)=(a*b^T+b*a^T)/2. These columns are coefficient bookkeeping only.\n\nAll columns needed to specify every matrix entry are\n\n Hc=sum_(i<15)h_i e^i,\n wc=M e^p-Hc-(1+S)e^15,\n tcL=sum_(i<4)h_i e^i, tcR=sum_(i<12)h_i e^i,\n acL=tcL+E e^L-M e^p, dcL=e^L-e^3,\n acR=tcR+J e^R-M e^p, dcR=e^R-e^11,\n bc=Hc+e^15-M e^p,\n dcT=sum_(i<15)t_i(e^15-e^i),\n uc=sum_(i=3)^10 h_i e^i, dcG=e^3-e^11,\n zc=sum_(i=4)^11 h_i e^i, dcA=e^L-e^R,\n vcL=sum_(i=3)^6 h_i e^i, jcL=e^3-e^7,\n vcR=sum_(i=11)^14 h_i e^i, jcR=e^11-e^15,\n vcO=sum_(i=7)^14 h_i e^i, jcO=e^7-e^15.             (6.1)\n\nSet f_3=-d_i,f_7=d_i-d_o,f_11=-kapO*d_i,f_15=Bterm, and all other\nf_alpha=0. Define\n\n Pmat=sum_(o in{0,4,8,12})nu_o sum_(j=0)^2\n                e_j(e^(o+j)-e^(o+j+1))(e^(o+j)-e^(o+j+1))^T,\n Gmat=sym(uc,dcG)-dcG*dcG^T,\n Amat=sym(zc,dcA)-E e^L(e^L)^T-J e^R(e^R)^T\n                                      +(E+J)sym(e^L,e^R),\n Cmat=k e^11(e^11)^T+(J-k)sym(e^11,e^R)-J e^R(e^R)^T,\n JLmat=sym(vcL,jcL)-jcL*jcL^T,\n JRmat=sym(vcR,jcR)-jcR*jcR^T,\n JOmat=sym(vcO,jcO)-jcO*jcO^T. For any advertised\nsmoothness L>0, multiplication of the constructed objective by L and use\nof steps h_k/L preserves these normalized scores and physical points.\n\n## 2. Frozen geometry and the exact original kernel\n\nFix one integer m>=1536 for the whole word and every child, and put\n\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_l=1+a c^l, h_k=H_(nu_2(k+1)), 0<=k<n.\n\nHere nu_2 is the exponent of two dividing its positive integer argument.\nH_l in displayed formulas denotes a single level-l step, whereas the\ntask's H_m denotes the complete recursively concatenated word. Define\n\n    S_j=sum_(k<2^j-1)h_k\n       =a c^j/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))2^j-1.               (2.1)\n\nAt level l<j there are 2^(j-l-1) occurrences. Summing the 1 and a c^l\nparts by finite geometric sums proves (2.1); no recurrence is evaluated.\n\nSet Kmix=N-1-m and eta=1/(N^2 Kmix). Binary labels are elements of F_2^m;\ntheir character pairing is modulo two. For every nonzero label b set\nchi_b(u)=(-1)^(b dot u). For b^(j)=N-2^j, 0<=j<m, the character amplitude\nsatisfies omega_(b^(j))^2=2^j/(N-1). Every other nonzero character has\nomega_b^2=eta. Retain all these positive amplitudes and set\n\n    w_k=((omega_b chi_b(Gray(k)))_(b!=0),1/sqrt(3)),\n    Gray(k)=k xor floor(k/2), T=4/3+1/N^2.\n\nXor is binary exclusive-or; pop is binary popcount. Cancellation of\nsuccessive Gray bits proves chi_(b^(j))(Gray(k))=(-1)^bit_j(k).\nThe sum over all binary characters at a nonzero label is zero: pair\ncharacters differing in a coordinate where that label is one. The sum\nis N at the zero label. Subtract the constant and distinguished\ncharacters from this identity to obtain the exact kernel\n\n    B_kl=<w_k,w_l>\n       =T-2(k xor l)/(N-1)+2eta pop(k xor l)\n                                   -eta N+eta N 1_(k=l).             (2.2)\n\nThus B_kk=T. For an actual level-l adjacent step, write\n\n    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta(N/2-l-1),\n    B_l^adj=T-2E_l.\n\nLet rho_l be the positive root of\n\n    rho_l^2+(H_l-2)(B_l^adj/T)rho_l+1-H_l=0.\n\nThe ONLY changed law in this round is\n\n    x=r_0=rho_0(1-2/N), y=r_1=rho_1(1-2/N),\n    r_l=1 for l>=2,\n    sigma_k=x^ceil(k/2)y^floor((k+2)/4),\n    v_k=sigma_k w_k, D0=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}.        (2.3)\n\nThe level-zero positive integers at most k are the odd integers, giving\nceil(k/2) occurrences. The level-one integers are 2,6,10,..., giving\nfloor((k+2)/4) occurrences. This proves the formula for sigma_k\nand sigma_(k+1)=sigma_k r_(nu_2(k+1)). No high root is substituted into\nthe law. Walsh orthogonality, with its positive constant vertical row,\nmakes the N frame vectors a basis of R^N. Positive radial scaling\npreserves independence. Let V be the matrix with columns v_k and\nG=V^T V, so G is positive definite. All tiny characters are essential\nto, and retained in, this full-dimensional assertion.\n\n## 3. Full legality, actual values, and closest-minimum interface\n\nDefine the original chronological prefixes and constants\n\n    P_k=sum_(j<k)h_j v_j, A_kl=<P_k,v_l>,\n    C_kl=A_kl-A_kk+G_kl-G_kk,\n    K_k=A_kk+G_kk, c_n=A_nn+G_nn/2.                 (3.1)\n\nLet fpot_k=<z_0,v_k> denote initializer potentials, not sampled function\nvalues. Every real potential vector has the unique realization\n\n    z_0=V G^(-1)fpot.                               (3.2)\n\nFor Phi(z)=max_(v in D0)(<z,v>-||v||^2/2), the exact projection normal is\n<z-v,w-v><=0 for all generating vertices w, including zero. This follows\nby differentiating squared distance along each segment; the converse\nfollows by expansion of squared distance. Completing the square gives\ngrad Phi=P_D0. This necessary condition is independent\nof all potentials and common shifts. Its converse is not used to assert\nfeasibility here. For a prescribed relative vector p, zero rows alone\ncan always be paid by the explicit common shift\n\n    gamma(p)=1+sum_(j=0)^n |K_j-p_j|.              (3.5)\n\nIndeed p_k+gamma(p)>K_k for every k. Basis independence in (3.2) ensures\nthis is an actual initializer potential vector, although the pair rows\nmay fail. In particular zero rows are not a hidden restriction on the\nrelative differences when every initializer is allowed.\n\nFor any potential vector, its exact squared distance to the complete\nminimum set is\n\n    r(fpot)=min_(d>=fpot) d^T G^(-1)d.              (3.6)\n\nTo see this, put d=V^T(z_0-zstar). The condition zstar in Kpolar is\nexactly d>=fpot and the squared norm of V G^(-1)d is the displayed\nquadratic. Coercivity supplies the minimum. This proves the radius\nidentity without choosing a vertical minimizer.\n\nFor clarity the associated perspective has variables tau,F,d and is\n\n    sup 2F_n-2tau c_n,\n    tau>=0, F_l-F_k<=tau C_kl, F_k>=tau K_k,\n    d>=F, d^T G^(-1)d<=tau.                        (3.7)\n\nA legal fpot maps to tau=1/r(fpot), F=tau fpot and d=tau d*, with d*\nminimizing (3.6). Conversely a feasible point with tau>0 gives a legal\nfpot=F/tau and r(fpot)<=1/tau. Its strictly positive numerator in (3.4)\nmakes its score at least the objective in (3.7). Thus the two suprema\nagree when the legal set is nonempty. At tau=0 positive definiteness\nforces d=0, and F>=0 with F<=d forces F=0. Hence (3.7) ALWAYS includes\nzero and equals the zero-adjoined supremum of legal scores. It must not\nbe equated to the conventional supremum of an empty initializer set.\n\nFor arbitrary nonnegative pair multipliers lambda_kl and zero-row\nmultipliers nu_k, put\n\n    rho=2e_n+out(lambda)-in(lambda)+nu,\n    kappa=2c_n-sum lambda_kl C_kl+sum nu_k K_k.\n\nHere e_n is the terminal coordinate vector and out/in are row/column\nsums. If rho>=0 and kappa>0, expansion gives\n\n    rho^T fpot-kappa\n      =2(fpot_n-c_n)\n       +sum lambda_kl[C_kl-(fpot_l-fpot_k)]\n       +sum nu_k(fpot_k-K_k) >=2(fpot_n-c_n).\n\nVrho is in the positive cone. For the true closest polar point,\nrho^T fpot<=sqrt(r(fpot))*||Vrho||. Completing the scalar square gives\n\n    2(fpot_n-c_n)/r(fpot) <= rho^T G rho/(4kappa).   (3.8)\n\nBoth signs and the terminal half-square are fixed in these identities.\nNo strong-duality premise is required. The inequalities for N and Kmix\nfollow by doubling from 2^m>=2m+2.\n\nFor l=0,1 write H_l=1+b with b=a or beta. The root identity is\n\n    (1-rho_l)(rho_l+b)=2(1-b)E_l rho_l/T. This gives\n\n    N(1-rho_0)>(280/459)(999/1000)>121/200.\n\nSubstituting the NEW x,y of (2.3), not the old law, now gives\n\n    13/5<N(1-x)<53/20<3,\n    2<N(1-y)<2001/1000<3.                          (4.4)\n\nFor example N(1-x)=N(1-rho_0)+2rho_0; its lower bound exceeds\n2+121/200-4/(3N)>13/5. Put theta=x^2y and\n\n    zeta=-(N/4)log(theta).\n\nIntegrating 1/(1-u) on [0,u] proves u<=-log(1-u)<=u/(1-u).\nConsequently\n\n    9/5<zeta<7301/[4000(1-3/N)]<11/6.              (4.5)\n\nThe last rational comparison follows from 194>132000/N.\nSet r=m-2, Lq=2^r=N/4, i=Lq-1 and\n\n    s=sigma_i=theta^(Lq/4)=exp(-zeta/4),\n    t=sigma_n=theta^(N/4)=s^4.\n\nUseful completely rational consequences are\n\n    63/100<s<16/25, t>5/32, s^3<27/100. Directly from its formula and N>=2^16,\n\n    -1<B<0, 9/8<T+B<7/6.                          (7.2)\n\nFor example T+B=7/6+2/N^2-3/[2(N-1)]-eta(N-4).\nThe negative correction is bounded by 3/N+2/N^2<1/24 for the lower\nbound; for the upper bound use 2/N^2<3/[2(N-1)].\n\nHere are finite closed expressions for every needed prefix, using the\nnew x,y. For 2<=j<=m define the polynomial\n\n Pcal_j(z,w)=\n [H_0 z+xH_1 z^2w+xyH_0 z^3w]\n       product_(b=0)^(j-3)[1+w(theta z^4)^(2^b)]\n +sum_(l=2)^(j-1)H_l w^l(theta z^4)^(2^(l-2))\n       product_(b=0)^(j-l-2)[1+w(theta z^4)^(2^(l-1+b))]. These are conditions on the ACTUAL original radius.\n\nSince beta>999/1000, (1.1) is strictly stronger than (G) on its scope.\nThe cap bounds (1.2)-(1.3) are not an unrestricted original-radius\nestimate: their remaining small-R regime is stated in Section9.\n\nThere is no claimed counterexample to (G). Section7 gives an actual\ncomplete-word counterexample ONLY to extending the balanced a-grid.\nSection8 proves that an unconstrained product of crossing gains is too\nlarge as a relaxation, and explicitly does NOT realize it as a trajectory.\n\n## 2. Legality, energies, and elementary payments\n\nFor each fixed ray the derivative has the three pieces\n\n    kappa*r       for 0<=r<=1,\n    r-v           for 1<=r<=v+B_sigma,\n    B_sigma       for r>=v+B_sigma.\n\nThe middle can have zero length. These formulas match at both knots.\nThe signed derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and piecewise has\nslopes kappa,1,0. Partitioning an interval at its knots shows that g is\n1-Lipschitz. Thus f is finite, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth.\nSince G_sigma(r)>0 for r>0, the unique minimizer is0 and f(0)=0.\nIn particular R=|s| is the original closest-minimum distance. The ray\nenergies, from integrating the displayed derivative, are\n\n    2f(sigma*r)=kappa*r^2                         (r<=1),\n                 (r-v)^2+kappa*v                 (1<=r<=u_sigma),\n                 2B_sigma*r-B_sigma^2-v*(2B_sigma-kappa)\n                                                  (r>=u_sigma).\n\nNo smaller-tail energy is replaced by a pre-cap formula.\nFor every ray and radius,\n\n    0<=G_sigma(r)<=min(r,B_sigma).                  (2.1)\n\nAt a level-k strict crossing, let y be the source radius, p its actual\nsource gradient magnitude and z the destination radius. Exactly\n\n    z=(1+d_k)*p-y <=d_k*p.                         (2.2)\n\nOn any segment with no crossing and a positive terminal, every point\nstays on one ray and its radii and gradients decrease. If its start is\nZ, its total mass is M, and its final gradient is q, then summing EVERY\nstep of this segment gives\n\n    Z>=r_end+M*q >=(1+M)*q.                        (2.3)\n\nA zero hit is treated separately because it makes the final gradient\nzero. A noncrossing complete H_j satisfies p_n<=R/A_j.\n\nAll reasoning below applies to actual updates of this fixed f. Source\nand destination gradients are not interchangeable, and no child input,\ncap, or curvature is independently selected.\n\n## 3. Quantitative masses and a sharpened physical product\n\nThe following elementary bounds hold uniformly:\n\n    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,\n    2/5<a<b<3/7<1/2,\n    beta>999/1000, cb>1.\n\nFor c, use 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and epsilon<1/1000; the upper comparison\n(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7 gives the displayed bound. The lower bound on a\nfollows from a>(999/1000)*(7/17)>2/5. The identity\nlambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda imply cb>1>beta, so a=beta/c<b.\nAlso b=c-2<3/7. These imply gamma=cb^2<153/343<1/2 and b<gamma.\n\nThe literal word masses satisfy\n\n    A_0=1, A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,\n    A_j=(1-a/b)*2^j+(a/b)*c^j.                    (3.1)\n\nThe last expression satisfies the initial condition and the recurrence\nbecause 2a/b+a=ca/b. Since0<a/b<1, it follows that\n\n    A_j>d_j/b for every j>=0,\n    A_j<c^j for j>=1,\n    d_j/A_j>=a for every j>=0.                    (3.2)\n\nThe last inequality is equality at j=0 and strict for j>=1.\n\nEvery occurrence of a level-k bridge within H_l has k<l. Its literal\nprefix and suffix masses M,N within that H_l obey\n\n    M,N>=S_k,  M+(1+d_k)+N=S_l.\n\nPut X=1+M, Y=1+N. Then\n\n    X,Y>=A_k, X+Y=A_l-d_k,\n    XY>=A_k*(A_l-d_k-A_k),                         (3.3)\n\nwhere the last inequality is (X-A_k)(Y-A_k)>=0.\n\nWe strengthen the source's product bound to\n\n    (1+M)*(1+N)>d_l*d_k/gamma.                    (3.4)\n\nTo prove it, let n=l-k>=1. Unrolling the finite mass identity\nalgebraically, without evaluating any recurrence, gives\n\n    A_l=2^n*A_k+d_k*(c^n-2^n)/b.\n\nConsequently, using A_k>d_k/b,\n\n    A_l-d_k-A_k\n       =(2^n-1)*A_k+d_k*((c^n-2^n)/b-1)\n       >d_l/b-d_k*(1+1/b)\n       >=(d_l/b)*(1-(b+1)/c)\n       =d_l/(bc).\n\nThe penultimate line uses d_k<=d_l/c; the final equality uses b+1=c-1.\nTogether with A_k>d_k/b and (3.3), this proves (3.4), including k=0.\nThis is a symbolic inequality for every physical occurrence; no balanced\ncaps, chosen crossing subset, or symmetry of trajectory masses is used.\n\nOne further mass comparison will pay exterior ancestors in Section5.\nLet a literal H_(l+1) lie inside H_j, and let E be the total mass outside\nthat occurrence. Then E=A_j-A_(l+1)>=0, and\n\n    A_l+E > A_j/c.                                (3.5)\n\nIndeed c*(A_l+E)-A_j=(c-1)A_j-c*(A_l+d_l), which is at least\n(c-1)*(2A_l+d_l)-c*(A_l+d_l)=b*A_l-d_l>0.\nAll masses outside the chosen occurrence remain in E; none is discarded.\n\n## 4. Zero or one crossing: original-radius conclusion\n\nWithout crossings, (2.3) and (3.2) give\n\n    p_n<=R/A_j <b*R/d_j <R/(2d_j).\n\nSuppose there is exactly one crossing, of level k. Let P,T be its\nENTIRE original prefix and suffix masses, and p its source gradient.\nThe prefix and suffix are noncrossing. Thus\n\n    (1+P)*p<=R,\n    (1+T)*p_n<=z<=d_k*p.\n\nApply (3.4) with containing word H_j to obtain\n\n    p_n<=d_k*R/((1+P)*(1+T))\n        <gamma*R/d_j <R/(2d_j).                    (4.1)\n\nThis argument includes both crossing directions, every source region,\nevery terminal region and every physical crossing level, including the\nleaf. Its prefix is the prefix from the original start; it is not a\nfirst-crossing price imported at a reset child.\n\n## 5. Exactly two crossings: all positions, all original ancestors\n\nAssume the positive-terminal history has exactly two crossings, at\nlevels u and v in chronological order. Let P,M,T be respectively the\nfull original mass before, between and after these two physical steps,\nand put X=1+P,Y=1+M,Z=1+T. If the source gradients are p_1,p_2, then\n(2.2)-(2.3) give\n\n    X*p_1<=R, Y*p_2<=d_u*p_1, Z*p_n<=d_v*p_2,\n    p_n/R<=d_u*d_v/(XYZ).                         (5.1)\n\nThis is a valid upper bound on the ACTUAL history. We do not assume it\nis sharp. The tree gives two exhaustive cases.\n\nChoose the smallest literal subtree H_(l+1) containing BOTH crossing\nsteps. Its center has level l. Either that center is one of the two\ncrossings, or the two crossings lie in its different children and the\ncenter does not cross. These alternatives also cover equal crossing\nlevels in disjoint children.\n\nLet E_L,E_R be the physical masses before and after this whole subtree\nin the original H_j; E=E_L+E_R=A_j-A_(l+1). These exterior portions have\nno crossings because the two selected ones are all the crossings.\nThey add to X and Z, not to the middle gap Y.\n\n### 5A. One crossing is the center of the smallest subtree\n\nFirst suppose the second crossing is the center, of level l, and the\nfirst, of level k<l, lies in its left H_l. Within that left H_l let\nX_0,Y_0 be one plus the masses before and after the level-k crossing.\nThen X_0,Y_0<=A_l, and (3.4) gives\n\n    X_0*Y_0>d_l*d_k/gamma.\n\nIn the whole original history,\n\n    X=X_0+E_L, Y=Y_0, Z=A_l+E_R.\n\nSince X_0<=A_l, multiplication of these nonnegative quantities yields\n\n    XYZ >=X_0*Y_0*(A_l+E_L+E_R)\n        > (d_l*d_k/gamma)*(A_l+E)\n        > d_l*d_k*A_j/(c*gamma).\n\nIf instead the first crossing is the center and the second is in its\nright H_l, interchange the left and right roles: the same proof uses\nX=A_l+E_L, Y=Y_0, Z=Z_0+E_R, with Y_0*Z_0>d_l*d_k/gamma and Z_0<=A_l.\nThis is a comparison of physical masses; it does not reverse the actual\ntrajectory or assume equal gradients on the two rays.\n\nCombining with (5.1) and A_j>d_j/b gives\n\n    p_n/R <c*gamma/A_j <c^2*b^3/d_j <1/(2d_j).      (5.2)\n\nFor the last comparison use\n\n    c^2*b^3 <(17/7)^2*(3/7)^3=7803/16807<1/2.\n\n### 5B. The center between the two crossings does not cross\n\nNow the first crossing is in the left H_l and the second in the right\nH_l. Let X_0,Z_0 be one plus the masses before the first and after the\nsecond, within this smallest subtree. Thus\n\n    A_u<=X_0<=A_l, A_v<=Z_0<=A_l.\n\nThe middle gap includes the entire immediate right H_u of the first\ncrossing, the level-l center, and the entire immediate left H_v of the\nsecond. These three pieces are disjoint physical pieces. Therefore\n\n    Y>=A_u+A_v+d_l>d_l.\n\nThe original gaps are X=X_0+E_L and Z=Z_0+E_R. Since X_0,Z_0<=A_l,\n\n    XZ>=X_0*Z_0*(1+E/A_l)\n       >(d_u*d_v/b^2)*(1+E/A_l).\n\nUsing d_l/A_l>=a and (3.5) then gives\n\n    XYZ >(d_u*d_v/b^2)*(d_l/A_l)*(A_l+E)\n        >a*d_u*d_v*A_j/(c*b^2)\n        >a*d_u*d_v*d_j/(c*b^3).\n\nConsequently\n\n    p_n/R <c*b^3/(a*d_j)<1/(2d_j),                (5.3)\n\nbecause\n\n    c*b^3/a <(17/7)*(3/7)^3*(5/2)=2295/4802<1/2.\n\nCases5A and5B exhaust the positions of two crossings. In particular a\nlarge noncrossing ancestor BETWEEN crossings has been paid in Y, and\nancestors BEFORE or AFTER both crossings have been paid in E_L,E_R.\nNo root-only itinerary, equal run mass, cap replacement, or independent\nchoice of a second child's input occurs. Together with Section4 and\nabsorbing zeros, this proves the scoped conclusion (1.1).\n\n## 6. Arbitrarily many crossings: quantitative last-two capture\n\nFor a crossed history ending on the larger ray, choose its last\ncrossing, at level k. Its source is on the smaller ray, with gradient\nat most K, so its landing satisfies z<=d_k*K. Its complete remaining\nsuffix has no crossings, contains the immediate right H_k, and has\nmass T>=S_k. Thus\n\n    p_n<=d_k*K/(1+T)<=d_k*K/A_k<b*K.                (6.1)\n\nThis rederives the source capture rather than adopting it as a fact.\n\nSuppose now that there are at least two crossings and the terminal is\non the smaller ray. The penultimate crossing is small-to-large, at\nlevel l; write L for its landing. Then L<=d_l*K. The last crossing is\nlarge-to-small, at level k, with source gradient p. Between these two\nconsecutive crossings all radii on the larger ray decrease.\n\nIf the last crossing is AFTER the whole immediate right H_l of the\npenultimate step, that child is noncrossing. Its endpoint gradient is\nat most L/A_l<b*K; all later larger-ray gradients up to the last source\nare no larger. The final suffix contains H_k and is noncrossing, so\n\n    p_n<=d_k*p/A_k <b*p <b^2*K <gamma*K.            (6.2)\n\nIf the last crossing is INSIDE that immediate right H_l, let M,N be\nits prefix and suffix masses inside H_l. The consecutive-crossing\nproperty gives\n\n    (1+M)*p<=L<=d_l*K.\n\nThe mass T of the whole suffix after the last crossing is at least N.\nWith z its last landing, (2.2)-(2.3) give\n\n    (1+T)*p_n<=z<=d_k*p.\n\nTherefore (3.4) applied to this actual bridge occurrence yields\n\n    p_n<=d_l*d_k*K/((1+M)*(1+N))<gamma*K.           (6.3)\n\nThe case l=0 has an empty immediate child and only the first alternative\ncan occur. Skipped ancestors and any earlier crossings remain present;\nthey do not affect the fact that these are the final two crossings.\n\nSince b<gamma, (6.1)-(6.3) prove (1.2) on either terminal ray. They also\nshow that a smaller-tail terminal cannot have two crossings: there its\ngradient would equal K, contradicting p_n<gamma*K<K. This is a\nquantitative extension of the earlier last-two-crossing tail exclusion.\n\nDividing by the ORIGINAL R proves (G) under exactly the thresholds\n(1.3), or the smaller threshold using (6.1) on the larger ray. The\ninequality p_n<gamma*K alone does not establish (G) below those thresholds.\n\n## 7. Legal counterexample to the unrestricted balanced a-grid, not (G)\n\nThe tempting induction extension\n\n    R<=d_j*Bmax implies |g(Psi_j(s))|<=a*Bmax       (7.1)\n\nis false for the present arbitrary-cap family. This does not refute the\nassignment's weaker original-radius bound (G).\n\nFor ANY enclosing m>=1536 and any 1<=j<=m, take the fixed function\n\n    kappa=1, v=0, B_-=1, B_+=A_j/d_j,\n    original signed start s=-A_j.\n\nHere B_+>1/b>1, so all cap hypotheses hold. For a chronological prefix\nwith mass U of this ACTUAL H_j, the point is exactly\n\n    x=-A_j+U.\n\nIndeed 0<=U<=S_j=A_j-1 implies x<=-1, so every queried derivative is\n-1 and the GD update advances U by the next actual step. This is an\ninduction on physical prefixes with all region guards shown, not an\nevaluated trajectory. The true terminal is x_n=-1 and p_n=1.\n\nIts original radius is R=A_j=d_j*Bmax, whereas (3.2) gives\n\n    a*Bmax=a*A_j/d_j=A_j/c^j<1=p_n.\n\nThus (7.1) fails at its q=0 boundary. Both fixed caps are retained and\nthe complete terminal is on its own cap knot. Moreover\n\n    a<K/Bmax=d_j/A_j<b,\n\nso this is precisely a permitted unbalanced cap pair, even arbitrarily\nnear the balance threshold as the parameters vary.\n\nIt is NOT a counterexample to (G): the noncrossing proof already gives\np_n< b*R/d_j<beta*R/d_j. This test shows why merely copying the old\nbalanced grid cannot decide mechanism1. It supplies no authorization\nto invoke mechanism2's weaker-global-bound alternative, which requires\nan actual counterexample to (G) itself.\n\n## 8. Why unrestricted crossing-gain products cannot close the proof\n\nFor a general list of t crossings with physical levels k_1,...,k_t and\nnoncrossing gap masses M_0,...,M_t, repeated use of (2.2)-(2.3) gives\n\n    p_n/R <= product_i d_(k_i) / product_r (1+M_r). (8.1)\n\nThis is an upper relaxation. If its right side is too large, nothing\nhas been proved about the existence of a corresponding actual history.\nSections4-5 used it only where the full physical mass bound sufficed.\n\nHere the relaxation itself can exceed the desired coefficient. Fix\nL=8 and j=m, and formally select every bridge of level at least L as a\ncrossing. Each gap is a literal H_L, with mass S_L. The coarse word has\n2^(m-L)-1 selected bridges and 2^(m-L) such gaps. Put t=m-L and\n\n    q_L=c*d_L/A_L.\n\nCounting each coarse level's physical multiplicity symbolically gives\n\n    product(selected d)/A_L^(2^t)\n       =q_L^(2^t)/(c*d_m).                         (8.2)\n\nThis is an exact formula for the RELAXATION, not an executed word or\na legal itinerary. Its derivation uses\nsum_(r=0)^(t-1) 2^(t-1-r)=2^t-1 and\nsum_(r=0)^(t-1) r*2^(t-1-r)=2^t-t-1.\n\nFor completeness it is analytically larger than beta/d_m throughout\nthe assigned m range. Expanding cb gives\n\n    1+6epsilon<cb<1+8epsilon,\n    b/a-1=(cb-beta)/beta<10epsilon.\n\nBy (3.1),\n\n    q_L=cb/[1+(b/a-1)*(2/c)^L].\n\nSince 2/c<5/6 and (5/6)^8<1/4 (equivalently\n4*390625<1679616), at L=8 this gives\n\n    q_8>(1+6epsilon)/(1+(5/2)epsilon)>1+3epsilon.\n\nThe last inequality is equivalent to\n(1/2)epsilon-(15/2)epsilon^2>0, valid here. At m=16 one has\n2^(m-8)>4m, and doubling preserves that inequality for every subsequent\ninteger m. Thus it certainly holds at m>=1536. The finite binomial\ninequality yields\n\n    q_8^(2^(m-8))>1+3epsilon*2^(m-8)>4>c*beta.\n\nInserting this in (8.2) proves failure of the proposed universal\ncombinatorial upper bound on the relaxed product. No giant integer,\ntrajectory, recurrence, or numerical table was computed.\n\nThe equality pattern of this relaxation is incompatible with the SAME\nfixed derivative. In particular, equality in a gap H_8 would require\nall its queried gradients to have a common magnitude p>0, its last\nsource radius to equal p, and its initial radius to equal A_8*p.\nThe condition G_sigma(p)=p forces kappa=1: for kappa<1 both kappa*p\nand p-(1-kappa) are strictly below p. With kappa=1, constancy over the\nnonempty radius interval from p to A_8*p forces the fixed ray cap to\nbe exactly p. Thus equality at consecutive visits to the same ray\nforces the same p.\n\nAt the first two formal coarse crossings the levels are8 and9. Equality\nwould give source magnitudes\n\n    p_2=(d_8/A_8)*p_1,\n    p_3=(d_9/A_8)*p_2=c*(d_8/A_8)^2*p_1.\n\nThe third source is back on the first ray, so the fixed cap would\nrequire p_3=p_1. But\n\n    c*(d_8/A_8)^2<c*b^2=gamma<1/2.\n\nThis is a contradiction. It does not prove that the whole formal\ncrossing pattern is impossible with strict losses, nor that all actual\npatterns satisfy (G). It shows exactly why the relaxed growing product\nis not an actual counterexample and why fixed-ray consistency cannot\nbe dropped from the unresolved proof.\n\n## 9. What remains genuinely open after these derivations\n\nMechanism1 is undecided. The direct partial results reduce its remaining\npositive-terminal histories to all the following simultaneous conditions:\n\n* at least THREE strict crossings in the actual complete H_j;\n* terminal strictly before its own cap knot;\n* if the terminal is on the larger ray,\n\n      0<R<b*d_j*K/beta and 0<p_n<b*K;\n\n  if it is on the smaller ray,\n\n      0<R<gamma*d_j*K/beta and 0<p_n<gamma*K;\n\n* one fixed derivative throughout every return to a ray, with the\n  original signed start s and enclosing m unchanged.\n\nThese conditions describe the remaining class for the NEW direct\nargument. The earlier balanced report supplies a separate JOINT\nUNVERIFIED candidate for cap ratio K/B>=b. Keeping that candidate at\nits stated status, the unresolved interface of the overall two-cap\nprogramme is the above class with K/B<b. No accepted theorem removes\nthat class, and its mere description is not a new invariant or a proof.\n\nThe small original radius here need not lie inside the common core or\nbelow the smaller cap knot: d_j can be large. Neither p_n<gamma*K nor\np_n<b*K produces the necessary original-radius decay in that class.\nThe two direct routes have concrete failures: the unrestricted a-grid\nis legally refuted by Section7, and the free alternating-run product\nis refuted AS A RELAXATION by Section8. Neither failure is a legal\ncounterexample to (G). A proof must retain and exploit the quantitative\nloss from using the same two caps and common inner derivative over\nthree or more crossings.\n\nBecause the required decisive first result was not obtained,\nmechanism2's all-cap Huber reference, objective assembly and weaker-\nglobal-bound alternative were NOT initiated. The already paid smaller-\ntail terminals do not by themselves close the remaining pre-cap class.\nNo claim of the complete scalar theorem or the full W hypothesis is\nmade. The original minimax problem remains OPEN, W_n=2*mathcal R_n.\n\n## 10. Checks, provenance, and resource closure\n\nThe fixed family is never replaced during a history. All signs, cap\nknots, kappa=1, collapsed middle pieces, zero hits, level0 empty children,\nactual crossing positions and exterior ancestor masses are accounted\nfor in the proofs above. The strict mass inequalities use the actual\nsame-m condition a<b, not the false identity cb=1.\n\nThe original-radius estimates in Sections4-5 use full original prefixes\nand suffixes. Section6 is honestly a cap-scale estimate except where\nthe explicit original-radius threshold is imposed. Section7 is an\nactual complete H_j example, but refutes only its labeled stronger\ngrid. Section8 is explicitly an unrealized relaxation. Its fixed-cap\nincompatibility argument concerns equality, not every nearby history.\n\nThis is a textual self-screen, not independent or formal verification.\nThe current TASK expressly forbids fact_submit; no acceptance, fact_id,\nor verified completion is claimed. The initial broad recall and some\ncombined reads were truncated, and the blank guidance query returned\ncount0; the operative latest guidance and relevant source sections were\nthen read directly in bounded chunks. No empty result was treated as\nproof that the shared stores lacked guidance or prior results.\n\nSkills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving,\nconstruct-counterexamples. Matlas returned constant-step, self-contracted\nand projected-gradient statements with incompatible hypotheses. Web\nfallback supplied ordinary silver-schedule background rather than this\naltered same-m interface. No external theorem was used as a premise.\nThis remained the previously examined piecewise-affine programme, so\nno new literature machinery was imported or rejected without reading.\n\nResource use: ZERO mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic replay,\nexecuted trajectories or recurrences, solvers, optimizers, benchmark DP,\nformal calls, fact_submit, installations, consultations or subagents.\nOnly permitted retrieval, text reasoning, local/shared text persistence\nand administrative readback/hashes were used. No live TASK overwrite,\nlifecycle action, outward publication, finalization, revocation,\nshutdown, old credit reuse, automatic renewal or Goal completion.\n\nReturn at the explicit one-invocation cap. {"id": "21c296f036834f2f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:03:40.435442+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Finite terminal-block improvements wash out under the Zhang–Jiang ConPD rule.\n\nDefine phi(x,y)=(-x-y+sqrt((x+y+2)^2+4(x+1)(y+1)))/2 and psi(x,y)=(3-2y+sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9)))/4 for x,y>=0. Define S_circle(0)=S_bullet(0)=0 and, for n>=1,\nS_circle(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+phi(S_circle(k),S_circle(n-k-1))+S_circle(n-k-1)],\nS_bullet(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+psi(S_circle(k),S_bullet(n-k-1))+S_bullet(n-k-1)].\nWrite B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nFor a nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}), let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t)/L.\n\nLet E be any nonempty finite set of finite-length nonnegative schedules, allowing the empty schedule. For b in E, write m_b for its length and s(b) for the sum of its entries. Define\nC_E=max_{b in E} max{0,sqrt(2s(b)+1)-sqrt(B_{m_b})}.\nConstruct a schedule by choosing b in E and an integer r>=0, setting H_0=b, and, for j=1,...,r, choosing any nonnegative schedule a_j of length k_j>=0 with s(a_j)<=S_circle(k_j) and setting\nH_j=[a_j, psi(s(a_j),s(H_{j-1})), H_{j-1}].\nIf N=m_b+sum_{j=1}^r(k_j+1), then every such schedule satisfies\nW_N(H_r)>=1/(sqrt(B_N)+C_E)^2\n=U_N^ZJ (1+C_E/sqrt(B_N))^(-2).\nMoreover B_N>=2N+1. Consequently the lower-bound ratio tends to 1 uniformly over all schedules in this construction as N tends to infinity. In particular no fixed positive relative asymptotic improvement below U_N^ZJ can be obtained solely by using a fixed finite library of improved terminal blocks while retaining the displayed ConPD rule and the primitive-block sum caps. This is a statement about that construction class, not the unrestricted minimax problem.", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "0be8b54e0a33381c", "links": {"source_id": "65e3a18284ee82c4", "predecessors": []}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "已按文本顺序核验全部五部分，未发现错误或缺口。第1部分构造的 Huber 函数满足凸性、可微性及 L-Lipschitz 梯度条件；非负步长保证全部迭代留在所用线性区间，所得 W_n(h)>=1/(2s(h)+1) 正确，包括 n=0。第2部分的拼接恒等式、单调性、平方根变量下的 1-Lipschitz 估计及 psi>=1 均成立。第3部分选取递推中的合法索引 k_j，并使用块和上界，正确证明 E_j<=B_{N_j}。第4部分将初始平方根偏差以同一常数 C_E 传递，得到所述有限步数下界。第5部分正确证明 B_N>=2N+1，并利用有限种子库给出与具体构造无关的阈值，排除该构造类中固定正比例的渐近改进。空种子、零长度前置块、r=0 和 C_E=0 均被涵盖。结论仅涉及下界比值及指定构造类，没有声称实际性能比值收敛或解决无限制 minimax 问题。陈述自包含；证明没有外部定理或 fact_id 引用，无需引用检索，也未触犯所列硬性禁令。", "critical_errors": [], "gaps": []}}
{"id": "efd820ee2e5d5663", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:10:42.602070+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Orthogonal-moment obstruction for randomized gradient-descent adversaries. Define c_0=0 and c_{k+1}=c_k+2+2 sqrt(c_k+1) for all integers k>=0, and define beta_n=4/(1+sqrt(1+2c_n))^2. Let n>=1 be an integer, L>0, and h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}) in [0,infinity)^n be any predetermined schedule with finite entries. Consider any probability distribution on legal instances in a common finite-dimensional Euclidean space: each realization consists of a convex differentiable f:R^d->R with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and an initial point x_0 with ||x_0-x_star||=1. For each realization let x_{i+1}=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i), 0<=i<n, and set g_i=grad f(x_i)/L, including g_n. Assume all data used in expectations are measurable and E<g_i,g_j>=0 whenever 0<=i<j<=n. Then\nE[2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)] <= beta_n <= 2/n^2.\nThe deterministic conclusion follows by taking a point-mass distribution: every legal trajectory whose n+1 gradients are mutually orthogonal has normalized last-iterate objective error at most beta_n. Moreover n^2 beta_n tends to 2. Consequently, for any positive comparison sequence V_n with n^2 V_n tending to infinity, every family of such randomized adversaries has expected normalized error divided by V_n tending to zero, uniformly over schedules and distributions satisfying the assumptions. This bounds the strength of the orthogonal-moment adversary class; it is not an upper bound on the unrestricted worst-case error W_n(h), and it does not resolve the project's unrestricted minimax target.", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "0e7e78056dc6258c", "links": {"source_id": "b0a9e2cb6cd019f7", "predecessors": []}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "The proof is correct. All six sections were checked in textual order. Section 1 correctly derives the smooth-convex inequality. Section 2 establishes integrability for each fixed finite schedule and handles vanishing gradient energies. Sections 3 and 4 correctly use orthogonality in expectation to obtain the displacement bounds and the second-moment inequality, without assuming samplewise orthogonality. Section 5's induction and square identity yield the stated beta_n bound. Section 6 establishes beta_n <= 2/n^2 and n^2 beta_n -> 2. The point-mass specialization and uniform comparison with every positive V_n satisfying n^2 V_n -> infinity follow from the distribution- and schedule-independent bound. The statement is self-contained; there are no external-paper or fact_id citations requiring reference checks. No hard-prohibition violations, critical errors, or gaps were found. The conclusion is restricted to the specified orthogonal-moment adversary class.", "critical_errors": [], "gaps": []}}
{"id": "3cfe38f43343bd7e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:13:46.690968+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Exact single-cut Huber lower bounds are asymptotically insufficient for the Zhang–Jiang profile.\n\nFor any integer n>=0 and h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}) in [0,infinity)^n, let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, all x_0!=x_star, and GD x_{t+1}=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). Here L>0 is fixed and f_star=f(x_star). Put s(h)=sum_{t=0}^{n-1}h_t. For 0<=j<n let P_j=sum_{t<j}h_t and T_j=sum_{t>j}h_t, and let (u)_+=max(u,0). Define\nK_n(h)=max{1/(1+2s(h)), max_{0<=j<n} (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]},\nwhere the inner maximum is omitted at n=0.\nThen W_n(h)>=K_n(h) for every such schedule. Every nonzero term indexed by j is attained as the normalized error of an explicit one-dimensional convex L-smooth instance with a minimizer.\n\nFor x,y>=0 define\nphi(x,y)=(-x-y+sqrt((x+y+2)^2+4(x+1)(y+1)))/2,\npsi(x,y)=(3-2y+sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9)))/4.\nDefine S_circle(0)=S_bullet(0)=0 and, for n>=1,\nS_circle(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+phi(S_circle(k),S_circle(n-k-1))+S_circle(n-k-1)],\nS_bullet(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+psi(S_circle(k),S_bullet(n-k-1))+S_bullet(n-k-1)].\nPut B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, and p=log_2 3.\nFor all n>=0, S_circle(n)+1<=(n+1)^p and B_n<=1+4n^p.\nFor every integer n>=2, the schedule ell^(n)_t=t+1, 0<=t<n, satisfies\nK_n(ell^(n))<=16/n^2 and\nK_n(ell^(n))/U_n^ZJ<=16(1+4n^p)/n^2 -> 0 as n->infinity.\nConsequently this particular maximum of the global Huber witness and all these single-cut witnesses cannot itself yield a uniform asymptotic lower bound (1-o(1))U_n^ZJ. This is only a limitation of K_n; it makes no small-error assertion about W_n(ell^(n)) and does not resolve the unrestricted minimax target.", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "b98bb807d514e80d", "links": {"source_id": "90e2504de7621d07", "predecessors": ["0be8b54e0a33381c"]}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "已按文本顺序核验全部论证，未发现错误或缺口。单切点实例的截断导数连续、单调且为 1-Lipschitz；前缀与后缀迭代保持在各自的恒梯度区域，最终归一化误差恰等于所声称的非零切点项，乘以 L 的缩放也正确。已读取引用事实 0be8b54e0a33381c，其证明第 1 部分确实支持全局 Huber 下界，包括零步长和空序列，且未发现 P3 链式缺陷。由 phi、psi 导出的 Omega、G 递推、根式估计及两项归纳上界均正确。线性步长按 j 是否小于 n/2 分段的估计对所有 n>=2 成立；由于 1<p=log_2(3)<2，所给比值上界趋于零。结论准确限定为 K_n 的不足，没有据此推断实际 W_n 的小误差或解决无限制 minimax 问题。陈述自包含，无须核查的外部论文引用，也未发现其他硬性禁令违规。", "critical_errors": [], "gaps": []}}
{"id": "9acba22314e61e60", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:32:17.721169+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Complex-polynomial transcripts, an explicit legal spectral lens, and a quantitative limit of that lens.\n\nFor each integer n>=1 and each real nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}), define Poly_0(z)=1 and Poly_i(z)=product_{t=0}^{i-1}(1-h_t z) for 1<=i<=n. Identify the complex plane with the real Euclidean plane, with inner product <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). For z=alpha+i beta set xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z Poly_i(z) for 0<=i<=n, and xi_*=gamma_*=0. Put kappa=alpha-|z|^2 and I_n={*,0,...,n}. For each ordered pair define\nq_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2,\nregarded as the weight on the directed edge j->i. For each directed simple cycle C in I_n define\nArea_C=sum_{j->i in C} Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i),\nEnergy_C=sum_{j->i in C}|xi_i-xi_j|^2.\nDefine D_h to be the set of z satisfying kappa Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C| for every such cycle.\n\n(a) The data (xi_i,gamma_i), including the optimum sample *, are interpolable by a finite everywhere-defined convex differentiable function f:R^2->R with 1-Lipschitz gradient and grad f(0)=0 if and only if z belongs to D_h. For z in D_h let ell_i be the largest q-weight of a directed path i->*, allowing the empty path at *, and put v_i=-ell_i. The maxima exist over simple paths and ell_*=0. There is such an f with f(xi_i)=v_i for every i. These values are simultaneously maximal among all interpolating f satisfying f(0)=0. Its exact GD recursion for h starts at xi_0=1, and its normalized terminal error is\nGap_h(z):=-2ell_n.\nIn particular\n|z|^2|Poly_n(z)|^2 <= Gap_h(z) <= (2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2.\n\n(b) The parameter lens\nLens_n={alpha+i beta: alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))}\nis contained in D_h for every h.\n\n(c) Let Cheb_j be the polynomials defined by Cheb_0(x)=1, Cheb_1(x)=x and Cheb_{j+1}(x)=2xCheb_j(x)-Cheb_{j-1}(x). For every n>=2 define the positive schedule Chi_n, in any permutation, by the n entries\nsec^2((2k-1)pi/(2(2n+1))), k=1,...,n.\nIts terminal polynomial is (-1)^n Cheb_{2n+1}(sqrt(z))/((2n+1)sqrt(z)), interpreted as a polynomial including z=0, and its sum is s(Chi_n)=2n(n+1)/3. For all z in Lens_n,\nGap_{Chi_n}(z) <= 2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2.\n\nFor an arbitrary schedule of length n>=1 define\nV_lens(h)=max{sup_{z in Lens_n} Gap_h(z), 1/(2s(h)+1)}.\nLet W_n(h) be the project worst-case normalized objective error, namely the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite dimension, convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, using x_{t+1}=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). Here L>0 is fixed and s(h)=sum h_t. Then V_lens(h)<=W_n(h). For the exact benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/(2S_bullet(n)+1) defined by the recursions in predecessor b98bb807d514e80d, and p=log_2 3 from that predecessor, the explicit schedules satisfy\n0<=V_lens(Chi_n)/U_n^ZJ\n <= 2cosh^2(2)(1+4n^p)/n^2 ->0.\nThus the universal-lens witnesses, even augmented by the canonical sum-based Huber witness, cannot prove the required uniform matching lower bound. This is a limitation of that witness family only, not an upper bound on W_n(Chi_n) and not a decision of the unrestricted minimax target.", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "dfde2fa326c908bf", "links": {"source_id": "87a12b76b7bec957", "predecessors": ["b98bb807d514e80d"]}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "Verified all eight proof sections in textual order. {"id": "38ff2d6af43ba18d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:38:06.046896+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Exact alternating-ray multi-cut hard functions with retained remote offsets and function-value areas.\n\nFix any integer n>=0, L>0, and any predetermined finite nonnegative schedule h of length n for gradient descent x_{t+1}=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). Write W_n(h) for the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star.\n\nChoose k checkpoint steps, where 0<=k<=n, and write h in its exact chronological order as (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,b_{k-1},block_k). Each block_i is a possibly empty list of nonnegative steps; s_i is its sum. Each b_i>=0 is the intervening checkpoint.\n\nFor k>=1 set m_0=1/(1+s_0) and O_0=0. For i=0,...,k-2 define\nm_{i+1}=((b_i-1)m_i-O_i)/(1+s_{i+1}), O_{i+1}=O_i+s_i m_i.\nRequire m_i>0 for 0<=i<k and m_i>=m_{i-2} for 2<=i<k.\nDefine z_term=(b_{k-1}-1)m_{k-1}-O_{k-1}, and define m_old=0 if k=1 and m_old=m_{k-2} if k>=2. Require z_term>=(1+s_k)m_old.\nDefine e_term=sum_{0<=i<k, i congruent k mod 2}s_i m_i^2.\nChoose any q in the closed interval [m_old,z_term/(1+s_k)].\n\nThen an explicitly constructed one-dimensional convex differentiable function f with L-Lipschitz derivative, f(0)=0=min f, and initial point x_0=1 realizes the full supplied GD schedule and has exactly\n2 f(x_n)/L = 2e_term+2q z_term-(2s_k+1)q^2.\nConsequently this expression is a lower bound for W_n(h). The largest value within the stated q interval is attained at\nq_opt=max{m_old,z_term/(2s_k+1)}.\n\nFor k=0, use z_term=1, e_term=0, m_old=0, s_k=s_0 and q in [0,1/(1+s_0)], without defining any m_i or O_i. The same construction and payoff formula apply and their maximum is 1/(1+2s_0). The empty horizon n=0 is included.\nFor k=1 the feasibility condition is exactly b_0>=1, and the optimized payoff is (b_0-1)^2/[(1+s_0)^2(1+2s_1)].\n\nDefine F_ray_n(h) as the maximum of the optimized displayed payoff over all feasible checkpoint choices, including k=0. This is a finite maximum because h is finite. Then\nK_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h)<=W_n(h),\nwhere K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2sum_t h_t), max_{0<=j<n}(h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_{t<j}h_t)^2(1+2sum_{t>j}h_t)]} is the lower-bound functional established in predecessor b98bb807d514e80d, with its inner maximum omitted at n=0 and (u)_+=max(u,0).\nFor h=(1,5,1,9,1), the checkpoint choice (5,9) has exact optimized payoff77/4, whereas K_5(h)=1/3.\nNo uniform comparison between F_ray_n and the Zhang–Jiang profile is asserted.", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "1fca3e5dd555cb4a", "links": {"source_id": "1ba418a0f41e7ef5", "predecessors": ["b98bb807d514e80d"]}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "Verified the proof sequentially. The offset recurrence and same-ray monotonicity conditions permit every extension while preserving previously constructed data. Nonnegative block steps and the feasible terminal interval keep all iterates on their prescribed plateaus. The completed derivative is globally continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz. The retained ramp and plateau areas give the claimed exact objective; the constrained quadratic optimizer, k=0 and k=1 cases, empty horizon, zero steps, and scaling to arbitrary L>0 are valid. The example gives 77/4 and K_5=1/3 as stated. The finite maximum therefore satisfies K_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n. The cited fact b98bb807d514e80d was inspected: its K_n definition agrees and its statement contains no prohibited conditional premise. No external theorem is invoked, and no hard prohibition is violated. The isolated v_j in (10) and Z in the final area display are unambiguous notational slips for m_j and z_term, respectively; the surrounding explicit area calculations establish the intended identities without a mathematical gap.", "critical_errors": [], "gaps": []}}
