{"id": "5480845b8a751e16", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:53:45.741128+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Exact unqueried-terminal reduction and full-certificate re-selection for arbitrary nonnegative gradient-descent schedules.\n\nFor every integer n>=0, every L>0, and every finite h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) in [0,infinity)^n, let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). The empty schedule n=0 is allowed.\n\nFor the following finite formulations use normalized smoothness1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, normalized sampled values f_i, formal gradients g_i, and x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. Put I_n={star,0,...,n}, J_pre={star,0,...,n-1}, and Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 for distinct i,j in I_n. When n=0, J_pre={star}.\n\n(a) The finite Gram maximization of 2f_n subject to G_pr>=0, G_pr_(x0,x0)<=1, all Q_ij<=0 with distinct i,j in J_pre, and all Q_(n,j)<=0 with j in J_pre, has attained optimal value W_n(h). Here G_pr is the full Gram matrix of the n+2 formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with no rank constraint. No incoming terminal inequality Q_(j,n)<=0 is imposed. For any feasible prefix jets in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space, define cjet_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j, H_j(v)=cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2, H(v)=max_(j in J_pre)H_j(v), and F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)). Then F is an everywhere finite convex differentiable 1-smooth function interpolating all prefix jets, F(0)=0=min F, and it is the pointwise maximal such prefix interpolant. For every reduced-feasible terminal pair (g_n,f_n), F(x_n)>=f_n. At an optimal reduced transcript, F(x_n)=f_n and grad F(x_n)=g_n; hence every optimal reduced transcript is fully interpolable and obeys the deleted incoming terminal inequalities.\n\n(b) There exist nonnegative multipliers lambda_ij on all distinct pairs in I_n, with lambda_(j,n)=0 for every j in J_pre, finite-node flow sum_j lambda_ij-sum_j lambda_ji=2*1_(i=n), and a FULL positive semidefinite quadratic certificate\nM_cert=W_n(h)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\nafter the GD substitution. This certificate is PSD on the original formal space (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including every gradient at a zero step, without imposing equality between duplicated formal gradients. Thus some tight full certificate has no incoming edge at the unqueried terminal sample for every nonnegative schedule. For schedules with positive steps this follows from an explicit strict primal and a separation argument. If there are zero steps, choose the original index of each positive step as the representative of its compressed query and the original index n as the terminal representative; embedding a certificate for the positive-step compression gives the asserted full-space certificate. The all-zero and empty schedules have W_n(h)=1 and the direct certificate lambda_(n,star)=2, all other lambda zero, with M_cert=||x_0-g_n||^2.\n\n(c) For any real certificate level q_cert and any h as above, certificates with no incoming terminal multipliers are parameterized exactly as follows. Choose alpha_j>=0 for j in J_pre, with sum_j alpha_j=1, and nonnegative prefix multipliers lambda^pre_ij for distinct i,j in J_pre whose finite prefix flow is out_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i for 0<=i<n, where out_i^pre=sum_(j in J_pre,j!=i)lambda^pre_ij and in_i^pre=sum_(j in J_pre,j!=i)lambda^pre_ji. Define y_j=x_j-g_j, delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2, V_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j y_j, with y_star=delta_star=0, and\nR_alpha=q_cert||x_0||^2-2sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j delta_j-||V_alpha||^2+sum_(i!=j in J_pre)lambda^pre_ij Q_ij.\nThe prefix flow cancels all f_i in R_alpha. Set lambda_(n,j)=2alpha_j, all incoming terminal multipliers zero, and retain lambda^pre. The complete identity is\nM_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha.\nConsequently full PSD of M_cert is equivalent to PSD of the entire prefix quadratic R_alpha in (x_0,g_0,...,g_(n-1)). For n>=1, with w_(-1)=alpha_star and w_t=alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j, one has 0<=w_(-1)<=w_0<=...<=w_(n-1)=1 and\nV_alpha=alpha_star x_0-sum_(t<n)[h_t w_t-(w_t-w_(t-1))]g_t.\nFor n=0, alpha_star=1, V_alpha=x_0, and R_alpha=(q_cert-1)||x_0||^2. Any actual normalized trajectory attaining a feasible q_cert has g_n=V_alpha, R_alpha=0, and multiplier complementarity.\n\nThis result deletes no interpolation inequalities inside the queried prefix. It asserts existence of a selected optimal certificate, not that every optimal certificate has the selected support. {"id": "23f6ee1a9002183c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T03:14:04.821293+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Uniform enclosure of the original Zhang--Jiang denominator rays and a conditional full-certificate four-step seed lift.\n\nFor a nonnegative integer n and a finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite dimensions, all convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and at least one minimizer x_star, and all x_0!=x_star, with x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Set C_0=B_0=1 and, for every integer n>=1, C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)) and B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)). For every positive integer b,\n\n    lim_(j->infinity) B_(5(b*2^j-1)) W_(5(b*2^j-1))(Hsub_(b*2^j-1)) = Z_(5b)/(A Z_b).\n\nIn particular, for EVERY integer j>=25, with n_j=3*2^j-1 and N_j=5n_j=15*2^j-5,\n\n    W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-1/50000) U_(N_j)^ZJ.\n\nThis theorem does not assert existence of the four-step word and multipliers in its hypothesis, does not assert their impossibility, and does not resolve the original unrestricted asymptotic minimax question.\n", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "04219554e41d9750", "links": {"source_id": "a030cb8fa1095928", "predecessors": ["0be8b54e0a33381c", "b98bb807d514e80d", "1252b8823bb5a290"]}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "已按第1–13节顺序核查，未发现数学错误或证明缺口。三条内部事实的引用内容及条件均匹配，未发现继承的未证前提。外部对称递推结果在定理检索未命中后，已与 [arXiv 原文推论4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1) 的核函数、初值和叶数索引核对一致。重新执行第8节完整整数程序，全部4096个基础不等式及所有整数端点断言通过。误差累积、射线极限与固定偏移、种子矩阵恒等式、两种完整空间拼接恒等式、替换后的步数与质量界、Huber取等，以及每个 j≥25 的严格改进均成立。四步种子及其乘子的存在仍是明确假设；证明未将其视为已经构造，也未据此宣称解决无约束渐近极小极大问题。", "critical_errors": [], "gaps": []}}

{"id": "6b60716216f869d6", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T21:15:11.981492+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Exact fixed-b=3 benchmark thresholds and conditional full-certificate amplification for five- and six-step seeds.\n\nFor a nonnegative integer n and a finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), let W_n(h) be the supremum of\n\n    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)\n\nover all finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, all convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and at least one minimizer x_star, and all x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here L>0 is arbitrary and fixed; the empty schedule is allowed. This is the W=2R normalization, where R is the same supremum without the factor2.\n\nFor nonnegative real a,d define\n\n    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,\n    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.\n\nRetain the ORIGINAL ALL-split benchmark recurrences\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),  integer n>=1,\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2).\n\nEvery split index k is an integer. For every positive integer b let Z_b be the positive finite limit lim_(j->infinity)lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1), established in predecessor04219554e41d9750. Define\n\n    Gamma5_c107=7977607/806000,\n    Gamma6_c107=387153999/32240000,\n\nand let Gamma_r_c107 denote Gamma5_c107 for r=5 and Gamma6_c107 for r=6. With the denominator ray FIXED at b=3, the strict comparisons are\n\n    Z_18/Z_3 < Gamma5_c107 < 99/10 < 292666/29275,\n    Z_21/Z_3 < Gamma6_c107 < 1201/100 < 90/7.\n\nThe following assertions are conditional. Fix r in {5,6}. Suppose h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)) is an ACTUAL nonnegative seed word with s=sum_(i=0)^(r-1)h_i and capacity A=1+s>Gamma_r_c107. Suppose there is a full real (r+1)-by-(r+1) array Lambda with Lambda_ii=0 and Lambda_ij>=0 for EVERY ordered distinct i,j in {0,...,r}. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1; let the real matrix T have T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i. Set\n\n    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).\n\nRequire\n\n    L_flow^T e=omega,\n    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2\n        positive semidefinite on the entire R^(r+1).\n\nHere diag of a column is the diagonal matrix with that column, and a superscript T on a matrix or column means transpose. Thus r=5 requires all30 ordered multipliers and all6 formal gradients, and r=6 requires all42 multipliers and all7 formal gradients. A zero physical step permits neither deletion nor equality of formal gradient coordinates. No rank, support, symmetry, or zero-remainder restriction is imposed.\n\nFor each nonnegative integer n choose ANY fully expanded maximizing tree of the original B_n recurrence, expanding every C child by the original C recurrence. Replace each of its n primitive empty leaves by the actual word h, retain every internal node, and leave its one terminal dominant empty leaf EMPTY with mass1. Recompute each physical bridge as follows. A primitive join, with actual child capacities A_left=1+sum(left) and A_right=1+sum(right), uses the unique positive d_pp solving d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left*A_right and concatenates (left,1+d_pp,right). A dominant join, with primitive left capacity A_left and actual dominant right mass D_right=1+2sum(right), uses zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2 and concatenates (left,1+A_left/zeta,right). Write Hsub_n for this actual physical word and Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n for its mass.\n\nFor every n>=0 the accepted full substitution identities give\n\n    length(Hsub_n)=(r+1)n,\n    W_((r+1)n)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,\n    Dsub_n>=A B_n(1-ell_n)^2,\n    ell_n=(1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_n).\n\nThe equality is an all-function objective upper certificate with a matching one-dimensional Huber lower witness. For every nonnegative integer j put\n\n    n_j=3*2^j-1,\n    N_j=(r+1)n_j=3(r+1)2^j-(r+1),\n    q107_j=(2/3)^j,\n    epsilon_c107=1-Gamma_r_c107/A in (0,1).\n\nAt these TRUE physical horizons,\n\n    lim_(j->infinity) B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))\n        =Z_(3(r+1))/(A Z_3)<1.\n\nFor every j with q107_j<1/3 one has the finite bound\n\n    B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))\n        < (Gamma_r_c107/A)/(1-3q107_j).\n\nIn particular, for EVERY integer\n\n    j>=ceil(log(6/epsilon_c107)/log(3/2)),\n\nwhere log is the natural logarithm,\n\n    W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-epsilon_c107/2) U_(N_j)^ZJ.\n\nEach of the following separate sufficient hypotheses gives the uniform strict bound W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-1/16000)U_(N_j)^ZJ for EVERY integer j>=27:\n\n    r=5 and A>=99/10, with N_j=18*2^j-6;\n    r=6 and A>=1201/100, with N_j=21*2^j-7.\n\nThis theorem supplies sufficient upper enclosures for this fixed ray and is conditional on an actual full seed. {"id": "8d9df029b8d549d4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T05:39:02.285517+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Finite multiplicative phase covers and conditional full-certificate amplification for actual primitive seeds.\n\nAll vector spaces and inner products are finite-dimensional real Euclidean ones. Fix L>0. For every nonnegative integer n and every nonnegative schedule v=(v_0,...,v_(n-1)), let W_n(v) be the supremum of\n    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)\nover all convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, all minimizers x_star of f, and all x_0!=x_star, where x_(i+1)=x_i-(v_i/L)grad f(x_i) for 0<=i<n. The supremum ranges over every finite dimension. The empty schedule is included. Write R_n(v) for the same supremum without the factor 2, so W_n=2R_n.\n\nFor nonnegative real x,y define\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\nDefine the original ALL-split benchmark by C_0=B_0=1 and, for every integer n>=1,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),\nwhere every split k is an integer. Put U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, lambda=1+sqrt(2), and rho=log_2(lambda). For every positive integer b, let Z_b be the finite positive limit lim_(j->infinity)lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1), whose existence is part of predecessor 04219554e41d9750. All logarithms denoted log are natural logarithms.\n\nFix an integer r>=1 and an ACTUAL nonnegative seed word h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)). Put s=sum_(i=0)^(r-1)h_i, A=1+s, and m=r+1. Suppose there is an entire real (r+1)-by-(r+1) matrix Lambda satisfying Lambda_ii=0 and Lambda_ij>=0 for EVERY ordered distinct i,j in {0,...,r}. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1. Define T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i, and set\n    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)),\n    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.\nHere diag of a column is the diagonal matrix with that column; a superscript T means transpose. Require the EXACT flow equation L_flow^T e=omega and positive semidefiniteness of M_prim on the ENTIRE R^(r+1). Thus all r(r+1) ordered signs and all r+1 formal gradients are retained, including gradients at zero physical steps, with no coordinate deletion or imposed gradient equality. No symmetry, support, rank, zero-remainder, product-balance, or h_i>=1 condition is imposed. Nonzero and singular positive semidefinite remainders are allowed.\n\nFor each integer n>=0 choose any fully expanded maximizing tree of the original B_n recurrence, expanding every primitive C child by the original C recurrence. It has n internal nodes, n primitive empty leaves, and one dominant empty leaf. Replace each primitive empty leaf by the ACTUAL word h, leave the dominant empty leaf EMPTY with mass 1, and keep all internal nodes. Recompute every physical bridge and concatenate (left,bridge,right): a primitive join with actual child capacities A_left=1+sum(left) and A_right=1+sum(right) uses 1+d_pp, where d_pp>0 uniquely solves d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left A_right; a dominant join with primitive left capacity A_left and right dominant mass D_right=1+2sum(right) uses 1+A_left/zeta, where zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2. Denote the resulting physical word by Hsub_n and its actual mass by Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n. Ties may be fixed deterministically by the least maximizing split at every node.\n\nThe following conclusions hold jointly.\n\n(1) The physical substitution has length(Hsub_n)=mn and the full-function equality\n    W_(mn)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,\n    (sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1))^2<=Dsub_n<=A B_n.\nThe equality combines an objective upper certificate valid for the complete function class with a matching one-dimensional Huber example. For positive integers b<=c one has Z_b<=Z_c; for every integer ell>=0, Z_(2^ell b)=lambda^ell Z_b. For b>=1 and j>=0 define\n    L_pc(b,j)=lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1),\n    U_pc(b,j)=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b*2^j))+3/100)^2,\n    E(b,j)=8sqrt(2)b^(rho-1)2^(-j)+(3/25)(b+1)^(rho/2)lambda^(-j/2)+(9/10000)lambda^(-j).\nThen L_pc(b,j)<=Z_b<=U_pc(b,j) and 0<=U_pc(b,j)-L_pc(b,j)<=E(b,j). For every integer d_shift>=0 with b*2^j-1-d_shift>=0,\n    0<=Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1-d_shift)\n      <=E(b,j)+8sqrt(2)d_shift b^(rho-1)2^(-j).\n\n(2) General anchored finite cover. Choose positive integers b_pc0,d,K, an integer ell>=0, and a positive real C_pc with Z_d/Z_(b_pc0)<=C_pc. Suppose\n    m^K b_pc0<=2^ell d,\n    A^K>P_pc, where P_pc=lambda^ell C_pc.\nFor 0<=i<=K put b_pc_i=b_pc0 m^i. The exact finite product satisfies\n    1<=product_(i=0)^(K-1) Z_(m b_pc_i)/Z_(b_pc_i)\n      =Z_(m^K b_pc0)/Z_(b_pc0)<=P_pc.\nIn particular P_pc>=1. Choose any real certified capacity floor a and positive gamma with\n    1<a<=A, a^K>P_pc, K gamma<=1-P_pc/a^K.\nThese strict cover assumptions admit such choices with a and gamma rational. The symbol a in this theorem denotes this local capacity floor. For 0<=i<K define q_pc_i(a)=Z_(m b_pc_i)/(a Z_(b_pc_i)). Some fixed i_star in this finite list satisfies\n    Z_(m b_pc_i_star)/(A Z_(b_pc_i_star))<=q_pc_i_star(a)<=1-gamma.\n\n(3) Exact physical rays, uniform limits, and certified selection. For 0<=i<K and integers j>=0 put\n    n_(i,j)=b_pc_i 2^j-1,\n    N_(i,j)=m n_(i,j)=m b_pc_i 2^j-m,\n    H_(i,j)=Hsub_(n_(i,j)).\nThe true physical offset is N_(i,j)=m b_pc_i 2^j-1-(m-1). One has\n    0<=A Z_(b_pc_i)-lambda^(-j)Dsub_(n_(i,j))\n      <=A E(b_pc_i,j)+2(sqrt(A)-1)sqrt(A Z_(b_pc_i))lambda^(-j/2),\n    lim_(j->infinity)B_(N_(i,j)) W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))\n      =Z_(m b_pc_i)/(A Z_(b_pc_i)).\nThe convergence is uniform over the finite phase list and over all maximizing-tree ties.\n\nAt stage t>=0, define the certified ratio upper bound\n    F_pc_i(t)=U_pc(m b_pc_i,t)/(a L_pc(b_pc_i,t)).\nThe rule that takes the first integer stage t at which some F_pc_i(t)<1-gamma/2 and then freezes the smallest successful index i terminates after finitely many stages. It does not require finding a minimizing phase. When a and gamma are supplied as certified rational inputs, there is an entirely rational outward-enclosure version: at each integer stage t>=1, enclose each L_pc(b_pc_i,t) and U_pc(m b_pc_i,t) with absolute error at most 2^(-t), using a positive lower endpoint for L_pc and an upper endpoint for U_pc, and accept only upon the resulting strict rational test. This version also terminates and its chosen index has the same conclusion. For an arbitrary real seed, existence of rational a,gamma is asserted, but their effective certification must be part of the supplied input; no capacity oracle is presumed.\n\nSet b_pc_max=b_pc_(K-1) and\n    J=ceil(log(60 b_pc_max/gamma)/log(3/2)).\nEquivalently J is the least nonnegative integer satisfying (3/2)^J>=60 b_pc_max/gamma. For the one fixed index i selected by either certified rule, EVERY integer j>=J satisfies\n    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-gamma/4) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.\nThis includes the equality case in the integer test defining J. The horizons N_(i,j) form a strictly increasing unbounded integer sequence. Search stage t and output depth j are distinct; no bound t<=J or computational efficiency is asserted. The phase selection and all schedules depend only on the seed and benchmark, before any objective or trajectory is observed.\n\n(4) Unanchored equivalence. With b_pc0=d=1 and C_pc=1, the existence of integers K>=1, ell>=0 with m^K<=2^ell and A^K>lambda^ell is equivalent to A>m^rho. Hence A>(r+1)^rho is a sufficient seed-capacity interface for the conclusion in (3). For a general certified comparison Z_d/Z_(b_pc0)<=C_pc, the anchored sufficient capacity is A>(lambda^ell C_pc)^(1/K) whenever m^K b_pc0<=2^ell d. At K=1, ell=0, b_pc0=3 and d=3m, the accepted choices C_pc=Gamma5_c107=7977607/806000 for r=5 and C_pc=Gamma6_c107=387153999/32240000 for r=6 recover the sufficient capacity conditions of predecessor 4c92d3fc4eb6153e; that predecessor's specialized depth bounds retain their own proved scope.\n\n(5) Three exact rational corollaries, each conditional on the entire seed hypothesis stated above:\n\nIf r=5 and A>=49/5, use m=6, a=49/5, K=17, ell=44, b_pc0=d=C_pc=1 and gamma=1/10000. There is one certified selected index i in {0,...,16}, with b_pc_i=6^i and N_(i,j)=6^(i+1)2^j-6, such that for EVERY\n    j>=J5=ceil(log(600000*6^16)/log(3/2)),\n    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/40000) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.\n\nIf r=6 and A>=12, use m=7, a=12, K=11, ell=31, b_pc0=d=C_pc=1 and gamma=1/20000. There is one certified selected index i in {0,...,10}, with b_pc_i=7^i and N_(i,j)=7^(i+1)2^j-7, such that for EVERY\n    j>=J6=ceil(log(1200000*7^10)/log(3/2)),\n    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/80000) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.\n\nIf r=5 and A>=197/20, use m=6, a=197/20, K=9, ell=21, b_pc0=3, d=15, C_pc=19299/2500 and gamma=1/2000. The accepted strict comparison Z_15/Z_3<C_pc supplies the anchor. There is one certified selected index i in {0,...,8}, with b_pc_i=3*6^i and N_(i,j)=18*6^i*2^j-6, such that for EVERY\n    j>=J5a=ceil(log(360000*6^8)/log(3/2)),\n    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/8000) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.\nAt this same K=9, the least unanchored exponent with 6^9<=2^ell is ell=24, whereas lambda^21 C_pc<lambda^24. This compares these finite product bounds only, without claiming phase optimality. The sufficient floors 49/5, 12, and 197/20 improve the respective earlier convenient floors 99/10, 1201/100, and 99/10 by 1/10, 1/100, and 1/20.\n\nThis theorem is conditional. It supplies no actual seed or multiplier array and does not settle the unrestricted exact-profile problem. It asserts no arbitrary-template inflation, exponent-based finite capacity ceiling, real-base phase interpolation, global phase optimum, all-horizon affirmative result, or anytime schedule. {"id": "35035d35d2000737", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T07:54:22.513446+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Strict local maximality of full primitive-certificate capacity at the balanced seven-step word.\n\nAll vectors and matrices are real. Put q58=sqrt(2), lambda=1+q58, u=q58-1=1/lambda,\n    hbar=(q58,2,q58,1+lambda,q58,2,q58), Abar=lambda^3.\nIndices of the seven physical steps run from0 to6; indices of coefficient arrays run from0 to7. Let e be the all-ones column in R^8. For h=(h0,...,h6) in[0,infinity)^7 set s=sum_(i=0)^6 h_i and A(h)=1+s. For a real zero-diagonal8-by8 array X set L(X)=diag(Xe)-X. Define the8-by8 matrix T(h) by T(h)_ii=1, T(h)_ij=h_j for j<i, and T(h)_ij=0 for j>i. For a square real matrix K let sym(K)=(K+K^T)/2. Set\n    omega(h)=(-2h0,...,-2h6,2s)^T,\n    D(h)=diag(h0(2-h0),...,h6(2-h6),-s(s+2)),\n    M(h,Lambda)=D(h)+sym(L(Lambda)T(h)).\nCall (h,Lambda) feasible if Lambda is a real8-by8 array with Lambda_ii=0, Lambda_ij>=0 for every one of the56 ordered distinct pairs, L(Lambda)^T e=omega(h), and M(h,Lambda) is positive semidefinite on the entire R^8. No symmetry, support, rank, zero-remainder, or equality between formal gradient coordinates is assumed. Nonzero singular positive semidefinite remainders are included.\n\nDefine Lambdabar by its following twenty entries, with every other entry equal to zero:\n    01:2; 10:2lambda; 13:2lambda;\n    21:2lambda*q58; 23:2lambda^2+2lambda*q58; 31:2lambda;\n    32:2lambda^3; 37:2lambda^2; 43:2lambda*q58;\n    45:2lambda^2; 47:2lambda*q58; 53:4lambda;\n    54:2lambda^3; 57:2lambda^3+4lambda; 63:2lambda*q58;\n    65:2lambda^3*q58; 67:2lambda^4+2lambda^3*q58+2lambda*q58;\n    73:2lambda; 75:2lambda^3; 76:2lambda^5.\nHere ij denotes row i, column j. Let E58 be these twenty ordered pairs and Z0 the other36 ordered distinct pairs. The pair(hbar,Lambdabar) is feasible and M(hbar,Lambdabar)=0.\n\nFor p in R^7 and a real zero-diagonal8-by8 array Y, let S=sum_(i=0)^6 p_i, let Tp have entry p_j for j<i and zero elsewhere, and put\n    op=(-2p0,...,-2p6,2S)^T,\n    D1(p)=diag(2(1-hbar_i)p_i for i<7,-2Abar*S),\n    W(p,Y)=D1(p)+sym(L(Lambdabar)Tp+L(Y)T(hbar)),\n    f(p,Y)=L(Y)^T e-op.\nThe symbol f(p,Y) denotes this vector-valued flow defect, distinct from scalar objective values in the proof. Define\n    C58={(p,Y): f(p,Y)=0, Y_ij>=0 for ij in Z0, W(p,Y) is positive semidefinite on R^8}.\nThe entries of Y on E58 and all components of p are unrestricted real numbers in this definition. Use the Euclidean norm on physical vectors and the Frobenius norm on arrays, and write\n    ||(p,Y)||_* = sqrt(||p||_2^2+||Y||_F^2).\n\nThe following intermediate conclusions hold jointly. Every(p,Y) in C58 has S<=0, and S=0 in C58 holds if and only if p=0 and Y=0. Consequently Lambdabar is the unique feasible coefficient array at h=hbar. Every feasible pair with h in[1,4]^7 satisfies sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij<=2^24. For every sequence of feasible pairs(h_k,Lambda_k) with h_k tending to hbar, the entire sequence Lambda_k tends to Lambdabar; thus this convergence holds uniformly over arbitrary choices of feasible arrays.\n\nFor arbitrary displacements p,Y, the exact full-matrix expansion is\n    M(hbar+p,Lambdabar+Y)=W(p,Y)+R2(p,Y),\n    R2(p,Y)=diag(-p0^2,...,-p6^2,-S^2)+sym(L(Y)Tp),\n    ||R2(p,Y)||_F<=13||(p,Y)||_*^2.\nDefine K58={(p,Y) in C58:||(p,Y)||_*=1}. This set is nonempty and compact, and\n    m58=min_((p,Y) in K58)(-sum_(i=0)^6 p_i)>0.\n\nWith c=m58/2, there exists epsilon>0 such that for EVERY h in[0,infinity)^7 and EVERY full feasible array Lambda at h, the condition ||h-hbar||_2<epsilon implies\n    A(h)<=lambda^3-c*sqrt(||h-hbar||_2^2+||Lambda-Lambdabar||_F^2).\nIn particular, within this physical neighborhood capacity equals lambda^3 if and only if(h,Lambda)=(hbar,Lambdabar), and every such feasible array satisfies\n    ||Lambda-Lambdabar||_F<=(sqrt(7)/c)||h-hbar||_2.\nOnly h is required epsilon-close in the hypothesis; there is no hypothesis restricting coefficient displacement or selecting a particular certificate. The coefficient estimate is relative to the base, with no assertion of pairwise Lipschitz selection or uniqueness away from the base. No numerical neighborhood radius, global capacity ceiling, or local/global gradient-descent objective minimax optimality is asserted. The original all-word exact-profile problem is not resolved by this local certificate theorem.\n", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "a23356adcbb96c7e", "links": {"source_id": "623924e1b987677e", "predecessors": ["04219554e41d9750"]}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "The joint statement is correct. {"id": "155c16fe70addc6e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T10:06:39.610115+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "verification", "claim": "A static smooth-convex lower witness with an eventual strict benchmark margin for the prescribed original ruler family.\n\nAll vector spaces, norms and inner products below are finite-dimensional real Euclidean ones. Log denotes the natural logarithm. Set lambda=1+sqrt(2). For each integer m>=6 put beta=1-1/m, c=lambda*(1+1/m), a=beta/c, and define H_0=() and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j) for integers 0<=j<m. Every child uses these same enclosing-m parameters. Write n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j and b_j=1+a*c^j. The step word under consideration is exactly H_m.\n\nFor nonnegative real x,y define Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2 and G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8*x*y)/2. Retain the original ALL-split benchmark C_0=B_0=1 and, for every integer n>=1, C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)) and B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)), with each split index r an integer. Put U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Let Z_1=lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m, whose existence, positivity and finiteness are unconditional conclusions of predecessor fact 04219554e41d9750.\n\nUse exactly K=14/5, E=1/100, kappa=K/m, epsilon=E/m and the following costs:\n\n    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),\n    chi=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp), d=1-kappa^2,\n    T=(exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d,\n    H=chi*[1+(1+kappa)*T]-1,\n    A=epsilon+(H+1)/epsilon,\n    R_cut=max{A/epsilon,1+2/epsilon^2},\n    J=max{1,ceil(log(R_cut/a)/log c)}.\n\nThe unsubscripted H in this display is a scalar allowance, distinct from the words H_j. If J<m, let k=m-J and select only the first occurrences of levels J,J+1,...,m-1, at physical zero-based step indices 2^(J+i)-1 for 0<=i<k. Their values are b^(i)=b_(J+i). Set s_0=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-1) for 1<=i<k, and s_k=S_(m-1). These are the sums of the actual incoming ordinary blocks and terminal ordinary block, with every unselected step retained. Define\n\n    P_m = 1/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]\n          * product_(i=0)^(k-1)\n              [b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i))]^2.\n\nIf J>=m, set k=0 and P_m=1/[2*(1+S_m)]. Define the constant\n\n    L_joint=Z_1/(2*lambda)\n       *exp[-1+(8+4sqrt(2)-14*pi/5-1/50)\n                    *(1-5*pi/(28*log lambda))].\n\nFor any nonnegative integer n and any finite nonnegative word h of length n, define W_n(h) as the supremum of 2*(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite dimensions, all convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimizer x_star, and all x_0!=x_star, under actual gradient descent x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t). Here L>0 is fixed and arbitrary; this is the W=2R normalization.\n\nThere exists a finite integer M>=6 such that J<m for every integer m>=M and the following holds for EVERY such m and EVERY L>0. The explicit construction in the proof gives a single static function F_m:R^(m-J+1)->R, independent of L, such that f_m=L*F_m is convex and differentiable, has L-Lipschitz gradient, and satisfies f_m(0)=min f_m=0. Starting at the standard unit vector e_0, actual gradient descent uses EVERY step of H_m in its original physical order, and its exact payoff about the specified actual minimizer 0 is\n\n    2*(f_m(x_(n_m))-f_m(0))/(L*||e_0-0||^2)\n        =P_m>(1+1/1000)*U_(n_m)^ZJ.\n\nThe specified radius is exactly ||e_0||=1. For this same function, let C_min=argmin f_m and rho_m=dist(e_0,C_min). The minimum set is the explicit nonempty closed convex polyhedron derived in the proof, and 0<rho_m<=1. The payoff about the closest minimizer is exactly P_m/rho_m^2>=P_m. No exact limit for that different normalization is asserted.\n\nAll actual query points, the terminal point, and any minimizer satisfy EVERY ordered smooth-convex interpolation inequality: if f_u=f_m(x_u) and g_u=grad f_m(x_u), then for every ordered pair u,v,\n\n    f_u-f_v+<g_u,x_v-x_u>+||g_u-g_v||^2/(2L)<=0.\n\nTheir Gram matrices are positive semidefinite because they are actual Euclidean vectors. The exact limit about the specified minimizer and the resulting worst-case lower bound are\n\n    lim_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*P_m\n        =L_joint>5107867121/5100000000>1001/1000,\n    liminf_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*W_(n_m)(H_m)\n        >=L_joint>1001/1000.\n\nOnly the unconditional original benchmark/ray conclusions and certified saved-endpoint scope of fact 04219554e41d9750 are used as accepted input. This is a lower witness for ONE prescribed family. It asserts no minimax counterexample, no optimality over schedules or checkpoint selections, no exact closest-radius limit, no numerical value of M, and no resolution of the original all-n/all-nonnegative-word problem.\n", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "5b7ff4253c38b90a", "links": {"source_id": "6be9249882fd0868", "predecessors": ["04219554e41d9750"]}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "The proof was checked sequentially through Sections 1–14 and establishes the stated prescribed-family theorem. The enclosing-m word identities, physical checkpoint indices, ordinary-block sums, and finite thresholds are correct. The supporting-normal argument and backward recursion establish the required projections and the uniform displacement allowance without assuming kappa <= epsilon or q <= 1. The finite Moreau envelopes have the stated gradients and zero regions. Their static half-sum has a 1-Lipschitz gradient, realizes every original GD update, and yields exactly P_m, including the terminal factor and empty-selection case. The minimum polyhedron, specified radius 1, closest-radius comparison, ordered interpolation inequalities, and positive semidefinite Gram matrices follow as claimed. The activation expansion retains the logarithmic correction; the summed bounds control the entire growing product and prove P_m/P5_m -> 1. The exceptional first checkpoint and terminal block give the stated constant L_joint. Fact 04219554e41d9750 was read and supplies the unconditional benchmark-ray conclusions used here; its separate conditional seed result is not invoked. Its certified table hash and the saved B_1023 lower endpoint match the citation. The rational estimates establish L_joint > 5107867121/5100000000 > 1001/1000, and convergence gives a finite M uniform in L. The external theorem search returned unrelated results; subsequent inspection confirmed the background construction in [Ye–Liu, Appendix A.2–A.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2). The present proof independently establishes the properties it needs. No hard-prohibition violation, critical error, or proof gap was found. Acceptance concerns this prescribed family and does not resolve the unrestricted minimax problem.", "critical_errors": [], "gaps": []}}
{"id": "41ba515ef9c7e65a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T12:20:38.353004+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Exact optima and uniform deficits for two static spiral witness families with independently chosen stage parameters.\n\nAll spaces, norms and inner products are finite-dimensional real Euclidean ones. The notation defined below is local to this statement and its proof. In particular, local arc constants C_i are distinct from the benchmark sequence C_n; local coordinate states are distinct from hierarchy masses. The two constructions below are separate families.\n\nFix an integer m>=1536 and a real parameter 0<tau<=1/4. Set lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-tau/m, c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta/c, H_0=(), and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j) for 0<=j<m. Every child uses this same enclosing pair (m,tau). Put h=H_(m,tau)=H_m, S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1, and H_base(h)=1/(1+2S_m). Every original physical entry of h is retained in its original order.\n\nFor advertised smoothness L>0 and an objective g with an attained minimum, use the actual updates x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad g(x_t), 0<=t<n_m, and define\n\n    E_L(g,h)=2(g(x_(n_m))-min g)/(L*dist(x_0,argmin g)^2).\n\nAll instances below start at x_0=e_0, the first standard coordinate vector, and have strictly positive closest distance. Write E=E_1. The functions F and f below are constructed at smoothness one; their general-L instances are L*F and L*f.\n\nChoose any ordered subset of original positions of h, of any size 1<=k<=n_m, and write\n\n    h=(block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k), s_i=sum block_i.\n\nEach block is the complete original chronological list between selections. Empty blocks are allowed. For each 0<=i<k choose independently kappa_i>0 and 0<epsilon_i<=1/2. These choices may depend on the entire word, m, tau and every selected position, and are fixed before defining one static objective. Put\n\n    theta_sp,i=arctan(1/epsilon_i),\n    C_i=sqrt(1+epsilon_i^2)*exp(kappa_i*theta_sp,i),\n    T_i=(exp((1-kappa_i^2)*theta_sp,i/kappa_i)-1)/(1-kappa_i^2) if kappa_i<1,\n    T_i=theta_sp,i/kappa_i if kappa_i>=1,\n    H_sp,i=C_i*[1+(1+kappa_i)*T_i]-1,\n    A_i=epsilon_i+(1+H_sp,i)/epsilon_i.\n\nFor source eligibility require b_i>=2(1+epsilon_i^(-2)). For main eligibility define\n\n    qmax_i=1 if kappa_i<=epsilon_i,\n    qmax_i=sqrt((1+epsilon_i^2)/(1+kappa_i^2))\n           *exp(kappa_i*[theta_sp,i-arctan(1/kappa_i)]) otherwise,\n    N_i=max(0,kappa_i*qmax_i-epsilon_i),\n    B_i=1+max(epsilon_i^(-2),N_i/epsilon_i),\n\nand require b_i>=B_i. All eligibility equalities, epsilon_i=1/2, kappa_i=1, kappa_i>1, and all mixtures of different neighboring parameters are included.\n\nThe exact local construction used in each family is as follows. For one stage suppress i, and define, on 0<=theta<=theta_sp,\n\n    R(theta)=C*exp(-kappa*theta),\n    e(theta)=(cos(theta),-sin(theta)),\n    t(theta)=(-sin(theta),-cos(theta)),\n    v(theta)=R(theta)*e(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)),\n    K=conv({0} union {v(theta):0<=theta<=theta_sp}).\n\nFor a chronological local list alpha_1,...,alpha_r>=0, set theta_r=theta_sp and r_r=v_r=(epsilon,-1). Define the states backward. If r_j=(u_j,-y_j) and alpha=alpha_j, use v_(j-1)=(C,0), theta_(j-1)=0, r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha*(C,0) when y_j<=kappa*[u_j+(alpha-1)*C]. Otherwise theta_(j-1) is the unique root theta in (0,theta_j] of\n\n    <r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)*t(theta)>/R(theta)=1-alpha,\n\nand set v_(j-1)=v(theta_(j-1)) and r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha*v_(j-1). These are exact finite mathematical definitions, including zero local steps. Define the ordered outputs\n\n    P=sum_(j=1)^r alpha_j*p(theta_(j-1)),\n    Q=sum_(j=1)^r alpha_j*q(theta_(j-1)).\n\nThe empty list has P=Q=0. Given its local checkpoint b_loc and positive scale sigma as specified separately below, set\n\n    z_circ=(1-sigma*(epsilon+P),sigma*(1+Q))=(c_1,c_2),\n    U=1-sigma*(P+epsilon*b_loc), Y=sigma*Q,\n    V={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_r},\n    phi(z)=sigma*max_(v in V)<v,z-z_circ>,\n    f_loc(x)=env_1(phi)(x)=min_z[phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2].\n\nHere env_1 denotes this quadratic envelope. The local query states are x_j^loc=z_circ+sigma*r_j. Define Hub_d(z), for d>0, as 0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=d, and d*z-d^2/2 for z>=d.\n\nSOURCE family. For each eligible stage use its own parameters, local list block_i/2, local checkpoint b_i/2, and\n\n    sigma'_i=1/[C_i*s_i/2+epsilon_i*b_i/2+A_i].\n\nPrimes on P'_i,Q'_i,r'_(i,j),v'_(i,j),phi'_i,f'_i,c'_(i,1),c'_(i,2),U'_i,Y'_i denote exactly these half-step local objects; f'_i is the local envelope. Put\n\n    G'_i=sigma'_i*b_i/2,\n    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,\n    D'_(i+1)=D'_i*G'_i, ell'_(i+1)=D'_i*sigma'_i*Q'_i,\n    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k),\n    F(x)=(1/2)*sum_(i<k)(D'_i)^2\n         *f'_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)\n         +(1/2)*Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k), x in R^(k+1).\n\nMAIN family. For each eligible stage use its own parameters, the full physical local list block_i, and local checkpoint b_i. Let P_i,Q_i,r_(i,j),v_(i,j) be these full-step objects. Put\n\n    M_i^tr=P_i+max(epsilon_i*b_i,epsilon_i+(1+Q_i)/epsilon_i),\n    sigma_i=1/M_i^tr,\n    D_0=1, ell_0=0,\n    D_(i+1)=D_i*sigma_i*(b_i-1),\n    ell_(i+1)=D_i*sigma_i*(1+Q_i).\n\nUse these scales in the local definition of phi_i,c_(i,1),c_(i,2),U_i,Y_i. Define\n\n    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2*phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,\n    delta=D_k/(1+2s_k), Phi_k(z)=delta*max(z_k-ell_k,0),\n    J(z)=max_(0<=i<=k)Phi_i(z), f=env_1(J), z in R^(k+1).\n\nEach F and f is a single everywhere finite convex differentiable function with 1-Lipschitz gradient and attained minimum zero. All parameters, planes, scales and translations in these definitions are fixed before the run. In SOURCE, at the start of stage i, coordinates j<i are frozen at ell'_j+D'_j*U'_j, the current coordinate is ell'_i+D'_i and later coordinates are zero. At the ordinary and checkpoint queries the active local state is x_j^loc and the exact gradient and value are\n\n    grad F(x)=D'_i*sigma'_i*v'_(i,j)/2 on coordinates (i,i+1),\n    F(x)=(D'_i*sigma'_i)^2\n         *[<v'_(i,j),r'_(i,j)>-||v'_(i,j)||^2/2]/2.\n\nAll other gradient coordinates vanish. Thus a physical step h_t implements precisely the local half step h_t/2. The checkpoint leaves coordinate i at ell'_i+D'_i*U'_i and the next coordinate at ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). In its tail, at already executed physical mass u in [0,s_k],\n\n    x_k-ell'_k=D'_k-delta'*u/2,\n    grad F(x)=delta'*e_k/2,\n    F(x)=[delta'*(D'_k-delta'*u/2)-(delta')^2/2]/2,\n    2F(x_(n_m))=(D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)].\n\nIn MAIN, at the start of stage i, coordinates j<i are frozen at ell_j+D_j*U_j, the current coordinate is ell_i+D_i and later coordinates are zero. At all ordinary and checkpoint queries the exact data are\n\n    grad f(x)=D_i*sigma_i*v_(i,j) on coordinates (i,i+1),\n    f(x)=(D_i*sigma_i)^2\n         *[<v_(i,j),r_(i,j)>-||v_(i,j)||^2/2].\n\nAll other gradient coordinates vanish. Every full physical step implements exactly the full local step; the checkpoint freezes coordinate i at ell_i+D_i*U_i and puts the next coordinate at ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1). In its tail, at already executed physical mass u in [0,s_k],\n\n    x_k=ell_k+D_k-delta*u,\n    grad f(x)=delta*e_k,\n    f(x)=delta*(D_k-delta*u)-delta^2/2,\n    2f(x_(n_m))=D_k^2/(1+2s_k).\n\nThe complete SOURCE minimum set is\n\n    C_source={x: x_i<=zeta'_i,\n       epsilon_i*x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon_i*zeta'_i-D'_i*c'_(i,2), i<k;\n       x_k<=ell'_k}, zeta'_i=ell'_i+D'_i*c'_(i,1).\n\nIt contains zero. For an exact formula retaining every row, let A_rad consist of rows e_i^T and (epsilon_i*e_i-e_(i+1))^T for i<k, followed by e_k^T, and let t_rad be their right sides in this polyhedron. For every linearly independent row subset I put A_I=(A_rad)_I, r_I=A_I*e_0-(t_rad)_I, K_I=A_I*A_I^T, lambda_I=K_I^(-1)*r_I. The empty subset contributes zero. The true squared closest distance is\n\n    rho_S^2=dist(e_0,C_source)^2\n       =max_(I independent,lambda_I>=0) r_I^T*K_I^(-1)*r_I,\n    0<rho_S^2<=1.\n\nThus its actual payoff is (D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)*rho_S^2]. In particular the first-center expression is only a lower distance bound:\n\n    rho_S^2>=(sigma'_0)^2*[(epsilon_0+P'_0)^2+(1+Q'_0)^2].\n\nThe complete MAIN minimum set is\n\n    C_main={x: x_i<=zeta_i,\n       epsilon_i*x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon_i*zeta_i-D_i*c_(i,2), i<k;\n       x_k<=ell_k}, zeta_i=ell_i+D_i*c_(i,1).\n\nIt contains zero. Its true closest point to e_0 is z_bar=(c_(0,1),c_(0,2),0,...,0), and\n\n    rho_M^2=dist(e_0,C_main)^2\n       =sigma_0^2*[(epsilon_0+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2]>0,\n    E(f,h)=(b_0-1)^2/\n       {[(epsilon_0+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2]*(1+2s_k)}\n       *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i^tr]^2.\n\nAll original physical query rows, the unqueried terminal row and every minimizer row satisfy every ordered smooth-convex interpolation inequality. Explicitly, for g=L*F or L*f and any two such samples x_u,x_v,\n\n    g(x_u)-g(x_v)+<grad g(x_u),x_v-x_u>\n       +||grad g(x_u)-grad g(x_v)||^2/(2L)<=0.\n\nTheir full Gram matrices are positive semidefinite. No step, gradient coordinate, query, terminal point or minimizer inequality is omitted.\n\nInclude also the empty-selection members: SOURCE has F=Hub_(1/(1+S_m))/2 from 1, with closest distance 1 and E(F,h)=1/[2(1+S_m)]; MAIN has f=Hub_(1/(1+2S_m)) from 1, with closest distance 1 and E(f,h)=H_base(h). For either family and every L>0, scaling the constructed objective by L and using the physical steps h_t/L preserves every iterate and gives E_L(L*F,h)=E(F,h), respectively E_L(L*f,h)=E(f,h).\n\nFor the original benchmark define Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2 and G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2 for x,y>=0. Set C_0=B_0=1 and, for every integer n>=1,\n\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,\n\nwith every original integer split retained. Let Z_1=lim_(m->infinity)lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1), whose existence, positivity and finiteness are unconditional conclusions of predecessor 04219554e41d9750. Only that predecessor's unconditional benchmark/ray and certified saved-enclosure conclusions are used; no seed hypothesis is assumed.\n\nThe following bounds hold uniformly for every m>=1536 and 0<tau<=1/4, all eligible nonempty selections of arbitrary size, and all independent local parameter choices above:\n\n    E_L(L*f,H_(m,tau))<(441/500)/(1+2S_m)       [MAIN],\n    E_L(L*F,H_(m,tau))<(9801/10000)/(1+2S_m)  [SOURCE].\n\nDefine L_source,stage and L_main,stage as the suprema of these actual closest-distance normalized payoffs over their respective selections and independent parameter arrays, including their respective empty-selection members. Define L_both,stage as the supremum over the union of the two separate families and L_source,stage^H=max{L_source,stage,H_base}. Then the exact family optima are\n\n    L_source,stage(H_(m,tau))=1/[2(1+S_m)],\n    L_main,stage(H_(m,tau))=L_both,stage(H_(m,tau))\n       =L_source,stage^H(H_(m,tau))=1/(1+2S_m).\n\nFor every m>=1536 they satisfy the strict original-benchmark deficits\n\n    B_(n_m)*L_both,stage(H_(m,1/4))<237/250,\n    B_(n_m)*L_both,stage(H_(m,tau))<79/80 for every 1/5<=tau<=1/4.\n\nThe same two bounds hold for each of the other three named optima. For each fixed 0<tau<=1/4, the B_(n_m)-scaled limit of each of the four named optima, as m tends to infinity, is exactly Z_1/(2*exp(tau)).\n\nThese are optima only over the two explicitly specified static witness families on this hierarchy. The statement gives no upper bound for unrestricted W_n, no tau=1 theorem, no optimality over other functions or schedules, and no resolution of the original exact minimax problem.\n", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "5fd396eba75651e7", "links": {"source_id": "0aa8eeac5354d633", "predecessors": ["04219554e41d9750"]}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "Verified the proof in textual order. The backward spiral construction, local envelopes, static SOURCE and MAIN assemblies, complete minimum sets, and closest-distance formulas are justified, including zero steps, eligibility equalities, kappa >= 1, and independently chosen neighboring parameters. The discrete turning estimate and deletion inequalities establish both uniform bounds for nonempty selections; comparison with the empty members establishes the four exact family optima. The statement and relevant proof sections of fact 04219554e41d9750 confirm the unconditional benchmark ray enclosure. Its certified table's SHA256 and row 1024 match the quoted artifact and endpoint. No conditional seed hypothesis is used. The subsequent scalar estimates establish the claimed finite benchmark deficits and scaled limits. The constructed static functions also justify all ordered interpolation inequalities, Gram positivity, and smoothness scaling. No external-paper theorem is imported by this candidate, and no hard-prohibition violation, critical error, or proof gap was found.", "critical_errors": [], "gaps": []}}
{"id": "9ad907f670e5621a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T10:11:42.777670+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "verification", "claim": "# Exact six/eight-anchor multistage completions and two special word families\n\nAll norms and inner products are Euclidean. For an integer n>=0, a finite\nnonnegative physical schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), and L>0, let W_n(h)\nbe the supremum of\n\n    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)\n\nover every finite-dimensional convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz\ngradient, every minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, using the actual updates\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Write R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2. The witnesses\nbelow use smoothness1 and the true closest initial distance to the complete\nminimum set. Multiplication of their objective by any L>0 gives the same\npoints with steps h_i/L and the same normalized payoff.\n\n## Definitions of the two completed functions\n\nFix real parameters M=M_mc>=2, B=B_mc>=2, A=A_mc>=4B+4, and\nepsilon in [0,1]. These are local construction parameters, distinct from\nthe benchmark sequences C_n and B_n below. Put a=a_mc=1-1/M,\nC=C_mc=2B+1, and let e_1,e_2,e_3 be the standard basis of R^3. Define\n\n    nu=sqrt(2A-4),\n    p=p_mc=e_1/M,\n    q=q_mc=(-2e_1+nu e_2)/M,\n    Q=Qnorm_mc=||q||^2=2A/M^2,\n    r=r_mc=-q/2+sqrt((B/2-1/4)Q)e_3,\n    R=Rnorm_mc=||r||^2=BQ/2,       <q,r>=-Q/2,\n    D_mc=conv{0,p,q,r},\n    F_old(x)=max_(g in D_mc){<x,g>-||g||^2/2},\n    y=y_mc=-Ae_1-Mq,\n    z=z_mc=y-BMq-Mr,\n    w=w_mc=z-CMr-Mq,\n    t_P=a e_1,              delta_P=a/M,\n    t_Q=y+(M-1)q,           delta_Q=(M-1)Q,\n    t_R=z+(M-1)r,           delta_R=(M-1)R,\n    t_D=w+(M-1)q,           delta_D=(M-1)Q.\n\nHere conv denotes convex hull. For each ordinary list L_i, choose any\nfinite nonnegative list of total mass M-1 and retain every entry, including\nzeros. With concatenation in the displayed physical order, define\n\n    h_three=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,1+M epsilon),\n    h_return=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,CM+1,L_3,1+M epsilon).\n\nTheir physical lengths are sum_(i=0)^2 length(L_i)+3 and\nsum_(i=0)^3 length(L_i)+4. Assertions about h_return additionally assume\nM epsilon>=2B^2. Define, for v,x in R^3,\n\n    H_three(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,<t_P,v>-delta_P,\n               <y,v>,<t_Q,v>-delta_Q,<z,v>,<t_R,v>-delta_R},\n    H_return(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,<t_P,v>-delta_P,\n               <y,v>,<t_Q,v>-delta_Q,<z,v>,<t_R,v>-delta_R,\n               <w,v>,<t_D,v>-delta_D},\n    F_three(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H_three(v)},\n    F_return(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H_return(v)}.\n\nThese are one fixed globally defined function per full physical run,\nspecified before that run. To define its complete old prefix, run F_old\nfrom x_0=e_1 on the appropriate schedule and retain every triple\n(x_i,grad F_old(x_i),F_old(x_i)) with 0<=i<n, together with the\nzero optimum triple. The terminal index n is unqueried by the updates.\nFor any of the resulting actual functions, its signed ordered residual is\nQ_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 on\n{star,0,...,n}, where f_i=F(x_i), g_i=grad F(x_i), and\nx_star=g_star=f_star=0.\n\n## The specifically defined collapsed comparison\n\nFor the SAME h_three or h_return, retain the first list L_0 and bridge\nAM+1, and treat all subsequent entries before the last 1+M epsilon as\none ordinary list, retaining their physical indices. Its mass is M_2-1,\nwhere\n\n    M_2=M_col_three=(B+2)M,\n    or M_2=M_col_return=(B+C+3)M=(3B+4)M.\n\nSet q_col=(-2e_1+nu e_2)/M_2 and define the static old collapsed function\n\n    F_col_old(x)=max_(g in conv{0,p,q_col}){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nRun F_col_old from e_1 on that same full physical word and retain all\nits old queried triples at indices 0<=i<n.\nThe collapsed completion class consists exactly of all everywhere defined\nconvex differentiable 1-smooth functions on R^3 interpolating its ENTIRE\nold queried prefix, including values and gradients, and the optimum triple\n(0,0,0). It is nonempty. For any such completion F_col, the same actual word\nfrom e_1 has normalized true-distance payoff V_col(F_col). Define the positive\nor zero upper comparison number\n\n    U_col_three=2A epsilon^2/(B+2)^2,\n    U_col_return=2A epsilon^2/(3B+4)^2,\n    rho_col_three=[B(B+2)^2/2](1-1/(2B^3)),\n    rho_col_return=(3/4)(3B+4)^2.\n\n## Exact named reference channels and original benchmark\n\nFor any h of length n, put s(h)=sum_(i<n)h_i, P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i,\nT_j=sum_(i>j)h_i, and (u)_+=max(u,0). The accepted single-cut channel is\n\n    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2s(h)),\n          max_(0<=j<n)(h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]},\n\nwith the inner maximum omitted at n=0. Define the entire convex-quadratic\nchannel by\n\n    Q_n(h)=max_(0<=t<=1) t product_(i<n)(1-h_i t)^2.\n\nThis is the supremum of the normalized payoff over all finite-dimensional\nconvex quadratics with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L, with arbitrary\ninitial points and chosen minimizers, in the original normalization.\n\nFor n>=1 and a complex variable zeta=alpha+i beta, put\nPoly_0(zeta)=1, Poly_i(zeta)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t zeta),\nxi_i=Poly_i(zeta), gamma_i=zeta Poly_i(zeta), and xi_star=gamma_star=0.\nIdentify the complex plane with the real plane using <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). On the index set\nI_n={star,0,...,n}, the weight of the directed edge j->i is\n\n    q_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2.\n\nLet ell_n be the maximum sum of these weights on directed simple paths\nn->star. Define Gap_h(zeta)=-2ell_n for zeta in the exact universal lens\n\n    Lens_n={alpha+i beta:\n       alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))},\n    L_lens_n(h)=sup_(zeta in Lens_n) Gap_h(zeta),\n    V_lens(h)=max{L_lens_n(h),1/(1+2s(h))}.\n\nThe meaning and legality of this transcript are those of accepted fact_id\ndfde2fa326c908bf; the reference is restricted to Lens_n. No upper\ncomparison with the full schedule-dependent complex legal region is made.\n\nUse the original all-split sequences, exactly as in accepted fact_id\nb98bb807d514e80d. The joins have real arguments x,y>=0, and the\nrecurrences apply for every integer n>=1:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,      lambda=1+sqrt(2),      Kc=cosh(2).\n\nEvery maximum here ranges over ALL integer splits, with C_n=S_circle(n)+1\nand B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 in that predecessor's notation.\n\nFor each integer N>=2^60, set\n\n    M=2N(N+1)+1, A=M^(1/12), B=M^(1/120), epsilon=M^(-1/3),\n    T_Cheb=(2N+1)^2=2M-1.\n\nLet Chi_N be the list of entries\nsec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]), 1<=j<=N. Let L_up be three consecutive\nincreasing copies of Chi_N and L_down three consecutive decreasing copies.\nUse L_0=L_up and L_i=L_down for every subsequent i. Thus the two base\nword lengths are 9N+3 and 12N+4. For every integer n>=9*2^60+3 define\nthe padded three-stage word by N=floor((n-3)/9), followed by inserting\nn-(9N+3) leading zero steps into that base word. For every integer\nn>=12*2^60+4 define the padded return word by N=floor((n-4)/12) and\ninsert n-(12N+4) leading zero steps. These are the two SPECIAL families\nin the conclusions below, with their respective N and parameters.\n\n## Conclusions\n\n(i) F_three and, on its additional parameter domain, F_return are finite\neverywhere, convex, continuously differentiable, and have least global\ngradient Lipschitz constant EXACTLY1. Each is the pointwise maximal legal\ncompletion of its complete specified old prefix. The exact maximum of\nall physical prefix conjugate quadratics is H_three or H_return, with\nsix or eight distinct affine anchors, respectively; the first r-query\nanchor t_R is retained in BOTH laws. Every old prefix point, value and\ngradient is preserved. The actual queried gradients are p,q,r for the\nthree-stage law and p,q,r,q for the return law, one constant gradient on\neach ordinary list and its following bridge query. Every signed ordered\ninterpolation residual, including every remote, optimum and terminal pair,\nin both orientations, is nonpositive for the resulting actual function.\nThe complete minimum sets and minimum values are\n\n    argmin F_three=conv{0,y,z},   min F_three=0,\n    argmin F_return=conv{0,y,z,w},   min F_return=0.\n\nOrigin is the unique closest minimizer to e_1, at distance1 in both cases.\n\n(ii) For the three-stage law set\n\n    L_stage=B+a, E_3=M(B+a)q+Mr,\n    D_3=||E_3||^2=2A[(B+a)^2-(B+a)+B/2],\n    b_3=Aa(epsilon-2/M), theta_3=(b_3)_+/D_3.\n\nThe actual terminal point, gradient and normalized payoff are exactly\n\n    x_n=z-M epsilon r,\n    grad F_three(x_n)=-M epsilon r-theta_3 E_3,\n    V_three=2F_three(x_n)\n       =AB epsilon^2-[Aa(epsilon-2/M)_+]^2/D_3,\n    AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))<=V_three<=AB epsilon^2.\n\nThis includes epsilon=0, epsilon=2/M when in range, M=2, and epsilon=1.\nFor the return law set\n\n    d_R=C+a, E_R=Mq+M d_R r,\n    Dhat_R=1-d_R+(B/2)d_R^2, D_R=||E_R||^2=2A Dhat_R,\n    b_R=2A[(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M)], theta_R=b_R/D_R.\n\nThe actual terminal quantities are exactly\n\n    x_n=w-M epsilon q,\n    grad F_return(x_n)=-M epsilon q-theta_R E_R,\n    V_return=2F_return(x_n)=2A epsilon^2-b_R^2/D_R,\n    2A epsilon^2(1-1/(2B))<=V_return<=2A epsilon^2.\n\nThey include the feasible boundary M epsilon=2B^2. The denominator\nbounds are 2B^3<=Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3; the lower-point identity is exactly\n1-(2B+1)+(B/2)(2B+1)^2=2B^3+2B^2-3B/2.\nIn particular W_n(h_three)>=V_three and W_n(h_return)>=V_return.\n\n(iii) Every member of the narrowly defined collapsed completion class\nhas true closest initial radius1 and 0<=V_col(F_col)<=U_col for its word.\nOn the entire respective parameter domains the WEAK comparisons are\n\n    V_three>=rho_col_three U_col_three>=rho_col_three V_col(F_col),\n    V_return>=rho_col_return U_col_return>=rho_col_return V_col(F_col).\n\nAt epsilon=0 in the three-stage domain, V_three=U_col_three=V_col(F_col)=0:\nthe displayed weak comparison has equality and no payoff ratio is asserted.\nWhen epsilon>0 the comparison upper U_col is positive and the ratios\nV_three/U_col_three>=rho_col_three>1 and\nV_return/U_col_return>=rho_col_return>1 are defined and give strict\ndomination of every such collapsed completion. Epsilon>0 is automatic\nin the return parameter domain. Both factors diverge on their specified\ngrowing families. The comparison covers this exact retained prefix and\nsecond slope, with no claim about other partitions, slopes, or witnesses.\n\n(iv) For each of the two base growing words and each of its padded words,\nthe following bounds hold with each law evaluated on its corresponding\nword and ACTUAL physical horizon n; V denotes that law's attained payoff:\n\n    V_three>=(7/8)M^(-23/40),\n    V_return>=(3/2)M^(-7/12),\n    V>=(3/2)M^(-7/12)  for either law,\n    K_n(h)<=41M^(-5/6),\n    Q_n(h_three)<=64M^(-49/60),\n    Q_n(h_return)<=1600M^(-4/5),\n    L_lens_n(h_three)<=128Kc^18 M^(-49/60),\n    L_lens_n(h_return)<=3200Kc^24 M^(-4/5).\n\nWriting d_N=floor(log_2 N), the actual original benchmark obeys\n\n    B_n V >= [3lambda^3/(2*20^(7/12))]\n                       (lambda/2^(7/6))^(d_N)>1.\n\nConsequently each SPECIAL family supplies an attained static witness at\nEVERY integer horizon above its stated threshold with V>U_n^ZJ, and\nV/U_n^ZJ tends to infinity. Meanwhile\n\n    max{K_n(h),Q_n(h),L_lens_n(h)}=o(U_n^ZJ)\n\nalong each family, also with V_lens in place of L_lens_n. These results\nneither upper-bound unrestricted W_n on these words nor quantify over\narbitrary words. They give no comparison with all nonlinear scalar\nfunctions, all earlier adversaries, or the full complex legal region.\nThe original arbitrary-word asymptotic exact-profile theorem remains OPEN.\n", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "c3bfc54321ddec65", "links": {"source_id": "3c6eeac040d2feae", "predecessors": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d", "dfde2fa326c908bf"]}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "The proof passes sequential verification of all ten sections. {"id": "bd14f1cbf6ddd767", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T16:22:25.408065+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "verification", "claim": "A tighter exact all-split benchmark envelope and a conditional GD certificate threshold.\n\nFix L>0. For every integer n>=0 and predetermined nonnegative finite word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), run gradient descent\nx_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t), 0<=t<n.\nLet W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite Euclidean dimension, every convex differentiable function f with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star. Let R_n(h) be the same supremum without the numerator factor 2, so W_n(h)=2R_n(h). Put W_n_star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h), including the empty word at n=0.\n\nDefine lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda). For nonnegative real x,y put\nOmega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,\nG(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\nDefine C_0=B_0=1 and, for EVERY integer n>=1,\nC_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\nB_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)).\nThese are the original primitive and dominant benchmark capacities, with EVERY integer split retained. Put U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nFor every integer n>=0,\nC_n<=(n+1)^rho and B_n<=(12/5)(n+1)^rho.\n\nFor each integer m>=1536, set epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=lambda(1+epsilon), and a=beta/c. Define the finite words\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\nThe same enclosing m, epsilon, beta, c, and a are used at every child. Here the hierarchy symbols H_j,a,c are local to this statement. Put S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1, and D_m=1+2S_m. Then H_m is a predetermined strictly positive word of length n_m and\nB_(n_m)/D_m<47/50\nfor every integer m>=1536.\n\nFinally, the following is a CONDITIONAL implication, with its entire hypothesis left unproved: IF\nW_(n_m)(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) for EVERY integer m>=1536,\nas a bound on the full function-class supremum W defined here, THEN for every such m,\nB_(n_m) W_(n_m)(H_m)<799/800,\nW_(n_m)_star/U_(n_m)^ZJ<799/800,\nand\nliminf_(n->infinity) W_n_star/U_n^ZJ<=799/800<1.\n\nNo unconditional full-function upper bound on W_(n_m)(H_m) is asserted. The unconditional conclusions are the two all-n benchmark envelopes and the hierarchy benchmark ratio. The original unrestricted minimax problem remains unresolved by this result alone.", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "931b9653ce0f7201", "links": {"source_id": "20c7af333e2c7cbf", "predecessors": ["b98bb807d514e80d"]}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "The proof is correct for the stated unconditional bounds and conditional implication. {"id": "bc932e39db02fefb", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T22:43:08.437804+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Affine-height polytope Moreau envelopes represent the full finite-word gradient-descent supremum.\n\nAll spaces, norms, and inner products below are finite-dimensional and Euclidean. For every integer n>=0, every finite nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), and every L>0, define W_n(h) as the supremum of\n    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)\nover every convex differentiable f:R^d->R with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L and an attained minimum, every selected minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star, where\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i), 0<=i<n.\nThe word is fixed before the objective is selected. Empty words, zero steps, and arbitrarily large finite steps are allowed. Write R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2.\n\nFor the same n,h, define W_aff_n(h) by taking the supremum of\n    2 Phi_ah(X_ah(n))/||X_ah(0)||^2\nover the following choices in arbitrary positive finite dimension D. Let K_ah be a compact polytope with at most n+2 vertices, containing 0, and let lambda_ah in R^D satisfy\n    <lambda_ah,Y> > 0 for every Y in K_ah with Y!=0.\nThe degenerate polytope K_ah={0} is allowed. Define the single global objective\n    Phi_ah(X)=min_(Y in K_ah){<lambda_ah,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2}.\nChoose X_ah(0)!=0 with <lambda_ah,X_ah(0)> = 0, and run the actual updates\n    X_ah(i+1)=X_ah(i)-h_i grad Phi_ah(X_ah(i)), 0<=i<n.\n\nThen every such Phi_ah is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable with gradient Lipschitz constant at most 1, and has minimum value 0 and complete minimum set {0}. In particular the denominator in its payoff is its true squared initial distance to the entire minimum set. For every finite n and nonnegative h,\n    W_aff_n(h)=W_n(h).\n\nMore precisely, the following constructive assertion holds. Let f:R^d->R be mu-strongly convex and ell-smooth, with mu>0 and 0<ell<1, minimum f(0)=0, and x_0!=0. Strong convexity means\n    f(v)>=f(u)+<grad f(u),v-u>+(mu/2)||v-u||^2\nfor all u,v; ell-smooth means the gradient is ell-Lipschitz. Run the same word with advertised smoothness 1, so x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad f(x_i). For all i=0,...,n, including the terminal sample, put\n    g_i=grad f(x_i), v_ah(i)=f(x_i),\n    y_ah(i)=x_i-g_i, delta_ah(i)=v_ah(i)-||g_i||^2/2.\nUse an extra optimum label star with x_star=g_star=y_ah(star)=0 and v_ah(star)=delta_ah(star)=0. Define\n    gamma_ah=(1/ell-1)/2,\n    z_ah(0)=0,\n    z_ah(i+1)=(1-h_i)z_ah(i)+h_i delta_ah(i), 0<=i<n,\n    e_ah(i)=z_ah(i)-delta_ah(i), 0<=i<=n.\nFor a real number t let [t]_+=max(t,0). Define the finite scalar\n    M_ah=max { [e_ah(i)(delta_ah(j)-delta_ah(i))]_+\n                    /(gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2):\n               0<=i<=n, j in {star,0,...,n}, g_i!=g_j },\nwhere the maximum of an empty set is defined to be zero. For every finite B_ah>0 with B_ah^2>=max(1,2M_ah), put, in R^(d+1),\n    lambda_ah=(0,B_ah),\n    Y_ah(i)=(y_ah(i),delta_ah(i)/B_ah),\n    X_ah(i)=(x_i,z_ah(i)/B_ah),\n    G_ah(i)=(g_i,e_ah(i)/B_ah), 0<=i<=n,\n    Y_ah(star)=0,\n    K_ah=conv{0,Y_ah(0),...,Y_ah(n)}.\nHere conv denotes convex hull and the zero in lambda_ah is the horizontal zero vector in R^d. This K_ah, lambda_ah, and X_ah(0) belong to the stated restricted class. Writing P_ah for Euclidean projection onto K_ah, all the following hold simultaneously:\n    P_ah(X_ah(i)-lambda_ah)=Y_ah(i),\n    grad Phi_ah(X_ah(i))=G_ah(i),\n    Phi_ah(X_ah(i))=v_ah(i)+e_ah(i)^2/(2B_ah^2), 0<=i<=n,\n    X_ah(i+1)=X_ah(i)-h_i G_ah(i), 0<=i<n,\n    <lambda_ah,X_ah(0)> = 0,\n    dist(X_ah(0),argmin Phi_ah)^2=||x_0||^2,\n    2Phi_ah(X_ah(n))/||X_ah(0)||^2\n       =2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2+e_ah(n)^2/(B_ah^2||x_0||^2).\nThus the horizontal trajectory is exact in one extra coordinate and the terminal objective correction is nonnegative and is of order B_ah^(-2) for this fixed source and word.\n\nFor an arbitrary original source, the equality of suprema uses translation of its selected minimizer to zero, division of the objective by L, and then the regularizations\n    f_eps(x)=(1-2epsilon)f(x)+(epsilon/2)||x||^2, 0<epsilon<1/2.\nFor each fixed source and finite word these are epsilon-strongly convex and (1-epsilon)-smooth with unique minimum at zero; their actual terminal values converge to that of the translated, normalized original source. The lift is chosen separately for each epsilon. This covers arbitrary selected original minima, including nonunique original minimum sets.\n\nThe assertion includes repeated samples, equal source gradients, zero source gradients, and all normals involving the original terminal. It asserts neither a quantitative convergence rate or benchmark comparison, nor a uniform approximation across words or horizons, attainment of either supremum, simplex geometry, a uniform bound on B_ah, or an adversary selectable from the schedule alone without a source trajectory.", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "d8b59c7d92425121", "links": {"source_id": null, "predecessors": []}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "The proof is correct. Checked all five sections in textual order against the full statement. Section 1 establishes the projection characterization, differentiability and 1-Lipschitz continuity of the envelope gradient, convexity, finiteness, and the unique minimum at zero. Section 2 correctly derives the ordered residual bound and its algebraic identity; strong convexity resolves equal gradients and zero gradients, while ell < 1 supplies strictly positive slack for distinct gradients. Section 3 correctly uses the finite maximum M_ah and the stated bound on B_ah to satisfy every projection normal simultaneously, including terminal and optimum comparisons. The polytope has the required vertex bound and strict positive height away from zero, and the projection, gradient, and value identities follow. Section 4 verifies the actual updates of a single fixed objective, initial orthogonality, the exact distance denominator, and the nonnegative terminal correction with the stated B_ah dependence. Section 5 proves finite-trajectory and terminal-value convergence under regularization, obtains the reverse supremum inequality without requiring uniform lift bounds, and correctly handles arbitrary selected minimizers and scaling by L. Empty words, zero steps, repetitions, and arrivals at the source minimum are covered. Nonnegativity of the steps is a permissible restriction; the algebra needs only their finiteness. The statement is self-contained. There are no external citations or fact_id dependencies to check and no violations of the hard prohibitions. No critical errors or gaps were found.", "critical_errors": [], "gaps": []}}
{"id": "6e8522564c55a465", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-03T03:03:12.133988+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "verification", "claim": "Projection-simplex universality for finite words and exact full-class value of the actual seven-step H_3 block.\n\nAll spaces, norms, and inner products are finite-dimensional real Euclidean ones. For every integer n>=0, every finite predetermined nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), and every advertised L>0, define W_n(h) as the supremum of\n    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)\nover every convex differentiable function f with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L and an attained minimum, every selected minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star, using the actual updates\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i), 0<=i<n.\nThe schedule is fixed before the function and start are chosen. Empty words and zero steps are included. Write R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2, and p=#{i:0<=i<n and h_i>0}.\n\n(A) For each fixed word h, W_n(h) equals the supremum restricted, at normalized smoothness one, to the functions\n    F_D(x)=max_(g in D)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),\n    D=conv{0,w_1,...,w_k} in R^k, 1<=k<=p+1,\nwhere w_1,...,w_k are linearly independent. Thus D is a full-dimensional simplex with the origin as a vertex. The restriction retains all starts, all selected minimizers unequal to the start, and the same actual word. Here conv denotes convex hull, P_D is Euclidean projection onto D, grad F_D=P_D, min F_D=0, and the complete minimum set is the polar cone\n    C_D={z in R^k:<z,g><=0 for every g in D}.\nFor positive scores one may equivalently use the closest-minimum denominator dist(x_0,C_D)^2. For general L the restricted objectives are L F_D and the steps are h_i/L, preserving the same score. This is equality of suprema, with no assertion of finite-parameter exact representation or attainment for each source run, or uniform conditioning or approximation over words.\n\n(B) For every integer m>=1536 set\n    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<3.\nEvery child uses this same m and these same parameters. Let S3 be the sum of the seven entries of H_3. For every nonempty compact convex set D0 containing zero in any positive finite dimension, define, for d>0,\n    U_d(x)=max_(g in D0)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2).\nLet T_H3 be the actual composition of x -> x-h_i P_D0(x) over the seven original steps of H_3 in order. Then for every start x and every real d>=1,\n    U_d(T_H3 x)<=U_(d+2S3)(x).\nAll physical and auxiliary projections use the same set D0. No interior or full-dimensionality assumption on D0 is required.\n\n(C) For every m>=1536 and its actual seven-step H_3 from (B), the full original function class satisfies\n    W_7(H_3)=1/(1+2S3).\nThe lower equality is attained by a one-dimensional Huber function with the original selected-minimum radius. These assertions make no all-depth H_j U claim and do not resolve the original asymptotic minimax problem.\n", "evidence": "verdict: correct", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": "1e0bf3f41457d8e4", "links": {"source_id": null, "predecessors": ["1252b8823bb5a290"]}, "glossary": {}, "verdict": "correct", "write_error": null, "verification_report": {"summary": "已按证明的书写顺序核验，未发现错误或缺口。A 部分的严格插值余量、提升构造、单纯形扰动、实际迭代保持、维数压缩及半径比较均成立；正分数逼近论证充分建立了上确界相等，并覆盖空字、零步和重复梯度。B 部分的单步及组合恒等式、H2 非负分解、辅助投影余项和桥接恒等式一致；给定参数保证所需系数非负，从而对同一集合上的实际七步 H3 及所有 d>=1 得到所述 U 不等式，无需集合满维。C 部分正确地通过 A 转移上界，所给一维 Huber 函数以原始所选极小点半径达到下界。已读取唯一引用事实 1252b8823bb5a290，其插值必要性与本证明的方向及适用条件一致，且不存在 P3 依赖链缺陷。本证明没有外部论文引用，也未触发其他硬性禁止项。结论仅限声明的 A、B、C。", "critical_errors": [], "gaps": []}}
